## 1. Summary

Sessions lets people work with the agents in their workspaces from a phone or a desktop. Each
session runs Claude Code in one workspace folder. On one screen a person sends messages, steers the
agent mid-task, stops it, answers approvals and questions, switches model, effort, ultracode and
permission mode, follows subagents and workflows, and attaches or downloads images and files. One
list across all workspaces puts the sessions that need input first. The interaction patterns come
from Claude's mobile Remote Control; the look is the portal's own.

Each session is the unmodified `claude` CLI in print mode with stream-json in and out, started
detached in the workspace by a host script from the foundation bundle, and driven only by short,
portal-initiated runtime `exec` calls: start, send a line, read from a byte offset. Workspaces never
call the portal, and the portal is never in the API path. Sessions survive closed tabs and lost
readers; after a container restart, one tap or the next message resumes them. They run on one of
two credentials: the keys the workspace's Account Pool delivers, or, on a workspace without a pool,
the Claude Code sign-in the person made for the session's Claude in that workspace. The portal checks
that sign-in's status and never reads the login itself. A session whose stream shows input the
portal didn't send is ended.

## 2. Background and problem

- Today Claude Code in a workspace is reachable only through the browser terminal. On a phone a
  full-screen TUI is cramped, approvals need a keyboard, and nothing says an agent is waiting.
- Remote Control and cloud sessions need a claude.ai subscription, refuse API keys, `apiKeyHelper`
  and custom base URLs (what Account Pool delivers), and keep transcripts at Anthropic
  ([Remote Control](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/remote-control)).
- Seven features need one session engine: Settings (modes, budgets, caps), Monitoring (state,
  usage), Persona (prompts), Skillset and MCP set (reload, status), Account Pool (retries), Browser
  & desktop (handoffs), Portal MCP.
- A controller that stays attached loses the agent's work: when a raw `docker exec` client died,
  Claude Code finished the turn headless and that output reached no client ✅ (S7b). An Agent SDK
  `query()` in the portal would fail the same way (inferred) and can't reattach.

## 3. Goals

| ID  | Goal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **Simple everywhere.** One screen per session (transcript, composer, pickers); New session is the same screen, with no wizard. One layout from a 320 px phone to a wide desktop.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| G2  | **Steer, don't block.** The composer stays live while the agent works: Send steers, ✕ withdraws a queued message, Stop interrupts, and every message shows whether Claude has read it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| G3  | **Session controls.** Model, effort, ultracode and permission mode per session, within policy, showing the values that actually apply.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| G4  | **Multi-agent monitoring.** Each session's subagents, workflows and background tasks, live beside the conversation, and one list of every session across workspaces, sorted by attention.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| G5  | **Files both ways, in the conversation.** Everything you send, photos, screenshots, documents, audio and video, is attached in the composer; everything the agent produces comes back as a card in the transcript, with a preview and a Download. No file manager.                                                                                                                                                     |
| G6  | **Durable.** No lost or duplicated events across closed tabs, network drops and lost readers. Container restarts end in a resumable Interrupted state.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| G7  | **Native, push-only, compliant, tamper-evident.** Unmodified CLI, portal-initiated `exec` only, all three targets, portal-delivered credentials only; input the portal didn't send ends the session.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| G8  | **Fast.** First token on screen within 250 ms of Claude Code writing it (p95, Docker targets, not counting characters held back for redaction); with the word-by-word reveal a lone first word shows 30 to 66 ms after it arrives, and in a fast steady stream the reveal sits about 0.2 s behind by design (p95 0.28 s, never more than 0.5 s). Smooth scrolling through 5,000 blocks on the reference phone (§12.7). |

## 4. Non-goals

- **Remote Control, cloud sessions or self-hosted environments as the transport,** or launching
  `claude remote-control` for anyone. **A proxy:** the portal never sees API traffic or keys.
- **Workspaces without an account pool,** including API keys or cloud credentials a user set up
  (Q17). The terminal and Claude's own apps serve them.
- **Driving terminal sessions** (Monitoring shows them; handover is Q15) and **parsing Claude
  Code's transcripts**, whose format is internal.
- **An in-app split view** (v2; ⌘- or Ctrl-click opens a session in its own tab). **Fast mode**
  until Settings prices it. Push and HTTPS (Web app & PWA), per-user identity, shared editing, diff
  comments, rewinding files, agent teams, and any Anthropic look (§6).

## 5. Users and stories

**Workspace user** chats with agents, often from a phone. **Admin** sets policy. Often one person.

| ID   | Story                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| US1  | On my phone, Sessions shows "2 need input". I tap one, read the request and allow it, or type what Claude should do instead, on one screen.                                                                           |
| US2  | I type a task and send. The session starts in my last workspace and folder, with its persona defaults and policy; I can change both first.                                                                            |
| US3  | While the agent works, I send a correction and see it queued, then read. I can pull it back before it's read, or stop the turn, everything, or one subagent.                                                          |
| US4  | I switch model, effort, ultracode or permission mode, see the value that applies, and see why an option is missing.                                                                                                   |
| US5  | I watch subagents, workflows and background tasks (activity, tokens, time) without leaving the conversation, and open one to read its messages.                                                                       |
| US6  | I attach photos, screenshots, documents, audio or video from my phone or desktop, and get the files the agent produces as cards in the conversation, each with a preview and a Download, and a zip of a turn's files. |
| US7  | I close my laptop, open the session on my phone and carry on. A portal restart loses nothing.                                                                                                                         |
| US8  | In a workspace without an account pool, Sessions tells me why it can't run there and offers to assign one or open the terminal.                                                                                       |
| US9  | As an admin, I see each session's estimated cost against its budget, and why a new session is refused at a cap.                                                                                                       |
| US10 | As an admin who allows Bypass permissions in one workspace, I can turn it on only in a session I allowed it for at the start, after an explicit confirmation and re-auth, and the session shows it until it's off.    |
| US11 | Before the first session in a freshly cloned repository, I see which MCP servers and hooks it would run, and trust it or not.                                                                                         |

## 6. Scope boundary and compliance

| Allowed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Not allowed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Running the unmodified CLI in the organization's own workspaces on one of two credentials. On a workspace with an Account Pool, what the portal delivered (its `apiKeyHelper`: Anthropic API keys or custom hosts), billed to the key owner. On a workspace without one, the Claude Code sign-in the person made for the session's Claude in that workspace (a claude.ai or Console login, [Workspace Claudes](workspace-claudes.md) FR4), under their own plan. Cloud-provider credentials only once Account Pool delivers them (Q17). | Running a session on a sign-in the portal made up or injected (`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, a copied credential file), on another Claude's sign-in, or on a mix: a pool session that falls back to a sign-in, or the reverse.                                                                                                          |
| Borrowing Remote Control's interaction patterns. Calling the feature "Sessions" or "Chat". Saying "runs Claude Code" in plain text.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Imitating Claude Code's or Anthropic's look, logos or names. Using Remote Control, cloud sessions or self-hosted environments as the transport.                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Pointing a workspace with no usable credential to the Claudes list (sign in), Account Pool (assign), the terminal and Claude's own apps.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Reading or recording a workspace's own login (account, organization or email), Claude Code's credential files, `~/.claude/sessions/*.key` files or transcripts. The only thing read is `claude auth status`'s `loggedIn` and method; the plan's tier name and usage limits are kept only as [Claude usage](claude-usage.md) §6 says. |

This boundary is a requirement. Stream-json runs are SDK use (the billing header says
`cc_entrypoint=sdk-cli` ✅). The
[Agent SDK overview](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview) bars offering "claude.ai
login or rate limits for their products, including agents built on the Claude Agent SDK" without
approval; the [legal page](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance) bars routing
"requests through Free, Pro, or Max plan credentials on behalf of their users". Branding rules bar
"Claude Code" in product names and look-alike visuals. The Commercial Terms apply (Q13).

Owner's decision (v0.7): this portal is self-hosted, and a session on a sign-in runs on the login the
person made for themselves, in their own workspace, with Claude Code's own sign-in. The portal offers
no login of its own and routes nobody else's requests through it. Keep it that way: a session on a
sign-in is for the person who owns that login. Before people who don't own a workspace's sign-ins
chat on them, the sign-off in Q13 comes first.

| #   | Rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 1   | **Eligibility from portal state; a guard that reads no login.** A workspace without an Account Pool assignment hosts sessions on its Claudes' own sign-in: before every create, launch, resume and wake, `claude auth status` runs with the session's Claude directory, and only `loggedIn` counts; signed out, nothing starts ("This Claude isn't signed in. Sign it in on its workspace, or assign an account pool."). A workspace with an assignment hosts sessions only while it is active and Synced (not paused, not waiting for quota), on the pool's keys. Before every launch, resume and wake, `cp-session authcheck` confirms one thing, that the pool's helper is the credential in use 🔬 (Q3), and answers `pass` or `fail`; on `fail` nothing starts. Each turn's `system/init` must carry `apiKeySource: "apiKeyHelper"` (✅ for a `--settings` helper; 🔬 Q3 for the managed one), or the session ends. Both checks only confirm the helper: they never open `~/.claude.json` or `~/.claude/.credentials.json`, never report which other credential, account or plan is present, and the portal stores only pass or fail. That keeps the rule that the portal never reads a workspace's own login ([README](README.md) ground rules, [Settings & policies](settings-policies.md) §16). These pool checks apply to pool sessions only. Login commands are refused in every session (FR13): signing in happens in the Claudes list or the terminal. |
| 2   | **No credential switch mid-session.** A per-workspace **credential-transition lock** is taken by an Account Pool assignment to a workspace without one, an unassign or move to own login (its §10.4 step 1 sends the next request with the workspace's own credential), a Settings pause (empty key file) and `portal decommission`. Taking it ends the workspace's sessions; while it's held, starts, resumes, wakes and persona runs are refused ("Pool change in progress"). Account Pool releases it after its step 2 or the pause write. When the foundation's drift check finds the account drop-in or a key file changed outside the portal in a workspace with live sessions, chat ends them at once, with a High event, before the repair. Unassign needs nothing from Q4 for chat: sessions end before step 1 removes the helper, and after it the workspace's sessions run on their Claudes' own sign-in. An assignment likewise ends sessions on a sign-in before the pool's keys take over. If Q4 finds a fallback, pause keeps Settings §13.2's sentinel plan.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 3   | **Subscription alarm, failing closed, on a pool.** On a session on its Claude's own sign-in, plan limits are expected: no `get_usage` check, a `rate_limit_event` doesn't end it, and no spending cap (`--max-budget-usd`) is passed, since the plan's own limits apply. On a pool session: after each `result` the controller sends `get_usage {skip_behaviors: true}`; API-key sessions report `rate_limits_available: false` ✅ (custom host, mock). Non-null `rate_limits`, a `rate_limit_event`, an error, or a reply without `rate_limits_available` ends the session with a High event. The field is contract-tested per CLI version; its positive signal was never observed (Q3). A task of type `remote_agent` or a Workflow with status `remote_launched` (cloud paths 🔬) ends it too.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 4   | **Branding.** "Sessions" in navigation and titles; "Runs Claude Code in api-dev" as plain text; no Anthropic or Claude Code logos, colors, glyphs or terminal look; mode names as plain labels.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 5   | **Bypass permissions** is blocked by default; FR20 is its one full description. It's never one tap, never in a session that didn't opt in at its start, and never over MCP. Claude's apps never offer it on phones ([mobile](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mobile)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| 6   | **Auto on custom hosts** sends conversation excerpts to that host for classifier checks, outside the cost estimate (✅ 10 classifier requests in one run, S9). Auto stays hidden there until an admin confirms it per endpoint (Settings FR6).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 7   | **Built-in sign-in.** Refusing login commands, like Account Pool's hidden `/login`, restricts an authentication method built into Claude Code, which the legal page says hosted products "may not remove, disable, or restrict". That's defensible only for the operator's own organization, billed to the key owner; written sign-off comes before anyone outside it uses Sessions (Q13).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

## 7. Solution overview

```
 Browser                       Portal (one Node process)             Workspace (DinD, host Docker or k8s)
 ┌──────────────────────┐     ┌───────────────────────────────┐     ┌───────────────────────────────────┐
 │ Sessions UI          │ WS  │ Session controller            │ exec│ cp-session (foundation bundle)    │
 │ /api/live, one per   │◀───▶│  policy → flags, first lines  │────▶│  send: stage, check, copy to FIFO │
 │ tab, resumes by seq  │     │  staged sends, answers        │     │  setsid claude -p (unmodified)    │
 └──────────────────────┘     │  one reader per live session  │◀────│   stdin  ◀── sessions/<id>/in     │
 ┌──────────────────────┐     │  integrity check → redact →   │bytes│   stdout ──▶ out.<n>.jsonl        │
 │ MCP clients via      │◀───▶│  SQLite, blob store           │     │  meta.json · exit.json · lock     │
 │ Portal MCP session_* │     │  guards: credential, trust,   │     │ claude ──▶ Anthropic API or host  │
 └──────────────────────┘     │  budget, caps, Bypass         │     │                                   │
                              └───────────────────────────────┘     └───────────────────────────────────┘
```

1. **Session host** (`/etc/claudepilot/bin/cp-session`) starts `claude -p` detached under `setsid`.
   Stdin is a FIFO in `~/.claudepilot/sessions/<id>/` that Claude Code opens read-write, so writers
   come and go; stdout appends to a JSONL segment on the home volume. Each send is staged and
   checked before one local copy into the FIFO. Readers replay from a byte offset and exit when
   their own stdin closes ✅ (S8). A lock enforces one writer.
2. **Portal controller**, the only client of each process. It turns policy into flags and first
   lines, keeps one reader per live session even when nobody watches, checks every line against
   what it sent (§10.4), redacts and numbers each line into SQLite (images into the blob store),
   answers requests, and enforces guards, budgets and caps.
3. **Browser protocol.** The Web app's one live socket per tab (`/api/live`). Tabs resume from
   their last sequence number; actions carry a client UUID, so a retry never sends twice.

Rejected: the Agent SDK in the portal (would lose output when the exec stream drops, inferred from
S7b; can't reattach; ships its own CLI); a long-running workspace daemon (a v2 option); an attached
`exec` (S7b ✅); tmux (send-keys into a pty fails with `-p` ✅, and hosting the FIFO in tmux only
adds a way to kill the session).

## 8. Functional requirements

### Sessions

| ID   | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ---- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| FR1  | **New session** (`/admin/sessions/new`) is the session screen, empty, in the last-used workspace and folder, the only eligible one, or the one a workspace's **Chat** button passes. A chip above the composer ("api-dev · ~/work/api ▾") opens a two-step sheet: workspace, then folder (`~/work` and git repositories two levels below). Ineligible workspaces stay tappable, with the reason and the way out: "Stopped · Start", "No account pool · Assign", "Pool change in progress", "4 of 4 running · Settings", "Update Claude Code". Mode and Model · Effort picks become launch flags. The first Send runs the trust step if needed (FR43), then starts the session with persona defaults and policy.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| FR2  | The **session list** spans workspaces in attention order: Needs input; Failed and Interrupted; Working (with Starting); Idle; Ended. Ended folds ("… 12 ended"), as does any group past 10 rows. Chips filter by state, one search field matches title, workspace and folder, and MCP-started sessions carry "via MCP". Titles come from the first message, renamable. Persona test sessions never appear (FR42). **Pinned** (2026-10-13, owner): a session can be pinned from its row's ⋯, the session's ⋯, or a swipe to the right on a touch screen (a swipe to the left archives, or unarchives in Archived; past the threshold the pane fills in and the release acts; the row moves inside its own box, the page never scrolls sideways). A pinned session leads its group whatever the grouping; archiving unpins. `pinned_at` (migration 381), `POST`/`DELETE …/pin`. **Order by attention** (T-100, 2026-10-13, owner; replaces T-1's "Pinned first, Idle the default"): the chips run Needs you · In progress (was Working; Starting counts) · Idle · Pinned · Ended · Archived · All. A state chip shows only when it has sessions (or while you are on it); Pinned and All always show; Archived once anything is archived. The list opens on the first chip with sessions in that order (All when there are none), until you pick one; a chip tapped as the list first shows stays picked (T-321: the default of the first render, while still pending, could put the chip back, so All snapped back to Needs you). Pinned overlaps on the chips (a pinned session counts there and on its state's chip). In **All** (no other grouping) the rows sit under headings in the same order, each with its count: a session goes under one heading, Needs you, then In progress, then Pinned (a pin that is idle or ended), then Idle, then Ended; a pinned row that needs you or works leads that heading with its pin marker. Every heading folds except Needs you; in All the separate Needs-you queue card is left out (its heading is it). Phones: chips are icon and count (the chip you are on also says its name), 44 px each, and only the chip row scrolls sideways. Rules in `apps/web/src/features/chat/listOrder.ts` (tested). **The card's corners** (T-100, the owner's iPhone): each row painted a square background over the card's 24 px curve, and the swipe box clipped all the time, which cut a row's ⋯ menu to its first item; now the top and bottom rows take the card's inner corners, the box clips only while a row moves or settles, and the menu opens whole past the card. Observed in Chromium (touch, safe areas) by `tools/qa/session-list.mjs` at 360, 390, 430 and 1280 px, light and dark, with a long title, a long branch, Bypass and Ended chips: 290 checks passed, 0 failing, at a159208 (2026-10-13; before the fix the same harness failed 76 checks: square row corners at rest and mid-swipe, corner pixels in the row colour, and the ⋯ menu with only its first item reachable). `tools/qa/scroll-lock.mjs` 26/26 and `tools/qa/mobile-drag.mjs` 11/11 passed on the same build. After the merge of `integration` (2026-10-14, 29d20ce plus the harness fix): `session-list.mjs` 290 passed, 0 failing, `scroll-lock.mjs` 26/26, `mobile-drag.mjs` 11/11, `session-swipe.mjs` 11/11 (the harness now waits for the chips and swipes a row that needs no scrolling), typecheck clean and the web tests 440/440. **The chip row's overflow** (T-288, 2026-10-14): the phone's icon chips and Select outgrow the row at 360 to 390 px; their sr-only labels (position absolute) had the page as containing block, so the page was 445 px wide at 390 (446 at 360) and the Resume sheet with it. The row is now `relative`, so they scroll inside it. The harnesses had missed it (a phone in emulation widens its layout to fit, so `scrollWidth` equals `innerWidth`); `session-list.mjs` and `recover.mjs` now check `innerWidth` against the screen too. Cluster Jobs: without the fix `session-list.mjs` 7 failing (no sideways scroll at 360 and 390) and `recover.mjs` failing at 390 (list sideways, sheet outside the screen) (claudepilot-job-7da66145); with it (2e0f96b) `session-list.mjs` 290 passed, 0 failing, `recover.mjs` PASS at 4 sizes, `scroll-lock.mjs` 28/28, `mobile-drag.mjs` 11/11, `session-swipe.mjs` 0 failing, typecheck clean, web tests 452/452 (claudepilot-job-7ecdc37e). **Two-line rows with the model and the quota** (T-375, 2026-10-15, owner: "aku pengen tau juga informasi bar quota, dan model yang sedang digunakan, buat lebih compact tapi clean dan tidak bloated"; a row was 4 to 5 lines at 390 px). A row is two lines, a third only when it carries something. The leading icon (40 px, a worker's 32 px) is the state's ring (amber needs you, accent working, a line otherwise) around the session's kind (a role, asleep, removed, a chat); the persona's avatar only when the rows on screen have more than one persona; Bypass is a red shield dot on its corner (an InfoTip says it; the word too from 640 px). Line one: the title (cut short), the pin, the role (an icon on a phone with the name in its tip and for screen readers; hidden when the title already starts with it), the crew count, the time at the right, then the fold and ⋯ (44 px). Line two, muted and one line, **in priority order, which is also the reading order** (T-375 fix, 2026-10-15, after a ux check of c84657f found at 390 px the cost cut mid-number, a row ending in "$", the Claude's name cut to "De" with no ellipsis, and the lead's cost gone): **state · quota bar and percent · cost · model · workspace (only when the card spans several) · Claude's name · branch · ticket** (`LINE_TWO_ORDER`, `lineTwo()` in `rowMeta.ts`, tested). The line is one line high and wraps: an item that does not fit whole moves to a hidden second line with all after it, so a lower-priority item is **dropped whole** and a number is never cut; a name that has to shorten (the Claude's, at most 96 px; the workspace's, 128 px; the branch's, 160 px) ends in an ellipsis, its full text in the tip. `tools/qa/session-rows.mjs` checks it at 390, 640, 1024 and 1280 px, light and dark (no text clipped, the lead's cost whole, each row's workspace in a card of two workspaces, a branch and a ticket closing line two, the asleep row, the lead header 44 px and Select all leaving it out). What follows is the first cut's description of the items: the state (a dot and a word; "Working · 2 agents" from 640 px), the model's short name ("Opus 5.5"; its id and effort in the tip; one helper with the model picker, `modelName.ts`), the Claude and its **mini quota bar** (28 x 4 px: the 5-hour window's percent used from the claude-usage snapshot the top bar's chip reads, neutral below 70, amber from 70, red from 90; a missing, old or reset reading, an unknown one, or a Claude not signed in is a grey bar with no number and "unavailable" in the tip, never a guess; a pool or an API key has no bar; the tip gives the 5-hour and weekly percent, the reset times and the reading's age; under a Claude heading only the bar), the cost (≈ while a turn runs, T-343), the workspace only when the card's rows span more than one (a card whose rows share one workspace does not repeat it), then the branch and ticket; the bar and its percent are an item of their own, before the cost, and the Claude's name a separate, lower one, so a short line (a lead's row at 390 px, with its fold button) drops the name, never the bar, and the bar cannot paint over the cost. Line three: only what it waits on (amber). So a row that does not wait on you is two lines at 390 px, at most 80 px (`tools/qa/session-density.mjs`, T-378; it first measured 90 px for rows with a branch or ticket on a third line, and the same workspace on all 47 rows of a card; its screenshots showed the bar over the cost). The folder left the row (it is on the session page). A worker on its lead's Claude shows no Claude and no bar (the lead's row or header right above says it; T-294's rule, kept because the ticket asks for no repeated labels and `tools/qa/session-crew.mjs` checks it); on another Claude it names it with its bar. "from <lead>" stays only for a worker whose lead is archived (it has no lead header, T-369). Rules in `apps/web/src/features/chat/rowMeta.ts` (tested: rowMeta.test.ts). The tips of the quota bar and the model are titles and screen-reader text, not InfoTips: the row is one link, and a button inside a link is invalid and would open the session on a tap.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

| FR3b | **Interrupted only on evidence (T-1650, 2026-10-19).** The portal marks a process gone (the liveness round, a dropped reader, a Restart's check, an unanswered usage check) only on two `cp-session status` readings of `gone` at least 10 s apart; between them it is unknown and nothing changes. When every live session of a workspace (more than one) reads gone at once, the workspace is suspect, not the processes: one log line, nothing marked, until that has lasted 3 minutes (a real end of them all, a pod restart, is then marked). The host says `gone` only on evidence (no pid file, an empty one, or a pid no process has), reads the pid file without a fork, and answers nothing (exit 4) when it cannot tell. Observed: at 04:01:38 UTC one round Interrupted all 13 live sessions of `kubernetes/kohi-event` (Bypass off) while their processes ran on; the workspace's cgroup was at `memory.max` (over a million `max` events), where a helper's fork or exec could fail and its empty answer read as gone. |

| FR43 | **Folder trust.** Print mode skips Claude Code's trust dialog and starts a project's `.mcp.json` servers unasked (Secrets & env T3). Before the first session in a folder, and whenever the scanned files' hash changes, the person sees by name its `.mcp.json` servers, the hooks, allow rules and plugins in `.claude/settings*.json`, and `env` names, and confirms "Trust this folder", stored per workspace and folder. Untrusted folders run only where Settings' `allowManagedHooksOnly` and `allowManagedMcpServersOnly` locks are on. MCP clients can't grant trust. **Rule changes (T-38, 2026-10-13):** with `chat.trust.autoPermissionOnly` on (off by default, a confirmation to turn on), a folder trusted before whose changed files hold nothing but `$schema` and `permissions` with `allow`, `deny` and `ask` lists of strings (no `.mcp.json`, every file read; no hook, server, plugin, env, helper command, `defaultMode` or `additionalDirectories`) is trusted again by itself at the launch that finds it (start, resume, wake, restart, Move, auto move), with a `trust.auto` audit row (medium: folder, old and new hash, files, allow count). The host's `trustscan` says `rulesOnly`; the portal also checks the names. Anything else still waits for a person; an auto move refused for trust is recorded as `folder_untrusted` and notified ("Move blocked"), a wake refused for it notifies `session_failed` with "trust the folder again". **First trust by repository (T-366, D-62, T-403 option a, 2026-10-15):** under the same setting (label "Trust folders by repository") a folder never trusted before is trusted by itself ONLY when it is the top of a git clone or worktree of a repository that already has a trusted folder in the same workspace: the same origin remote (normalised: host in lower case, `user:password@`, default port, query, fragment, trailing `/` and `.git` out, so https, ssh and `git@host:path` read the same; a local path as `file:<path>`; a relative or unreadable URL never matches) and the same scan hash, which pins byte for byte its settings files (`.mcp.json`, `.claude/settings*.json`) and the skill, command and agent `.md` files the scan read to those of that folder, which is scanned again at that moment and must still be at the hash it was trusted at (at most 10 such folders are tried; one gone or refused is skipped). Neither folder may hold what the hash does not pin (third review): a hook (its script), an MCP server (its code), a plugin, env, another settings key or helper, a settings file that can't be read, or a skill folder the scan could not finish (a link, a newline in a name, more than 200 files or one too big: the hashing stops there) refuses with its reason by name (the classifier's refusals except the broad-rule one, which the hash pins and a person trusted in that repository), and both folders must say the same `extras`. `trustscan` says `origin` (T-366: only beside a settings file, not at the files root, and only for the top folder of a git work tree; the URL with credentials, query and fragment taken out by the host and again by the portal; left out otherwise). First trust is never by what the files say: in-folder Edit plus any exact build or test rule (`npm test`, `make`, a script) is any command, which no reading of the rules can catch, so a repository with no trusted folder of the same origin waits for a person, whatever its settings say; a forged origin matches only files identical to ones a person trusted, and anything outside the hash refuses as above. Audited `trust.auto` reason `same-repo` (folder, hash, `matched`: the trusted folder, files, allow count), actor `portal:same-repo` on the trust row. A refusal's 409 says why: "it is not the top folder of a git clone or worktree with an origin remote" , the classifier's reason (for example "it loads hooks"), or "no trusted folder of the same repository has exactly these settings, skills, commands and agents". **Changes and broad rules (T-366, D-55):** a rules-only CHANGE to a folder already trusted is trusted again by itself only when the classifier below finds no broad allow rule and nothing that loads (the re-trust above); `trustscan` says `broadAllow` (a boolean, never the rule; a host that doesn't say counts as broad) and `extras` (fixed names, a host that doesn't say counts as `unscanned`). **Checked:** a blanket (a bare `Bash`, `Edit`, `Write`, `MultiEdit`, `NotebookEdit`, `WebFetch` or `Read`, a wildcard tool name, a spec of wildcards only); an `Edit`/`Write`/`Read` path that is absolute (`/x` and `//x` alike, `/x` taken as absolute, the stricter reading) or in home (`~/x`, `$HOME/x`) and not inside the folder, a relative `**`, any `..`; an `Edit`/`Write`/`MultiEdit`/`NotebookEdit` path inside the folder that may reach its `.git` (hooks and `core.fsmonitor` run programs) or `.claude` (the settings): the folder itself, a first segment `.git`, `.claude` or a glob (`Edit(./**)`, `Edit(*.md)`), or a single segment (gitignore-style, it matches at any depth), unless the same settings file denies `Edit(.git)`, `Edit(.git/**)` and `Edit(.claude/**)` (a worktree's `.git` is a file, so `.git` itself too; a skill's `allowed-tools` has no deny list); a Bash rule with `<` or `>` anywhere (a redirect), or with an argument that is a path (starts with `/`, `~` or `$HOME`, or follows `=`) outside the folder or on its `.git` or `.claude` (`Bash(cp a ~/.bashrc)`, `Bash(rm -rf ~)`), or any other `$`; a Bash rule with a wildcard (`*`, `:*`) whose command is not on a short vetted list (`ls`, `pwd`, `echo`, named bare; `echo` only because a redirect is caught): an allow list, so `cat *`, `head *`, `tail *`, `wc *`, `grep *` (they read any file, credentials included), `git status *` (`core.fsmonitor`), `make *`, `sed *`, `npm run *`, `npm test:*`, `node *`, `git push:*`, `git diff *` (`--output` writes anywhere), `kubectl get *` (a kubeconfig can run a program) and `rg *` (`--pre`) are broad; an exact Bash rule (no wildcard) whose command runs another command (sudo, env, the shells, eval, exec, xargs, flock, timeout, nice, nohup, stdbuf, time, watch, setsid, chroot, ssh, awk and the like; `node -e`, `python -c`, `perl -e`; `find -exec`; `npx`/`npm exec`/`uv run` checked on what they launch; git `-c`; `kubectl`/`docker exec`), matched on the base name, unquoted, in any case; parentheses inside a Bash rule; and, beside a settings file (not at the files root), the frontmatter of `.claude/skills/**/SKILL.md`, `.claude/commands/**/*.md` and `.claude/agents/**/*.md`: `hooks` or a `permissionMode` names the kind in `extras` (`skill:hooks`, `agent:mode`), an `allowed-tools` entry goes through the same classifier, and a link in those folders, more than 200 files, one over 256 KiB, YAML it doesn't read line by line (flow mappings, merge, complex or quoted keys, an indented first key), a `...` line (only `---` ends the frontmatter) or any key outside an allow list of plain-text keys (`name`, `description`, `model`, `tools`, `color`, `argument-hint`, `allowed-tools`: `mcpServers`, `hooks`, `permissionMode` and anything new) is `unreadable`; any of these clears `rulesOnly`, and the files read are in the hash after the settings files, so editing a skill asks again. When in doubt it is broad. The `trust.auto` row says `reason` `same-repo` or `changed` (folder, hash, the matched folder or the old hash, files, allow count). With the setting on, a refusal's 409 says why after the usual message ("Not trusted by itself: it loads hooks; an allow rule runs any command or covers everything"), names only. A portal worktree is still trusted first by copying its repository's trust at the same hash (`portal:worktree`, no audit row), then by this policy; since T-435 the copy passes the same-repo checks first (nothing outside the hash in the worktree or in the repository scanned again, the same `extras`), so a linked skill (a relative link resolves elsewhere from the worktree), a hook or an MCP server waits for a person, or for this policy, which refuses it too. **Not checked** (observed limits, not hoped-for ones): in a CHANGE, an exact rule that runs code the folder controls (npm test, make, a script) plus editing inside the folder is the same as any command; the classifier cannot catch it (`Bash(npm test)` with `Edit(./src/**)`, `Bash(scripts/job-run.sh --ref x -- npm test)`), which is why first trust is by repository (D-62); the origin URL is the repository's own config, so an agent in the workspace can set it, and then only the byte-identical files keep the match to what was trusted; whether Claude Code splits a compound command (`;`, `&&`, ` | `) under a vetted wildcard rule (`Bash(echo *)`); a symlink inside the folder that leads into `.git`or`.claude`; skills, commands and agents in a folder with no settings file (it needs no trust, as before T-366); and the home folder's own skills. |

| FR5  | User messages are bubbles with image thumbnails; replies are full-width markdown, with Copy always visible on code blocks and each reply; a turn's work (thinking, tool calls, the notes between them and sub-agents' lines) folds into one **activity line** above its reply: each step is a small icon (terminal, file, edit, search, web, agent, MCP, thinking) in an overlapping group, and only the latest is named beside it, shimmering while the turn runs ("Read MEMORY.md"); done, the group is the tool calls and the last one is named; a failed step's icon is red; and it opens to the steps, each an expandable row (read-only runs group; a path shows as its file name, in full when opened), and the turn's usage. The turn's time, tokens and cost aren't on screen otherwise: a "Done · 12 s · 126k in · 659 out · $0.137" pill rises in when a turn finishes and folds away after a few seconds (tapping it opens Usage), and ⋯ → **Usage** lists the session's totals and every turn. New bubbles rise in (a spring; nothing moves with reduced motion); history doesn't. System rows cover retries, interruptions, control changes, stops and peer messages; "Ready" shows only in Show all details. ⋯ → "Show all details" opens every row; "Show less" goes back. **Sub-agents (2026-10-13, T-31, T-41):** a sub-agent's lines, streaming or stored, never stand as a reply and never take the main reply's place: they fold into the activity line of the turn whose `Task`/`Agent` call started it (also when a background agent writes after the main turn ended), where the call is one agent row (its name, model and latest line, shimmering while it runs) that opens the agent's detail view (§22); while one runs, the activity line names it and what it does. The reply slot only ever shows the main agent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

Consecutive browser calls group into one row ("Browser · 12 steps · github.com", with Watch); calls refused while a person has control show as one muted "Waited" row, and take-overs as system rows ([Browser & desktop](browser-desktop-pool.md) §15.6). **Input and Output (T-24, as built 2026-10-13):** a tool row opens (closed by default, a failing one open; a tap is remembered per row while the page lives) to two labelled blocks. **Input** is what the call was given, as the portal kept it on the `tool_use` event (`toolInput`: the fields the row shows of a built-in tool, an MCP tool's whole input, nothing for the rest, so a sub-agent's prompt is never stored; strings as written, disguising characters as `⟦U+XXXX⟧`, redacted, at most 8,000 characters each and 24,000 in all, "cut short" when bounded): Bash's command with its real line breaks, monospace, wrapped or scrolled at a switch, lightly coloured as shell by the web's own tokenizer (`shellTokens.ts`: commands, keywords, strings, flags, operators, variables, comments, heredoc bodies; its tokens joined are always the text), with Copy and "N lines"; Edit, MultiEdit and Write as the path and their lines (removed red, added green, a few lines of context); Read as the path and range; Grep and Glob as the pattern and path; an MCP tool as pretty JSON. A call stored before inputs were kept shows its one-line summary. **Output** is the stored result, monospace: up to 15,000 characters for a command or a failure (past that, the head and the tail with "… N characters not kept …" between), 2,000 for the other tools; past 85 lines it shows the first and last 40 with "Show all (N lines)" between; a failure in the danger tone with its exit status when the result says it ("exit 2"); images as before. The approval card and the glance widget's review draw what is asked with the same renderer (`CodeBlock.tsx`): the whole `full` text, always wrapped, coloured but never dimmed (a comment is not greyed in a review), escapes marked; what they guarantee about the tail and the scroll-to-the-end rule is unchanged. Agent rows are live (their latest line shimmering) and open that agent's detail view ✅ (§22).
