------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| FR39  | Caps count live processes and persona test runs (Settings FR13): 2 per workspace and 10 in total in C1, then Settings' values. A launch or wake also needs free memory (limit − (usage − inactive file)) for the workspace's measured average session RSS, MCP servers included, or 600 MB before any measurement. An ultracode session counts as two. A wake at a cap first sleeps the longest-idle eligible session, else waits ("Waiting for a slot · 4 of 4 running"). Refusals name the cap and its source. **As built (2026-10-12), first cut:** the caps are the shared settings `sessions.maxLivePerWorkspace` (4) and `sessions.maxLiveTotal` (10), read each time a session starts or wakes; an Ultracode session counts as two; at a cap the longest-idle session that may sleep (idle, nothing open or running, not moving) is put to sleep (audited `cap.slept`) to make room, and a start or wake with none that can is refused (409 `SESSION_CAP`, naming the cap, how many are running and the setting). **As built (2026-10-15, T-417): the cap never sleeps a lead.** A lead is a session with workers that haven't ended, one Keep going is on for or that names a program (a track's chat), or a chat of its own (no parent) with a workers limit (`maxWorkers`) set; it waits for its workers most of the time, so longest-idle alone picked leads (02:23 and 02:28 UTC that day: a slept lead lost Bypass and stopped routing while its workers worked on). The cap sleeps idle workers first, then idle chats without workers, longest-idle first in each (audited `cap.slept`, `worker` true or false). With none that may sleep, the start waits for a slot instead of being refused: a new session is made at once, asleep (not counted), and its start waits; the session says why (`slotWait` on the session, "Waiting for a slot · 4 of 4 running in <workspace> (Settings › Running agents)", and the same line in its transcript), first come first served (a newcomer, a new session or a wake, queues behind every start already waiting in its workspace, and behind any waiting on the total cap), looked at again every second; a slot given counts as running from that moment until its launch has settled (a slot granted and not yet live, and a launch in flight, both count), so a waiting start never takes the same room; after 15 minutes the new session reads Interrupted with the cap's reason (a later message starts it) and a waiting wake is refused with 409 `SESSION_CAP` as before. `session_spawn` answers at once with the wait in its summary and `slot_wait`; the worker gets its settings and first instruction when it starts, and no other message (its parent's `session_message`, its crew's) reaches it before them, waiting or not; a queued start that fails ends the worker at once and its lead gets a "Spawn report" line saying why, and settings not confirmed after a queued start reach the lead in such a line too. A lead that sleeps in Bypass (the idle sleep) keeps it: "Bypass paused for sleep", `bypass.left` with `kept`; its wake puts it back through the path a Restart uses (`#keepBypass`, `wake`), which checks now the policy, the session's capability and that its new process was launched with the flag, and, for a wake only, that it is still a lead and that the person's own `bypass.entered` record for this chat (the newest by a person) says the acknowledgement was ticked and the server code was fresh when the person entered (the wake asks for no new code); any check that fails leaves it on the start mode, said in the transcript ("Bypass not kept after the wake: …") and audited. A non-lead's wake keeps FR20: off after sleep. The free-memory check is built as the memory budget (FR39b, T-416), not as the measured average RSS above. **As built (2026-10-31, T-3215):** a start or wake an agent waits for (a worker's queued spawn, a wake asked by an agent's `session_message` or control) waits at most `agentSlotWaitMs` (60 s) for a slot, then ends with the cap's reason (a queued worker is ended and its lead told why); a person's start or wake keeps the 15-minute wait. A resume, mode, model, effort or ultracode control on a session that waits for a slot answers at once (409, the wait text) instead of waiting behind it with no answer. **T-3222:** a worker queued for a slot has no process, and every process starts in `default` (the spawn's mode is a control applied after the start), so it read `default` until it started; it now shows the mode its spawn applies then (`plannedMode`, display only: kept in memory, never stored, never read by a permission check; `session_family` gives it as `mode` with `mode_planned: true`, the app's mode chip "when it starts"). Once the process runs, the real mode alone shows, so an Auto the start refused (the Auto check runs at start as for any worker, audited `mode.refused`) reads as the mode it really has. Launching the process directly in the spawn's mode (a launch-flag change) was not built. Not built: persona test runs counted separately, showing `slotWait` in the app.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| FR39b | **Memory budget (T-416, D-61, 2026-10-15).** Before a start (create, before any worktree is made) and before every wake, the portal reads the workspace's memory through the runtime's exec as the workspace user: `memory.max` and `anon` + `active_file` from `memory.stat` of cgroup v2 (never `memory.current`, which counts the page cache the kernel gives back first). It refuses at once when used + the need + 1.5 GiB headroom (`MEMORY_HEADROOM_BYTES`) is over the limit. The need is 350 MB (`SESSION_MEMORY_ESTIMATE_BYTES`) for the session, one more per Agent-tool sub-agent a woken session has running, and one more per launch in that workspace not yet live. The refusal is 409 `MEMORY_BUDGET` with a readable reason ("Not enough memory in <workspace>: 6.4 of 8.0 GiB in use, and this session needs about 350 MB with 1.5 GiB kept free. End or sleep a session there, or wait for one to finish."), is audited (`memory.refused`, medium: where, used, limit, need, sub-agents, pending launches), and leaves the session as it was (no row on a start, asleep on a wake). An unavailable reading (no cgroup v2 files, `memory.max` is `max`, the exec fails) never blocks; it is logged once per workspace, and the live cap (FR39) stays the ceiling. A reading is kept 5 s. **The hold (2026-10-15, T-416 review):** a start, a wake and a queued start whose slot came each hold their estimate from the check on, in the check's own synchronous step, so two at once (two wakes after an OOM, a create right after a wake) see each other. A queued start holds nothing while it waits and is checked (and held) again when its slot comes; refused then, it reads Interrupted with the budget's reason (audited `at: queued`). A launch that fails lets go at once; one that runs keeps its hold until a reading taken at least 10 s after it settled (`MEMORY_SETTLE_MS`), so a cached reading from before, or a Claude still growing to its size, can't let a second through. A Restart of a running process is not checked: it needs no more than the process it stops, and a refusal after the stop would leave a Bypass lead in the start mode. A Restart of a session that is asleep or gone is a wake: it is checked and held as one (audited `at: restart`) before anything is stopped or changed, so a refusal leaves it, and its mode, as it was (T-416 review 6310). While a queued start checks its memory it still holds the session's lifecycle work, so a message sent meanwhile waits for that start instead of running a wake check of its own; a queued worker refused there is ended and its lead is told it could not start, with the reason. **The alert:** the 60 s liveness round reads each workspace with live sessions at most once a minute; past 85% of `memory.max` a Needs you item (kind `workspace_memory`, why `question`, so a Web Push through the attention bridge) names the five largest processes by name and MB only (no command lines); it stays one item while above 80% and clears below it; the next crossing is a new item and a new push. Code: `chat/memory.ts`, `workspaces/memory.ts`. **As built (T-675):** a lead running more Agent-tool sub-agents than N gets a `[Portal note: …]` line in the frame of every portal message to it (between the header and the text), where N is its own `max_workers` or, when none is set, 5 (D-43); the frame is the one place the portal already speaks to Claude, so a person's own typed message is never touched. The wake's `memory.refused` audit names the actor whose message woke the session (`ui` for a person, else the portal actor; Resume and Recover likewise); a path with no actor of its own still says `portal`. An item id with a workspace id over 100 characters is clipped and hashed (`memoryItemId`), so its Web Push is not dropped by the notify bus's 200-character subject id cap. **Bypass after an OOM (2026-10-16, T-416 S2):** a lead (FR39 definition) whose process vanished in Bypass (an OOM kill) keeps its Bypass on the record (`bypass.left`, why `stopped`, `kept`). When it runs again (a message, a resume) the restore goes through the same gated path as a lead's wake (`how: lost`): the policy, the capability, still a lead, and the person's own entry with the acknowledgement and a fresh server code on record, each checked again. The process is launched with the flag only if they pass, and a failing gate leaves it on the start mode (`bypass.left`, the gate named). A session that was not a lead, or any other relaunch, still drops Bypass (FR20a). **The kept record does not outlive a deliberate stop (T-416 S2b):** a workspace stop, a person's end of the chat and the Bypass restart each write a newer `bypass.left` (`superseded`, not `kept`), so a later message cannot bring Bypass back from a record that came before. Tests: `chat/bypassLost.test.ts`. **Lost touch is not a stop (T-1094, 2026-10-18):** when the session host stops answering (status unknown) and the re-follow retries run out ("Lost touch"), the process may still run. The session reads Interrupted and the row pauses Bypass as before, but the control state stored for THAT process is kept. A later start that attaches to it (run.lock held) reloads what it runs in, so its own Bypass report no longer ends the lead. The attach takes it out of Bypass first, fail closed (note "Bypass paused: the portal lost touch with Claude Code while it ran in Bypass.", `bypass.left` why `lost_touch`, `kept` for a lead). A lead's resume then puts Bypass back through every gate (`how: 'lost'`). A process confirmed gone is unchanged: its state is dropped, and a new process that reports Bypass without an entry ends for integrity. Tests: `chat/bypassLostTouch.test.ts`. **As built (T-1646, 2026-10-19, the k3s API outage):** after a vanishing (lost touch or confirmed gone), a top-level crew lead (marked a lead at its start, or one that started workers) counts as a lead for keeping Bypass even with no live worker, no workers limit and no Keep going (the most likely way the main Konfersi lead lost Bypass for good). The relaunch still asks every gate (policy, capability, the person's own entry with the acknowledgement and a fresh code, the grant); one that fails leaves it out of Bypass with a note naming it. A worker, or a chat that is not a lead, is unchanged; the cap still sleeps only by the T-417 lead test. A resume whose session host cannot be checked (the install failed: "fetch failed" while the API was down) now reads Interrupted, not Ended; a first start that fails it still ends. Tests: `chat/bypassAfterOutage.test.ts`. **T-1711, T-1692 (2026-10-20):** a dropped follower the host reads `live` never counts toward "Lost touch" (the re-follow backs off to 30 s instead); a top-level crew lead (marked, or with workers) keeps Bypass over a sleep by the same rule as over a vanishing, and its wake asks every gate; a Move (manual or automatic) of a lead whose sleep or vanishing kept Bypass puts it back through that wake's gates (`how: wake` or `lost`); the gated re-entry after an attach waits for the fail-closed switch out of Bypass. The supervisor's 45-minute silence wake (T-727) covers every top-level crew lead too, and lanes of a Keep going track. Tests: `chat/bypassKeep.test.ts`, `autotransfer/t423supervisor.test.ts`. | FR40  | Budgets: $10 per session in C1, then Settings'. Each result's cost delta adds to the session's total, and from streamed usage (`message_start` and `message_delta` ✅, S3; subagents' `task_progress`) the portal stops a turn mid-way at the budget ("Budget reached · est. $10.02 of $10"), with `--max-budget-usd` as the backstop (§10.2). Costs say "est.": interrupted streams count 0 ✅ (S4), and classifier calls and probes aren't counted. Without a price (custom hosts), budgets are off ("Budget unavailable: no price") and turn caps apply.                                                     |
| FR42  | Events, requests and files are kept 90 days after last activity; persona test sessions are hidden and deleted when their test ends or after 24 hours. Resume needs Claude Code's transcript (`cleanupPeriodDays`, 30 days by default); after that a session is read-only. | FR49  | **Usage over time** (the Usage tab): every finished request (turn) is stored as metrics, one row per model it used, from the change in Claude Code's per-model running totals (`modelUsage`), so sub-agents count with the request that started them; the row names the session, workspace and Claude (whose sign-in or pool it ran as) and how many sub-agents it started. Requests stored before this are backfilled from their cost. The tab shows, for a window of 30 minutes, 1 hour, 12 hours, 1 day, 7 days or 1 month (buckets on the viewer's clock): cost, requests and sub-agents, tokens in (with the cached share) and out; one column chart over time for cost, tokens or requests, each column a button with its value and time; the split by Claude (tap one to narrow everything to it) and by model; and a table of the same numbers. Short windows refresh every 30 s. The context window shows as a small ring by Send (FR19). **Audited (2026-10-12):** buckets line up with the viewer's clock and cover the whole window (tests, several offsets); usage rows survive deleting a chat and keep the Claude of each turn across a move; a workspace that stops interrupts its sessions at once (their turns' usage is already stored); a portal restart in the middle of a turn re-attaches and the turn's result and usage arrive. Not audited: very large transcripts, and the session list at thousands of sessions (3,000 take 65–100 ms and 1.9 MB; not paginated yet).                                                                          |
| FR50  | **Git worktrees per session** (Q12 decided: a worktree per session when the person asks, a shared folder otherwise). _Portal-managed, not Claude Code's `-w`_: the portal must validate the branch and base, check the new folder's project settings for trust, know the folder and branch (lists, tickets, CI, Files and Diff) and never remove one without being told, and `-w` does none of that. New session → "Work in a git worktree" (optional branch and base), or `worktree` in the create request (tickets, Portal MCP). The portal asks the session host (`wt-add`) for `git worktree add` from the repository containing the folder, in `<repo>.worktrees/<branch with / as ->` beside it (inside what Files and Diff may show; a repository at the files root itself is refused), on a new branch from the base (the repository's HEAD by default) or on an existing branch no other worktree has. Default branch: `ticket/<n>-<words>` for a ticket's chat (CI parses it), else `session/<words>-<6 hex>`. The chat's `cwd` is the worktree (the same subfolder when the request named one); Claude Code runs in it; the header and rows show the branch; Files and Diff work on it. **Hardening**: every git call is the host's hardened one (no hook, `core.hooksPath`, fsmonitor, pager, external diff or textconv; every configured filter's clean, smudge and process switched off; no prompts; timeouts), because `git worktree add` runs the repository's post-checkout hook. Verified with real git: a hook in `.git/hooks` and in `core.hooksPath`, a clean/smudge/process filter and an fsmonitor script all stay unrun, and plain git does run them. LFS content is therefore not smudged in a portal-made worktree (pointer files). **Trust**: the repository's folder must be trusted first (FR43); the worktree is scanned and is trusted at once when its settings hash is the one trusted for the repository, or by FR43's rules-only policy when that is on, otherwise the person is asked (409 `FOLDER_UNTRUSTED` for the new folder, carrying the plan to send again; the worktree stays `reserved` for the retry and a start that never comes through is swept after an hour, removed if nothing is in it). **Removing**: delete and archive keep the folder unless the person chooses _Remove the folder_; the dialog first reads what removal would lose (`wt-status`: changed files, untracked files, commits only this branch has). Changes or untracked files refuse the removal (409 `WORKTREE_DIRTY`, before the chat is ended) unless the person ticks that they may be discarded (`force`, audited as medium); the branch is deleted only when fully merged, so commits are never lost with the folder. `GET /api/chat/sessions/:id/worktree` (status), `GET /api/chat/worktrees?workspaceId=&folder=` (the portal's and, for a repository, git's), `DELETE …/:id?worktree=keep\|remove\|force`, `POST …/:id/archive?worktree=…`. Bulk actions never remove worktrees.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| FR51  | **Sessions that start and message sessions** (multi-agent; portal-mediated only: a session never talks to another except through the portal). A session's Claude has four tools through its own Portal MCP token (I8; `chat/portalTools.ts`): `session_spawn` (a worker in the same workspace, never in Bypass, with a first instruction and optionally a git worktree of its own, FR50; T-294: `claude` picks its Claude: `same`, the parent's and the default; a Claude's name or id, only one on a plan (an API key's or a pool's, billed per use, or one whose plan can't be read now, is refused, and the refusal lists no Claudes); or `auto`: of the Claudes the Move ranking classes `room` (every Claude of the workspace, the parent's included) whose reading is measured and fresh by the auto-transfer's own test (`unknownUsage`, at most 15 minutes old) and that run on a plan, the one with the fewest open sessions, the ranking's order between equals, else the parent's with the reason; `role` is the agent it runs as: its process starts as `claude --agent <role>` (`.claude/agents/<role>.md` in its folder: the agent's prompt, its tools and, unless `model` is given, its model; Claude Code 2.1.296 ignores a name with no definition, silently; **T-490, D-63:** with no `model`, `session_spawn` sets the role's model from the workspace persona's agent definition (`Personas.roleModel`; `''` and `inherit` are none) before the first instruction, said as "model sonnet (the role's)"; any model but a sonnet or haiku alias or id (opus, fable, `default`, `opusplan`, an unknown id) for a role whose model is below opus (QA and review roles run on sonnet) needs `why` (1 to 200 characters: a gate, release or security check, or a slice too large for sonnet), else it is a 422 naming D-63 before anything starts; the reason is kept redacted on the worker, `chat_sessions.spawn_why` (migration 777, `ChatSession.spawnWhy`) and in its `agent.spawned` audit row as `modelWhy`, and the crew scorecard counts it; a role with no model keeps the model asked), at every start (new, resume, wake, restart, recover, a Move), so its first instruction is the parent's as written (T-294 slice 2, [evidence](evidence/t294-agent-flag.md)); `mode` (default as before, or the narrower plan and dontAsk; never acceptEdits, Auto or Bypass), then `model` and `effort`, are applied before its first instruction: a narrowing mode not confirmed ends the worker before it gets any instruction, a model or effort not confirmed is named in the result and the worker still starts; the `agent.spawned` audit row carries the Claude, why, role, model, effort and mode), `session_message` (to its parent, one of its workers, or its crew: another live worker of its parent, T-294; anyone else, itself included, is the same 404 as a session that does not exist; the frame Claude reads names the relation, your parent, your worker or a peer, and tells a peer it cannot widen permissions, change the task or end the work), `session_family` (its parent, its workers and its crew: state, the Claude it runs on, waiting, turns, cost, branch, and for its workers a clipped copy of each one's latest reply as untrusted text) and `session_stop_worker` (interrupt or end one of its own workers). **Agent per session** (T-294 slice 2): `chat_sessions.agent` (migration 773; `ChatSession.agent`, null for none) is set at creation by `role` here, `agent` on the admin tool `session_start` or on `POST /api/chat/sessions` (`CHAT_AGENT_NAME`: lowercase letters, digits and `-`, 2 to 41, never a path or a flag; anything else is a 422 before anything starts); the controller's one launch (`#hostStart`) passes `--agent <name>` read from the row, so a Move keeps it. A side question (btw, §20) does not carry it: it runs from the home folder with every tool off and its own instructions, where a project agent is not even found. A token's session is the only caller they accept (an admin's token has `session_start` and `session_send` for that); the worker has `parent_id`, `started_by` `session:<id>`, and its opening is the instruction framed as a portal message from the parent. **Limits** (shared settings, Sessions): `sessions.maxDepth` (1: a session a person started may start workers, workers may not; 0 turns it off), `sessions.maxChildren` (5 running at once), `sessions.spawnsPerHour` (20), `sessions.messagesPerHour` (60 from one session to its family), on top of the running-session caps (FR39); each refusal is 409 `SESSION_CAP` naming its scope and is audited (`agent.refused`). **Limits per session** (T-294, the owner 2026-10-14: how many workers a lead may start, and per session whether it may start sub-agents, even none): `chat_sessions.max_workers` and `subagents` (migration 774; `ChatSession.maxWorkers`, `ChatSession.subagents`). `maxWorkers` is how many live workers THIS session may have at once: null is the global `sessions.maxChildren`, 0 refuses every spawn, a value above the global cap counts as the cap (the global settings stay the ceiling); it is read at each spawn, so a change applies at once, and its refusal is 409 `SESSION_CAP` scope `session_workers` (audited `agent.refused`). `subagents` off launches the process with `--disallowedTools Agent Task Workflow RemoteTrigger` (a forked skill can still run a sub-agent: the known gap) (the sub-agent tool under both its names) at every start through the one launch (new, resume, wake, restart, recover, a Move), so a change applies at the next start; Claude Code 2.1.296 was seen to drop both tools and keep `--agent` and the portal's `--allowedTools` working ([evidence](evidence/t294-subagents-flag.md)). It is on or off only: Claude Code has no flag for a number of sub-agents, so none is offered. Who sets them: `session_spawn` takes `max_workers` and `subagents` for the worker, never wider than the parent's own (a worker's `max_workers` at most the parent's effective value, sub-agents on only when the parent's are; what is not given is the parent's; a wider ask is a 422 before anything is made, audited `agent.refused` scope `worker_limits`), and depth still applies; a person (`PATCH CHAT_API.limits`, `manage` on the session: another user's session is the same 404) and an admin token (`session_configure`) change them, audited `session.limits`, the answer saying `restartNeeded` when the live process was launched with the other sub-agents setting (as built it compares the stored values, so turning sub-agents back says `restartNeeded` too; the app keeps what the process was launched with while Session info is open and shows nothing then). **In the app** (T-294, 2026-10-14): Session info has **Workers**, a stepper (− and + of 44 px, the value between them: None, 1, 2 … up to the global `sessions.maxChildren`, where it reads "Default (5)" and stores null, since the setting is the ceiling anyway; the cap is read from the settings list when the sheet opens, and without it the stepper shows the number only, up to 20), saved 0.4 s after the last tap or when the sheet closes, and **Sub-agents**, a switch; each explained in an InfoTip. A sub-agents change the live process needs a restart for shows one line under them, "Sub-agents off from the next restart." with **Restart**, the existing Restart sheet (not offered when the workspace is removed), or "… at the queued restart." when one is queued; nothing once a restart is under way, the process sleeps or the session ended. The role (`agent`) shows as a badge with an icon only when set: in the session header (the badge from 640 px; on a phone its icon, a 44 px target, which opens Session info, whose **Role** row names it with an InfoTip), on the session's row in the list and under its title in the lead's worker list. Rules in `apps/web/src/features/chat/crewLimits.ts` (tested); observed in Chromium by `tools/qa/session-limits.mjs` (below). A session never changes limits, its own or another's (a `session:` actor is refused; the admin tool is out of an agent token's scope). A second probe took `Workflow` and `RemoteTrigger` away too (none of the four in `system/init`); not covered: a skill that runs in a forked context still runs a sub-agent. **Provenance**: the parent's transcript notes "Started “title”" and "Sent to “title”: …", the worker's shows a "From session" card, the list shows the tree with a rollup (Crew, below); audit `agent.spawned` and `agent.ended_children`. **Ending a parent** asks what becomes of the workers still running (Leave them running, the default, or End them too; `?children=end` on end, archive and delete; ended deepest first). T-294's additions are tested against the test kit's fake process only (no real multi-Claude workspace yet); the live check follows the deploy. Verified before T-294 with Claude Code 2.1.296 in a Docker workspace: a session called `session_spawn` over MCP with `worktree`, the worker ran in its own worktree, answered the framed message, `session_family` showed it, and ending the parent with `children=end` ended it. **Crew in the list and on the page** (T-294, 2026-10-14, the owner: see which session each worker belongs to and which Claude it runs on, without a bloated list): a worker sits under its lead on a rail; the lead's row carries an icon and the number it started (however deep; the words, "6 started · 1 needs you · …", in its tip and for a screen reader; amber while one needs you); live workers show in spawn order (they do not move as they work), ended ones fold behind one "N done" row, shut until opened, which keeps its place; a worker names its Claude (a badge) only when it differs from its lead's. In All, a worker that does not need you sits under its lead's heading; one that needs you keeps Needs you (on top), under a header for its lead. The tree always shows (T-369, the owner, 2026-10-15): wherever a worker is shown while its lead is not among the shown rows but is in the list and not archived (a chip such as In progress while the lead waits idle for reports, a search, another heading or grouping), the worker sits under a LEAD HEADER, a muted context row for the lead (title, state, crew count, Claude; a tap opens it), with all that lead's workers in the view under one header in spawn order, deeper levels kept. The header is not a match: it is not in the chip's or the heading's count, Select all does not take it, and it has no swipe, checkbox or ⋯. It sits where its first worker would sit, and a lead with live workers under it is ordered by them (by itself only when it needs you; a header always by them). In a chip, a lead that matches with workers under it shows as the same header line, its state updated (a match: it counts, Select all takes it, it has a checkbox while selecting; still no swipe or ⋯), so when the lead flips between Idle and Working the header keeps its place and height and nothing below it moves (QA A9; `tools/qa/session-crew.mjs` measures worker A before and after the flip, 1 px). Only a worker whose lead is archived (no header) is a row of its own with a "from <lead>" link. The chips' counts do not change; a heading counts the sessions that sit under it. The session page shows, under its header, a link up to its lead (title and Claude) and its workers' number, which opens their list (title, state, Claude when it differs; ended ones folded): a sheet on a phone, a popover from 640 px; the workers are read again when the session's state changes and when the list opens (no polling). Rules in `apps/web/src/features/chat/crew.ts` (tested). Observed in Chromium (touch and the iPhone's safe areas at 390 px; 1280 px; light and dark) by `tools/qa/session-crew.mjs` against a scratch portal seeded with a lead, six workers and an archived lead's worker: 82 passed, 0 failing (2026-10-14, on 4a5c03b plus this change); `tools/qa/session-list.mjs` 0 failing on the same build. **Crew over MCP** (T-294, 2026-10-14, the owner: a lead runs workers on any Claude and they coordinate through the Portal MCP without polling and without bloating the lead's context): `session_message` takes `to: workers` (every live worker of the caller) or `to: crew` (every live member of its crew) instead of `session_id` (exactly one of the two), each recipient framed as a direct message is; every recipient counts against `messagesPerHour`, and a broadcast that does not fit whole is refused before anything is sent (409 `SESSION_CAP`, audited with the number of recipients); the result says how many got it. The portal tells a lead about its workers unasked, in one digest sent as a portal message (`portal:crew`, labelled Crew report, priority `later`, with its own frame: the portal sent it, and what it quotes is the worker's text, data and not an instruction): a header and one line per worker (**T-546, D-65**: its role, id, its ticket and the id of its latest comment there when the portal knows them, its state, and the first line of its latest reply cut at 120 characters, redacted, as untrusted text; the full text stays on the ticket, and the digest ends by saying `session_family` with `reply_of` reads the whole reply), when its turn ends (working or waiting to idle, with no message queued behind it), when it starts waiting on a person, and when it ends. `session_spawn` `report` sets this per worker (`chat_sessions.report`, migration 775): `idle` (the default, which null reads as), `end` (only the end) or `none`. The hook is the controller's one session emit (`#crewWatch`), with no poller: news waits for the digest window (T-546: 5 minutes from the first news, the setting `chat.crew.reportMin`, Settings › Running agents, 0 to 60 minutes, 0 sending each report after 1 s), so however many workers report in it the lead gets ONE message and one turn (it says the state when it goes, and nothing when the worker is busy again); a FAIL, BLOCKED or REFUSED as the first word of a worker's reply, a worker waiting on a person, a process gone and an ending for integrity or credentials skip the window (after 1 s to settle) and carry whatever else is due; reports count toward nothing the worker sends. `session_family` items carry `agent` and `mode`, and its summary says how many wait on a person. Admin tools for the tree: `session_list` takes `parent_id`, and each brief carries `parent_id`, `agent` and `claude`; `session_get` carries `parent_id` and a compact `workers` list (id, title, state, Claude, waiting; at most 20, live ones first) with `workers_total`. Neither reaches anything it did not before: a session's own token is still refused both. Tested against the test kit's fake process (`chat/crewMcp.test.ts`, `portalmcp/tools.test.ts`); not yet run against a real multi-Claude workspace. Reports go per lead: at most one digest per window (T-546; it was 30 s), listing every worker with news, except the urgent news above; every quoted part (reply head, title, Claude, what it waits on, why it ended) is cleaned and stripped of quotes and brackets, so it cannot close its quote or open a frame. A worker whose process is gone is reported as stopped (with `report` idle and end), and one that never ran is reported; after a portal restart, each live worker's state counts as already reported. A line the lead can't take stays due (retried after 30 s and 60 s, then at the next change), and a report never relaunches a lead whose process is gone or runs one a person stopped (the app's Stop or the admin tool `session_interrupt`) until someone else's message runs it; news is dropped only when the lead has ended. **Crew wake** (T-1613, D-65): on the liveness round, a top-level lead with live workers that has been idle `chat.crew.wakeMin` minutes (Settings › Running agents, default 15, 0 off) gets ONE portal message (`portal:crew`, labelled Crew wake, priority `later`) when a worker of it is idle with nothing waiting on a person or a verdict was recorded since its last turn on an open ticket it holds: the idle workers by title and for how long, the tickets with their verdicts, and "Close what is done, dispatch idle lanes."; at most once per lead in that window and never again for the same idle workers with no newer verdict (the window is the cost control: one lead turn at most), and never a lead that waits on a person, is busy, waits for a slot under the cap, was stopped by a person, or whose process is gone, interrupted or ended (`chat/crewWake.test.ts`). T-1616: the wake also lists, under "Ready to close or merge", the lead's held tickets that are in review and whose latest verdict (any role) is a pass (at most 15 lines, with the total when there are more; `tickets.readyToCloseHeldBy`), and adds "Close or merge these before new dispatch."; that list alone is news when it is non-empty and changed since the last wake, under the same window. **Limits and roles in the app, observed** (2026-10-14, on 94c1460 plus this change, a scratch portal at 390 px with touch and the iPhone's safe areas and at 1280 px, light and dark): `tools/qa/session-limits.mjs` 96 passed, 0 failing, three runs alike (two taps on − make one PATCH and the server reads 3; + up to the cap reads Default and null; Enter steps it; a pick is saved when the sheet closes at once; None is the bottom; Sub-agents off saved, its line and Restart, which opens the Restart sheet; the role on the header, in Session info, the list row and the worker list; no sideways scroll; 44 px targets); `tools/qa/scroll-lock.mjs` 28/28 and `tools/qa/mobile-drag.mjs` 11/11 on the same portal. Not built: cost and budget rollup to the parent as a cap, a worker's request surfacing in its parent's dock (the lead is told in one line, Crew over MCP, but answers nothing from its dock). T-294 item by item, built and not built: [Crew (T-294): what is built](#crew-t-294-what-is-built). |
| FR79  | **Lead rules** (T-561, D-63: a crew lead dispatches, it does not edit). A policy setting, `sessions.leadRules` (Settings › Running agents › Lead rules, Off by default, protective: turning it off asks first; a person sets it through the settings route, `platform` `manage`; no agent tool sets settings). When it is on, a **lead** gets the rules at each start through the one launch (`#hostStart`: new, resume, wake, restart, recover, a Move): a lead is a session with at least one worker (`parent_id` pointing at it) or one created with `lead: true` (`CreateChatSessionRequest.lead`, `chat_sessions.lead`, migration 778; the supervisor sets it, and a continuation of a lead is a lead). The rules are ONE inline `--settings` value, never two flags: the project's trusted permission rules (read by the host's `cp-session psettings`, permission lists only) merged with the lead rules (allow, deny and ask lists joined; hooks joined per event), holding one PreToolUse hook on `Edit rules (an older script, an unreadable file) the lead gets the lead rules alone for that start. The hook allows a path inside the session folder's `docs/dev-sessions/status/`, `docs/dev-sessions/notes/`or`/tmp/`(resolved with`realpath`of the nearest existing parent, so`..`, a symlink out of an allowed folder and a look-alike prefix are refused; a link whose target does not exist yet is read and judged by where it points (up to 8 levels, a longer chain is refused); an allowed folder that is itself a link leading elsewhere is dropped, so nothing under it is allowed; another worktree is refused) and otherwise exits 2, which Claude Code applies in every permission mode, Bypass included; its stderr, fed back to Claude, is the refusal: it names D-63 and tells the lead to dispatch a worker (`session_spawn`, or `session_message`to a live one). This document does not decide it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

**What is left, in rank order** (size S under a day, M a few days, L a week or more):

1. **Orphans to the queue, and the T-270 sweep over children** (M): when a lead ends and leaves workers running, or a worker ends holding a ticket, the ticket goes back to the queue (or to the lead's successor), and the ended-holder rule follows the parent as well as `continued_from`. Without it a crew's tickets can stay held by an ended session.
2. **A running worker is never moved** (S): the auto-transfer leaves a session with a parent alone (a move kills its sub-agents and re-writes its context, the spike T-294 is about); a test with a worker on a Claude over its threshold.
3. ~~**Admin `session_start` with a parent**~~ (S): built by T-336 (see the table).
4. **Choose by projection and cache affinity** (M): the 60 percent rule, the account that last ran the repo first, the context cap; unit tests with a fake usage summary. `auto` today picks the fewest open sessions.
5. **Reuse an idle child** (M, after 4): an idle worker of the same role and repo on a Claude under 60 percent with context under the cap gets the next slice by message instead of a new start.
6. **Persona from a pool on spawn** (M): `session_spawn` and `session_start` take a Persona pool entry (brief, model, effort); today only the role (an agent file) exists.
7. ~~**`crew_post` and `crew_read`, a stored crew channel**~~: built (see the table; orchestrator decision on T-294, comment 10595). What is left of it: delivery with origin `{kind: crew}` and its integrity rule with a negative twin, audit rows, priority, and the channel as one thread in the app (M).
8. **Bypass by lease** (M once decided): the owner's decision first; with the Bypass gates unchanged.

## 9. Data model

In the portal database ([Pools foundation](foundation-pools.md) §4); redacted (§13), no secrets.

| Table                  | Key fields                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `chat_sessions`        | id (the stable portal session ID; Claude Code's own session ID is `claude_session_id`, which changes when the chat moves to another Claude), workspace_id, claude_id, claude_session_id, cost_base_usd, segment_seq, ticket_id, parent_id, started_by, worktree_path, worktree_branch, worktree_repo, cwd, title, origin (`ui` \| `mcp` \| `persona_test`), state, state_since, process (`live` \| `asleep` \| `gone`), parent_pid, process_started_at, segment, byte_offset, last_seq, bypass_capable, applied (json: model, effort, ultracode, mode), launch (json: flags, `initialize` declarations, policy and account revisions, persona revision in effect, CLI version), usage_baseline (json: last cumulative totals per model), cost_est_usd, budget_usd, turns, created_at, last_activity_at, ended_reason, archived_at |
| `chat_segments`        | session_id, n, claude_id, claude_session_id, cwd, started_at, ended_at, ended_reason (`switched` \| `closed` \| …), cost_usd, turns: one row per run of the chat on a Claude                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `chat_session_aliases` | alias_id, session_id: the id of a session folded into another, so old links resolve                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `chat_events`          | session_id, seq, at, type, subtype, uuid (unique per session), parent_tool_use_id, task_id, request_id, payload (json: allowlisted fields only; base64 moved to blobs; over 256 KB to the blob store with a preview and `truncated: true`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `chat_inputs`          | Every line the controller sends: uuid or request_id, session_id, actor (device or MCP token), kind (`message` \| `control`), priority, state (`sending`, `queued`, `started`, `completed`, `cancelled`, `discarded`, `refused`, `failed`), attachments (blob refs), created_at                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `chat_requests`        | session_id, request_id, tool_use_id, kind (`tool`, `question`, `plan`, `workflow`, `elicitation`, `handoff`), tool_name, agent_label, title, detail (200 characters), full and full_cut (the whole text asked about, up to 8,000 characters, FR25a), created_at, answered_at, decision (json), outcome (`allowed_once`, `allowed_session`, `denied`, `withdrawn`, `expired`, `stale`), actor, proc (the process that raised it, T-284)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `chat_tasks`           | session_id, task_id, parent_task_id, tool_use_id, type, label, background, status, tokens, tool_uses, last_tool, started_at, ended_at                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `chat_files`           | session_id, kind (`upload` \| `tool_image` \| `download`), blob_sha256, name, workspace_path (where an upload was staged or an artifact was made: shown and audited, never read back), size, media_type (the sniffed one), width, height, event_seq (the transcript event it belongs to), meta (json: preview, reach, source, redacted), at                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `chat_uploads`         | id, session_id, blob_sha256, name, media_type, size, meta, created_at: a file uploaded and waiting for its message (dropped after an hour)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `chat_folder_trust`    | workspace_id, folder, scan_hash, scan (json, names only), trusted_at, actor                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `chat_worktrees`       | workspace_id, path, repo, branch, base, session_id, state (`reserved` \| `active` \| `kept` \| `removed`), created_at, removed_at. The portal's record of the worktrees it made (FR50); the chat's own folder and branch are `chat_sessions.worktree_*`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `chat_usage`           | at, at_ms, session_id, workspace_id, claude_id, model, input, output, cache_read and cache_write tokens, cost_usd, requests, agents, duration_ms: one row per model per finished request (FR49), kept when the session is deleted                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

- Token deltas reach browsers live (§13) and stay in memory for the open block only; the store keeps
  whole, redacted blocks. `chat_inputs` is also the record §10.4 checks the stream against.
- When a process exits (`exit.json`) or vanishes (Interrupted), the controller marks inputs that
  never reached a final state `discarded`, since it knows what it sent; the CLI's own `discarded`
  and `refused` states are kept as reported.
- `total_cost_usd` and `modelUsage` are cumulative per session ✅ (S2) and carry on after a resume ✅
  (S7c); `result.usage` covers the main thread only ✅ (S9). For each result the controller diffs
  per-model tokens and cost against `usage_baseline` (after a resume, against the restored totals; a
  decrease counts as a reset) and hands only deltas to Monitoring's `usage_minutes` (source `chat`)
  and Settings' session total.
- A running turn's cost (T-343): `total_cost_usd` arrives only with the turn's `result`, and a crew
  worker may do its whole brief in one turn. Until then the controller adds up the turn's API calls
  from the stream (each `assistant` line's `message.usage`, a sub-agent's too, the main call's final
  output from `message_delta`; one call counted once, at its largest counts) times the model's list
  price from the lab's one price table (`lab/prices.ts`). It lives in memory only and is shown as
  `turnCostEstimateUsd` on the session (on top of `costUsd`; null when a call's model has no known
  price: "unavailable", never a guess), `cost_usd` plus `cost_basis: estimate | unavailable` in
  `session_get`, `session_list` and `session_family`, and "≈ $x" on the session row. The `result`
  replaces it; a turn cut off without one keeps the stored total.
- The persona revision in effect is the first launch's until a `compact_boundary`, because a resume
  keeps the recorded system prompt ([Persona](persona-pool.md) §10.3).
- Files live in `<dataDir>/chat-blobs/<sha256>` (0700, files 0600), served only to a chat that links them. A chat may keep 500 MB and the portal 10 GB (`PORTAL_CHAT_SESSION_QUOTA_MB`, `PORTAL_CHAT_BLOB_QUOTA_MB`); they are kept 90 days after the chat's last activity (FR42), unlinked ones are swept after a day, and a deleted chat takes its files and its staged folder in the workspace with it. Backups carry the database (which files a chat had, their names and sizes), not the blob store's bytes.

## 10. Session host and protocol details

### 10.1 Files and commands

| Path in the workspace             | Content                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `/etc/claudepilot/bin/cp-session` | The host: POSIX `sh` with `flock`, `setsid`, `timeout`, `head` and `jq`, all in today's image (S8, Monitoring A6). In the foundation bundle, so existing workspaces get updates.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `~/.claudepilot/sessions/<id>/`   | `meta.json` (flags without secrets, `initialize` declarations, revisions, CLI version per process), `in` (the FIFO, recreated at start), `in.d/` (staged sends), `out.<n>.jsonl` (stdout, one segment per process), `err.log` (1 MB cap), `exit.json` (code, signal, time), `run.lock`. On the home volume, so the portal can finish reading after a restart, and inside Settings' "Protect portal files" rules (A6). |
| `~/.claudepilot/uploads/<id>/`    | Attachments (FR31)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

| Command                                         | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ----------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `start <id> <n> -- <flags>`                     | Recreates the FIFO, clears `in.d/`, and `setsid`s a parent shell that runs `flock -n -o run.lock claude …` with stdin `<>` the FIFO, stdout `>>` segment `n`, stderr to `err.log` and §10.2's environment. With `-o`, children never inherit the lock, so a dev server the agent left running can't hold it. When `claude` exits, the shell writes `exit.json`. Prints the parent's PID and start time.                                                            |
| `send <id> <uuid> <bytes>`                      | Reads exactly that many bytes (`head -c`, so Kubernetes needs no stdin half-close) into `in.d/<uuid>.jsonl`, checks it's one JSON line ending in a newline, then a detached copier writes it into the FIFO under `send.lock` and deletes it. A dropped exec leaves a whole line or nothing. The controller writes the `chat_inputs` row first and, before a retry, looks for the UUID in `command_lifecycle` or the replay. Unstaged sends took 37–270 ms ✅ (S8). |
| `run <id> -- <flags>`                           | One-shot runs (persona tests): stdin is a regular file holding the prompt line, so EOF arrives and Claude Code drains and exits (✅ on a plain pipe, S7a). A FIFO opened `<>` never sees EOF.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `tail <id> <n> <offset>`                        | Replays segment `n` from a byte offset and follows it for at most 10 minutes (`timeout 600`); exits when its own stdin closes, so a dropped client leaves nothing behind ✅ (S8). An offset past the segment's end is refused (exit 6, the size on stdout) rather than followed (GNU tail would replay the file from byte 0 at the next write; T-265); tail's stderr goes to `tail.err` in the session folder.                                                     |
| `ls`, `cat`, `diff`                             | Files and Diff (§13): open, then check the opened descriptor; git with command-bearing config neutralized.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `put`, `unstage`, `produced`                    | Chat content (FR31, FR32): `put <id> <name> <nchars> <nbytes>` writes an attachment (base64 on stdin, exactly the announced size, a plain ASCII name, never through a link) to `~/.claudepilot/uploads/<id>/`; `unstage` deletes a chat's folder; `produced <dir> <since> <max>` lists the files a folder gained since an instant (no `.git`, `node_modules` or caches, one file system, the same path policy as `ls`).                                            |
| `trustscan <folder>`, `authcheck`               | FR43's names and hash; `pass` or `fail` for §6 item 1, nothing else.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `list`, `status`, `stop [--all]`, `purge`, `gc` | Liveness is the parent shell alive, or a process whose `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` holds this session ID and that started after the container, never a bare PID; `end_session`, then SIGTERM after 10 s; delete segments, uploads and staged inputs; delete closed, ingested segments.                                                                                                                                                                                   |

### 10.2 Launch flags, environment and first lines

```
claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose \
  --include-partial-messages --forward-subagent-text --replay-user-messages \
  --permission-prompt-tool stdio --permission-mode <start mode> --name <title> \
  --session-id <uuid> | --resume <uuid> \
  [--max-budget-usd <per Q8>] [--max-turns <cap>] [--model <m>] [--effort <level>] \
  [--append-system-prompt-file /etc/claudepilot/persona/chat-prompt.md] \
  [--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions]                (only after FR20's opt-in) \
  --disallowedTools CronCreate CronDelete CronList ScheduleWakeup RemoteTrigger
                                                        (always, T-1863; plus Agent Task Workflow with sub-agents off)
```

Added at launch (resume, wake and move included): `--mcp-config` naming the portal's connector for the session's own agent token (I8; the connector refers to `${PILOT_MCP_URL}` and `${PILOT_MCP_TOKEN}` and holds no secret), with the token and URL in that process's environment, passed to `cp-session start --env <n>` as `NAME=value` lines on the exec's stdin, never on a command line or in a file (the host accepts a fixed list of names); `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false` while the account-MCP switch is on; and `--append-system-prompt-file /etc/claudepilot/persona/chat-prompt.md` when a persona is delivered (never `--system-prompt` or `--bare`). Checked on 2.1.296: `${…}` expands in the connector's url and header, the token is not blanked, and the appended file lands at the end of the system prompt.

| Item          | Rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Environment   | `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1` and `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1`, the lab's environment, so the CLI can't change under a session and fetched feature flags can't make production differ from the contract tests (the suite also runs once with flags fetched and records differences). `CLAUDEPILOT_CHAT=<id>`, plus `CLAUDEPILOT_CHAT_WAKE=1` on wakes, so hooks fold a wake into its session. Never a secret.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| First lines   | At every launch, resume and wake, before any user line: `initialize` declaring `perTaskStopAffordance: true` and never `rapidFollowupPreempt`; `apply_flag_settings {crossSessionInbound: "refuse"}` if the CLI accepts it 🔬 (Q8); on a wake, `apply_flag_settings {ultracode}`; then `set_permission_mode <start mode>`, whose reply must match or the session ends. That also covers a managed `defaultMode: bypassPermissions` (Settings' Autonomous preset) that might outrank `--permission-mode` 🔬 (Q5).                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Budget flag   | A C2 gate on Q8: about 90% of the remaining budget if restored totals don't count, or of the whole session budget if they do, leaving room for one response plus subagents.
