Never passed without a price.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Model, effort | Passed only when picked; a wake passes the applied values. `--effort` is never `ultracode`, which also forces xhigh.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Never         | `--no-session-persistence`, `--bg`, `--dangerously-skip-permissions`; `--settings`, `--mcp-config`, `--add-dir` (pools replace them, and resume wouldn't restore them); `--bare`, `--system-prompt` (they drop or replace persona parts); `set_max_thinking_tokens`. Never a key or `env` value in a flag or control request: `get_settings` echoes `env` values, API keys included, in plain text ✅ (X2).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Gating        | Before every launch, resume and wake, the workspace's `claude --version` (foundation §9) must be in the capability matrix the contract suite produces per version, so a wake onto a changed CLI is gated again. Below the minimum: "Update Claude Code to use Sessions"; newer than tested: "Claude Code <version> isn't tested with Sessions yet", until CI passes it. The first `system/init` must list `msg_lifecycle_v1`, `interrupt_receipt_v1` and `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1` (all present ✅, S1), or the session ends and the workspace is flagged; the `initialize` reply may list them sooner 🔬. An "Unsupported control request subtype" error (S5) turns that feature off for the session. |

### 10.3 What the controller sends and reads

Status comes from the 2.1.296 schema text the lab extracted: **internal** (marked `@internal`),
**experimental**, **plain** (present, unmarked) or **—** (outside the extract). The flag column
names a capability where one exists; every row is also contract-tested per version (§10.2).

| UI action                              | Line sent                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Status · flag                                       | Verified                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| -------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Send, steer; send when Claude finishes | `user` with `uuid`, `origin`, `priority` (`next`, `later`); states from `command_lifecycle` (internal)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | plain · `msg_lifecycle_v1`                          | ✅ S3                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ✕ on a queued message                  | `cancel_async_message {message_uuid}`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | plain                                               | ✅ X5                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Stop; Stop all; stop one agent         | `interrupt {cancel_queued: true}`; the same plus `stop_task` per live task; `stop_task`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | plain, `stop_task` — · `interrupt_cancel_queued_v1` | ✅ plain interrupt (S4, S8); 🔬 the rest                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Model                                  | `set_model`, after validation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | plain                                               | ✅ S5                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Effort; ultracode                      | `apply_flag_settings`, built only from `effortLevel`, `ultracode` and `crossSessionInbound`; no caller, MCP included, passes raw settings                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | —                                                   | ✅; ultracode off 🔬                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Mode                                   | `set_permission_mode`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | plain                                               | ✅ acceptEdits, plan, dontAsk, the Bypass refusal; 🔬 default, auto, leaving Bypass                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Allow; always allow; deny; deny & send | `control_response` to `can_use_tool`: for a tool, `{behavior: "allow"}` with no `updatedInput` (optional), so the CLI runs the input it holds, never the redacted copy; "always allow" adds `updatedPermissions` the portal builds (`addRules` only, `destination: "session"`, checked against Settings); `{behavior: "deny", message}`, with `interrupt: true` for Stop. `decisionClassification` says once, always or reject. `addDirectories` is never sent; a `setMode` suggestion becomes an FR17 change. | plain                                               | ✅ allow, deny (S5b); 🔬 the rest Claude Code takes a request back with `control_cancel_request {request_id}` (observed on an interrupt with an approval open): the request resolves as `withdrawn`, and the waiting count and state follow. A Read of a file the portal itself staged for an attachment (§FR31) is answered by the portal, on the record (actor `portal:attachment`). |
| Answer a question or plan              | `control_response` to that `can_use_tool` request, carrying only the answer the portal builds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | plain                                               | 🔬 shape (Q8)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Header, pickers, context, MCP sheet    | `get_settings`, `list_models`, `get_context_usage` (`summary` for the ring; `full`, which calls the token-count API, only when categories open), `mcp_status`, `mcp_toggle`; never `get_status`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | plain; `get_settings`, `mcp_toggle` —               | ✅ S5, X4                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Subscription check after each result   | `get_usage {skip_behaviors: true}` (without it, the CLI reads every transcript of the last seven days)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | experimental                                        | ✅ null rate limits, custom host                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Rename; end                            | `rename_session`; `end_session`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | plain; —                                            | 🔬; ✅ S8                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

Events read: `system/init` each turn (model, mode, `apiKeySource`, tools, skills, MCP servers,
capabilities, version, cwd) ✅, `command_lifecycle` (internal) ✅, messages with
`parent_tool_use_id` ✅, `stream_event` deltas ✅ (S3), `task_*` and `background_tasks_changed` ✅,
`api_retry` for 429, 529 and failing 401s ✅ (none when the helper fixes a 401 ✅),
`permission_denied` ✅ and `result` ✅; and, when present, `agents_killed` (internal; a system row),
peer-message frames, `session_state_changed`, `compact_boundary` and `tool_progress` 🔬. Unknown CLI
requests get an error response.

### 10.4 Integrity checks

The controller records every UUID and `request_id` it sends and ends the session at once
(`end_session`, then SIGTERM after 10 s), with a High event, when it reads:

- a `control_response` for a `request_id` it never issued, or a second one for one it did whose content differs from the first
  (an identical second copy is ignored and logged, T-136);
- a replayed user line, or a `command_lifecycle` `queued` state, for a UUID it never sent (peer
  messages included, unless their cause is in the stream, T-136; commands the CLI queues itself emit no `queued`);
- a `tool_result` or `permission_denied` for a tool whose request is still unanswered, other than
  after the portal's own Stop or `stop_task`;
- a turn with nothing behind it: no input from the controller, no finished background task and no
  scheduled task Settings allowed;
- a permission mode in a main-thread `system/init` or `system/status` other than the expected one, unless it is less permissive (case
  `mode-downgrade` below, T-277) or a plan transition the portal allowed; Bypass in a `set_permission_mode` reply other than the answer
  to the one request the portal sent under the Bypass grant (a reply to the portal's own `set_permission_mode` otherwise confirms the
  mode it carries; the downgrade rule is not about replies); or a `session_id` that changes.

Contract tests inject each kind of line through the FIFO and from a stdio MCP server and expect the
session to end.

One exception, precise (T-99, 2026-10-13): Claude Code starts a **turn of its own** when a sub-agent or a background task finishes, and
that turn's command appears as a `command_lifecycle` `started` for a UUID the portal never sent, with no `queued` before it and no
replayed user line (then `system/init` and the turn). The controller accepts it, and the rest of that command's lifecycle, only when a
task ran in the session's current process (`chat_tasks` since the process start), and logs it as a harness turn; the same line in a
process without any task, or a `queued` state for an unknown UUID, still ends the session. Observed on the Konfersi session at 12:50
(a role sub-agent finished; the session ended "queued a message this portal didn't send"); the raw lines are a test fixture.

**Accepted cases, one table (T-136, 2026-10-13).** Every line the controller believes without having sent it is an entry of
`INTEGRITY_CASES` (`apps/server/src/chat/integrity.ts`): the line, the rule, the evidence it rests on, and its negative twin, which still
ends the session; `integrity.test.ts` holds a positive fixture and the negative twins for each, built at runtime. Two were added after
three integrity endings in one day:

- **A sub-agent's message to the main conversation** (origin `peer`). Observed on the orchestrator fdb140be at 15:45 (raw `out.1.jsonl`,
  Claude Code 2.1.296): an assistant line on the sub-agent's thread (`parent_tool_use_id` its Agent call, `agent_id` its task id) with
  a `SendMessage` tool use `{to: "main", message}`; then that call's tool result, one text block `{"success":true,"message":"Message
queued for the main conversation's next turn."}`; then `system/init` (a new turn); then a replayed, synthetic user line with
  `origin: {kind: "peer", from, senderTaskId, name, body}` whose `body` is exactly the message. No `command_lifecycle` for that line was
  seen. The controller accepts the peer line only when all of it is there: the SendMessage of a sub-agent (never the main thread's), its
  own result saying exactly that (the phrase inside any other text, a ticket quoting it, does not count), the same task id, a task that
  ran in the current process (`chat_tasks`), the same text (compared as a sha256 digest; the text is never kept), within 2 minutes; each
  cause is used once. Otherwise (no cause, another body, another task, too late, a second copy) it still ends the session. A later
  lifecycle of that line's command, should one come, is the harness's, like a T-99 turn. Sub-agents are still told not to call
  SendMessage (dev-sessions README rule 7) until this is deployed.
- **A second, identical control response.** The orchestrator fe3df8bc ended "a second control response for request …"; no request id
  appears twice in any raw `out.*.jsonl` on the dev workspace, so the second copy was the same line read again by the portal, not a
  second answer from Claude Code. The first response's digest (sha256 of the parsed reply) is kept on its control line (migration 771);
  an identical second copy is ignored and logged, never processed again; a different one, or one for an id the portal never asked or
  answered, still ends the session. One whose first copy kept no digest (completed before migration 771) is ignored only when it is
  **the same answer by shape** (`sameAnswer`, case `control-replay-undigested`, T-265): a success whose body is one the portal's own
  requests produce (empty for `set_model`, `interrupt`, `apply_flag_settings`, `stop_task`, `cancel_async_message`; `{mode}` for a mode
  the portal set or expects on the session; only the keys `get_settings`, `list_models` or `get_usage` reply with), or, for the echo of
  an answer the portal gave, the `behavior` it recorded (allow, deny). A different mode, an error, another shape, another behavior or an
  id never asked still ends the session.

**An Agent-tool sub-agent's hand-back (T-3025, observed 2026-10-29).** Claude Code 2.1.296 delivers the final report of an Agent-tool
sub-agent, foreground or background, as a peer line of its own, with **no** SendMessage before it: `origin: {kind: "peer", from,
senderTaskId, name, body: "[Subagent hand-back] …", handback: true}`. The case `replay-peer` above never matched it, so every Agent-tool
hand-back ended the lead ("origin peer"; the Konfersi Business lead three times on 2026-10-29). Seen in that lead's own transcript: a
foreground Agent's main-thread tool result `{tool_use_result: {status: "completed", agentId, handback: "send"}}`, the hand-back read in
the same moment (absorbed mid-turn); a background Agent's launch result `{tool_use_result: {isAsync: true, status: "async_launched",
agentId}}`, the hand-back 14 minutes later. Case `replay-peer-handback`: the line is believed only when `handback` is the boolean
`true`, `from` equals `senderTaskId`, and this process showed that agent id under one of its own main-thread Agent (or Task) calls
stored in the transcript: in that call's tool result (`tool_use_result.agentId`, main thread only) or on a line of the sub-agent itself
(`agent_id` under the call). No time window (a background sub-agent runs for hours). Each sighting allows one hand-back; a sub-agent
seen again afterwards (continued) may hand back again. The causes are kept with the process's control state (`handbacks`, ids and time
only), so a portal restart keeps them and a new process drops them. The transcript gets a system row, "Sub-agent "<description>" handed
back its report" (the report itself is the Agent call's result, or the background task's output). Still ending the session: a hand-back
from an agent id this process never showed (another process's task, `replay-peer-unknown`), an id seen only on a call that is no
Agent/Task call or on a sub-agent's own nested Agent call, `from` naming another agent, a second copy, and a peer line without `handback:
true` (that one needs its SendMessage, `replay-peer`). Not covered (not observed): a hand-back that arrives without any line of its
sub-agent or its result in this process's stream.

**The report of a task the portal stopped (T-318, observed 2026-10-14).** When the host stops a background command (`stop_task`),
Claude Code 2.1.296 queues its own report of it, passive (it starts no turn), and delivers it with the NEXT turn, after that turn's
`system/init`: a replayed user line with `origin: {kind: "task-notification", runId}` and **no** `producer` (the CLI enqueues it
without the attribution that sets `session-task`), no `command_lifecycle` of its own, and exactly this text: `<task-notification>`,
`<task-id>`, an optional `<tool-use-id>`, `<status>killed</status>`, `<summary>Task "<description>" was stopped by the
user</summary>` (`TASK_STOPPED_LINE`; Claude Code escapes the description). The T-149 harness ended a session on it ("origin
task-notification"); it was reproduced with the real CLI against the stand-in API (`tools/qa/mock-anthropic.mjs`, a background `sleep`,
then `stop_task`, then a message). Case `replay-task-stopped`: the line is believed only when this portal sent `stop_task` for that very
task id to this session's current process within `TASK_STOP_MS` (12 h: the report waits for the next message) and Claude Code did not
refuse it (a stop it did not answer in time is kept: it may have stopped the task); each stop is used once and is kept with the process's control state (so a portal restart in between keeps it, a new process
drops it). The same line with no stop, a stop for another task, in another session, too old, from an earlier process or already used, a
`producer` key on it of any value (also one that is not a plain token), and any other text (another status, words added) still end the session. A stop by another agent (status `stopped`, "was
stopped by <agent>") and an MCP task's stop note are not accepted: not observed.

**A mode less permissive than expected (T-277, observed 2026-10-14).** The Konfersi chat 3907db7f ended at 02:50:24 UTC, during deploy
#20 (02:43 to 02:53), with "the permission mode changed to default without this portal (expected bypassPermissions)": after the portal
restarted, the expected mode was rebuilt from the stored row (Bypass), and a main-thread `system/init` or `system/status` reported
`default`. The rule (case `mode-downgrade`): the modes are ordered `plan < default < acceptEdits < auto < bypassPermissions`
(`MODE_RANK`), and a main-thread report strictly lower than the expected one, both in that table, is taken: the portal expects that mode
from then on, the row shows it, the transcript says "Bypass is off in this chat" (audited `bypass.left`, why `downgrade`) or "Mode is
<mode> in this chat", and the session event goes out; the portal does not re-enter Bypass there. **How Bypass comes back** after a
downgrade: only through `enterBypass` (`POST /api/chat/sessions/:id/bypass`), with every gate: the policy, a bypass-capable session, the
acknowledgement and a fresh server code; the supervisor does exactly that as the owner's delegate (T-256, at most every
`BYPASS_GAP_MIN` minutes). A Restart, a Move or a recover in place does NOT bring it back: their re-assert (`#keepBypass`) runs only
for a row that is in Bypass when they start, and after a downgrade the row is not. Keep going (T-259) reports a chat out of Bypass and
never re-enters it (the lease is its slice 2). The negative twins still end the session with the same
text: a report more permissive than expected (also back up after a downgrade), Bypass the portal did not enter, an unknown mode string
(`dontAsk` included, ranked above Bypass; an expected mode outside the table is never downgraded from), and a sub-agent thread's mode,
which is never a mode report (the expected mode stays and the next main-thread report is judged against it). Observed in
`integrity.test.ts` and `restart.test.ts` (a portal restart under a Bypass chat on the controller testkit), and in a scratch portal
restarted under the real Claude Code CLI (`tools/qa/restart-survive.mjs`, the proof below); not yet seen on the cluster.

**The control state, persisted (T-277 layer 2, 2026-10-14).** After a portal restart the portal no longer infers what the stream must
report from the row. What the integrity checks decide by is stored on the session (`chat_sessions.control_state`, migration 820, JSON
`StoredControl` in `chat/integrity.ts`) for the process it belongs to (Claude Code session id, segment and start): the expected mode, the
mode before plan, plan entered (EnterPlanMode seen), leaving plan approved (ExitPlanMode allowed), the id of a Bypass request sent under
the grant whose reply was not read yet, the sub-agent SendMessage causes a peer line may follow (tool use id, task id, sha256 digest,
time, queued; never text), and the `stop_task` requests whose report has not arrived (task id, time; `stops`, only when there are any,
T-318), and the Agent-tool sub-agents whose hand-back may follow (tool use id, agent id, time; `handbacks`, only when there are any,
T-3025). It is written with every change: a line's change commits in the same transaction as the line's byte offset,
so a restart resumes from exactly the state that line left; an answer (ExitPlanMode), a launch, a move and the gated Bypass request
write at once; it is cleared when the process is over. Control lines and the digest of their first reply were already stored
(`chat_inputs`, migration 771). When the portal attaches to a live process it holds nothing for, it reloads that state exactly; a state
stored for another process, or none (every row before migration 820, so the deploy that ships it), is never applied, and the row's
mode is expected as before. **Resync** (case `mode-resync`): the first main-thread mode report after that attach is compared once with
the stored state, within `RESYNC_MS` (60 s), and logged (`[chat] <id>: resync: expected …, reported …`); it is judged by the same rules
(equal, a plan transition the stored flags allow, or a downgrade), so it accepts nothing more permissive. **An unfinished Bypass entry**
(case `control-bypass-unfinished`): a portal that stopped between sending the gated `set_permission_mode` and reading its reply used to
end the session on that reply ("Claude Code switched to Bypass permissions"). Now, only for the stored request id and only after a
reload, the reply is the portal's own, and the entry is NOT finished: never shown or audited as entered; the process is taken out of
Bypass at once (`set_permission_mode` to the start mode; if refused or unanswered, the process is stopped; if that fails too, the session
ends), the transcript says "Bypass is off in this chat: the portal restarted while turning it on." and `bypass.left` (why
`restart_unfinished`) is recorded; the same when the stored state expects Bypass but the row never recorded the entry. A Bypass reply to
any other id, the same late reply in the same portal life, and a Bypass report with nothing pending still end the session. **A lower
mode during a re-assert** (review note c): when a status line saying a lower mode is read after the switch's reply and before `#keepBypass`
resumes, the portal never writes "Bypass kept" over it: the process is taken out of Bypass the same way and the transcript says "Bypass
not kept after the <restart|move|recovery>: Claude Code reported another mode during the switch." (`bypass.left`, why `downgrade`).
`MODE_RANK` is frozen. Observed in `integrity.test.ts` and `restart.test.ts` (a portal restart on the controller testkit) and in
`tools/qa/restart-survive.mjs` (the reload and the resync line of a real restart, the proof below); not yet seen on the cluster.

**Why a line is read twice (T-265, observed 2026-10-13).** The orchestrator 5c9fcb5f ended at 17:58:46 on the FIRST line of its stream
(the `set_permission_mode` reply of 15:46, which appears once in the 12 MB file), 20 minutes after the portal restarted at 17:38:44 for a
deploy. The reader follows a segment with `tail -c +<offset> -f` under `timeout 600`, renewed every 10 minutes from the stored byte
offset. Two things met. (1) GNU tail (coreutils 9.1 in the workspace image), started with an offset past the file's end, seeks there
anyway; at the next write it sees a size smaller than its position, prints `file truncated` on stderr, seeks to 0 and re-emits the
whole file: line 1 again. The stderr was merged into the stream (Kubernetes exec), dropped as non-JSON and even counted into the
offset. (2) The offset got past the end because two `tail` execs fed one reader: `#ensureReader` (a liveness round, or a tab's
`attach`) opened a follower while `#checkAndRetail` was still awaiting the dropped one's status, and the re-follow then opened
another; every byte counted twice. After a restart the liveness round (60 s) and the renewal (600 s) are phase-locked, so it hit at
the second renewal (17:48:45 two followers; 17:58:45 the next one started past the end; 17:58:46 Claude Code wrote, tail replayed).
Fixed: one follower per reader (the status check holds the reader; a replaced follower's exit is ignored; a new follower closes the
one it replaces); `cp-session tail` refuses an offset past the segment's end (exit 6 with the size on stdout; the controller moves the
cursor to the size and logs both numbers) and sends tail's stderr to `tail.err` in the session folder, never into the byte count;
and every integrity ending is logged (`[chat] <id>: ended for integrity: <reason> (line <type/subtype> at byte <start> of segment
<n>)`), never with the line's content. Not proven for want of the database: that two followers ran on 5c9fcb5f between 17:48 and
17:58 (its row's `byte_offset` would be larger than the file, 12,144,016 bytes).

Nothing else was loosened: an invented uuid, an unknown origin, a lifecycle in a process with no task and a message with no cause end the
session as before, and the Bypass checks are unchanged.

**Lineage (T-136 part E, data).** A session that continues an ended one names it in `continued_from` (migration 770; `continuedFrom` on
the wire); root to head the rows are one unit, and the server's lineage read (`ChatStore.lineage`) gives the chain and its cost summed.
Migration 772 repaired the chains split on 2026-10-13 (fe3df8bc → fdb140be → 5c9fcb5f, the orchestrator; 541948a5 → 929ae1e3,
Konfersi): matched by id prefix only when exactly one row matches, all in one workspace, never overwriting another link, otherwise a
no-op; a pin moves to the head, the head takes an earlier row's ticket link when it has none, and tickets held by an ended row of the
chain are assigned to the head with an `assigned` entry (actor `system`) on each timeline. Sessions carry no tags today, so none move.
Recovery in place (part B) is below; not yet: grouping usage and scores by lineage root (slice 3).

**Paged list (D-58, T-382 S2).** `GET /api/chat/sessions?limit=&cursor=&parentId=` answers one page, `{ items, next }` ([README](README.md), List API rule):
newest activity first, the session id breaking ties, 25 by default and at most 100. Each row is checked with `view` (`in:list(session)`) before it
is shown and `next` points at the last row shown, so a cursor never names a session the caller may not view; a bad, forged or foreign cursor
(another `parentId`) is a 400. The web loads the newest 50, has an Older button, and its 5 s refresh asks for that first page only (older pages
already loaded stay until a reload). Callers that need the whole set (the palette, the ticket's assignee picker, a lead's workers) follow `next`
up to five pages of 100 (`chatApi.listAll`). The in-process callers (`ChatController.list`, the portal MCP tools, tickets, repos, CI) never use the
route and are unchanged. Scripts under `tools/` read `data.items` of `?limit=100`.

**Paged audit trail (D-58, T-382 S4).** `GET /api/chat/audit?kind=&sessionId=&limit=&cursor=` answers one page, `{ items, next }`, newest first
by entry id (so an entry written during a walk never repeats or skips a row), 25 by default and at most 100 (it was 100 by default and 500 at most).
The cursor is signed and bound to the caller, `kind` and `sessionId`: a bad one, or one used with another filter or by another caller, is a 400.
An entry about a session is shown only when the caller may `view` that session; an entry with no session, or of a session since deleted, shows as
before, and `next` points at the last entry shown. The entries carry the same fields as before (`detail` is small by construction: ids, codes and
the redactor's output; there is no separate detail route). Scripts read `data.items` and follow `next` (`tools/qa/crew-e2e.mjs`,
`tools/coordinator/crew_metrics.py`). The in-process `ChatController.audit` is unchanged.

**Continuation (T-270, 2026-10-14).** `POST /api/chat/sessions` takes `continuedFrom: <session id>`. The session named must exist (404),
be in the same workspace and have ended (409 otherwise: a live or interrupted session keeps its work); this is checked before anything is
made. The new row is written with `continued_from` and, in the same SQLite transaction, every unfinished ticket held by the predecessor
and by its ended predecessors down the chain is assigned to the new session ([Tickets](tickets.md) FR18a); a failure of the move leaves no
row. A `session.continued` audit row names the predecessor and the tickets moved. The supervisor's start-again path sends it
(`createBody` in `docs/dev-sessions/supervisor.mjs`). Observed in `chat/continuation.test.ts` (real controller and tickets service, fake
workspaces); not yet seen on the cluster.

**Recovery in place (T-136 slice 2, D-30, 2026-10-14).** A session ended for integrity comes back as the SAME row: `POST
/api/chat/sessions/:id/recover` (operate on the session; `RecoverChatRequest {confirm?, by?}`), the Portal MCP tool `session_recover`, and
Resume on the ended row and on the session page. What it does, in order: the row goes from ended to Interrupted (no process); the trail
gets one marker, "Recovered in place after an integrity ending: <reason>" (`recovered: true`, shown like a Restart's divider), and a note
that the line that ended it was dropped; the process starts with `--resume <claude session id>` on a NEW log segment, the path a wake
and a Restart use, on the Claude it ran on (when that Claude is out of quota the auto move takes the live chat on from there, as for any
chat); model, effort and mode come back as after any resume; Bypass comes back only through `#keepBypass(id, 'recover')`, the grant a
Restart and a Move use (the policy and the session's capability; otherwise the start mode, and the transcript says "Bypass not kept after
the recovery: <why>"), and no sign-in code is involved; then the portal sends one short continuation (`portal:recover`, the supervisor's
MOVED shape: same conversation, new process, the old sub-agents stopped, carry on). The id, title, pin, ticket link, parent and children,
the URL and the cost (one series: the row's own, the recovery an event in its transcript) are the row's and stay. "Dropped" means the
portal never reads that line again (the old segment is not followed and nothing of it is resent); the line was already part of Claude
Code's own conversation when the portal saw it, and it stays there: that is why a suspicious cause needs a person (below).

What it is refused for (`chat/recover.ts`): a session that hasn't ended (409, a message or Resume picks it up), one a person closed
("closed by operator", 409), any other ending (a credential ending is for safety: a new session); past `chat.recover.maxPerDay` recoveries
in 24 hours (default 4; 409 `RECOVER_CAPPED`: it stays ended, and ONE incident ticket tagged `watchdog` is opened per session per 24 h);
within `chat.recover.pauseMin` of the last one (default 2; 409 `RECOVER_PAUSED` with `retryAfterS`). Both settings are in the registry,
explained in their InfoTip. Every refusal and every recovery is audited (`session.recover.{done,failed,refused,capped}`).

**A suspicious cause.** An integrity ending now records what it was for (`chat_sessions.ended_for`, migration 805): its case
(`ENDING_CASES` in `chat/integrity.ts`), the classification, the line's origin in words (never its text) and whether the session was in
Bypass; the payload carries `endedFor: {reason, classified, case, origin}`. **Harmless** are only the near misses of an accepted case
whose line came from the process's own machinery: a peer line from a sub-agent that ran in this process whose SendMessage cause was not
matched (`replay-peer`), and, for rows ended before this, the pre-T-265 "a second control response for request …" (the same line read
twice, `control-duplicate`). Everything else is **suspicious**: an unknown origin or none, a peer line from no task of the process, a
command the portal never sent, a different or unknown control answer, a tool request id seen twice, another session id, a mode or Bypass
the portal did not set, and any older reason. (The ending text for a different second control response is now "a second, different
control response for request …", so the two never read alike.) An agent (the MCP tool, or a person's sign-in that says `by: "agent"`,
as the supervisor does) is refused a suspicious cause (403 `RECOVER_SUSPICIOUS`, with the classification); a person gets 409
`RECOVER_CONFIRM` until they confirm a sheet that shows the reason, the origin and the classification. `session_recover` asks for a
confirmation every time (preview: the session, its ending and what happens); a person's Resume never needs one for a harmless cause.

**The supervisor** (`docs/dev-sessions/supervisor.mjs`) calls recover first for a track chat ended for integrity, as an agent
(`recoverBody`), and reads the answer with `recoverVerdict`: back in place; wait for the next round (the pause, a portal restarting); or a
NEW session continuing it (`continuedFrom`) when refused (absent, the cap, a suspicious cause, a person's close). Its "too many starts"
cap counts new sessions only. `DRY_RUN=1` makes no call. Observed in `chat/recover.test.ts` (fake workspaces: the row kept, the fresh
segment, the caps and the pause, Bypass kept and not, the refusals, the route, the MCP tool, the supervisor's shapes); not yet seen on the
cluster, and the tokens a recovery costs against a new session's start were not measured (they need a real Claude).

**The proof: a portal restart under live chats (T-136 slice 3, T-277's proof, observed 2026-10-14).** `tools/qa/restart-survive.mjs`
starts a scratch portal (`scripts/scratch-portal.sh`, under the heavy lock while it starts) whose local workspace runs the REAL Claude Code
CLI, answered by the stand-in Messages API (`tools/qa/mock-anthropic.mjs`). Chat A enters Bypass through the gated path (the policy with
its confirmation, a session started to allow it, the server code again, the tick) and runs a sub-agent; chat B, its twin, stays in the
start mode. The portal process is stopped; while it is down, Claude Code is switched through its own input FIFO (A to `default`, LESS
permissive than expected; B to `acceptEdits`, MORE permissive), and `tools/qa/claude-shim.mjs` keeps the reply to that request away from
the portal, so it learns the mode only from Claude Code's own next report. Then `scratch-portal.sh restart` brings the portal back on the
same data and session files, under the same Claude Code processes. It asserts: A survives (no integrity ending, it answers), it is flagged
("Bypass is off in this chat", `bypass.left` with why `downgrade`), the row and the stored control state both expect the reported mode, the
log shows the control state reloaded (expected Bypass) and the resync comparison, and one follower (`tail`) per session file; Bypass comes
back on A only through the gated path; B ends for integrity (`mode-changed`, suspicious) and an agent's recover is refused (403); A, then
ended for a harmless cause (a peer line naming its own sub-agent with no SendMessage behind it, `replay-peer`), is recovered in place by
`POST .../recover` as an agent: the same id, title, pin, creation time and Claude Code session, no new row, and it answers again. As
observed in Job `claudepilot-job-4a4522b8` (commit f89c64d): 29 checks, 0 failed, 122 s. What it does not cover: a cluster deploy (the
deploy's `chats` job, T-277 layer 4, is the live check), a Kubernetes or Docker workspace (the local runtime runs the same host script),
and the follower count is sampled (three looks during one short turn), not watched continuously.

### 10.5 Browser protocol

| Direction    | Messages                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Tab → portal | `subscribe {session, after_seq}`, `unsubscribe`, `watch_list {filters}`; `send {session, uuid, text, attachments, priority}`; `respond {session, request_id, decision or answer, note}`; `control {session, uuid, op, args}` (stop, stop_keep_queued, stop_all, stop_task, model, effort, ultracode, mode, end, resume, rename, trust); `presence {visible, session}` |
| Portal → tab | `events {session, from_seq, items}` (batched, at most 30 a second per session); `session {id, state, summary, applied, counts}`; `ack {uuid, ok, error, applied}`; `attention {count}`                                                                                                                                                                                |

Sessions ride the Web app's live socket (`/api/live`), checked like the shell socket (Origin
against `PORTAL_APP_URL`, the session cookie), with pings every 25 s and 1 MB frames. HTTP serves
older pages, uploads, downloads and blobs under the existing guards.

**History pages (T-376, server slice, as built 2026-10-15).** `GET /api/chat/sessions/:id/events` answers in two forms. With `?after=<seq>`
or no parameter it answers as it always did: the stored events after that seq as a `ChatEvent[]`, whole (bounded since T-2589, below); the
web of this release still calls it this way. With `?before=<seq>` or `?limit=<n>` (either is enough) it answers a `ChatEventPage`: the
newest events before `before` (before the newest event when it is absent or empty), read newest first on the `seq` key (never an offset,
D-58) and returned oldest first, at most `limit` (200 when absent, 1,000 at most) and stopped earlier once the events would pass 512 KiB
of JSON (a page always holds one); `more` says older events exist and `before` is what to ask with next (the first event's seq, null when
there are no more). In a page, a `tool_result` longer than 2,000 characters keeps its first 2,000 (never half a surrogate pair) and
`textLength` says how long the whole is; `GET /api/chat/sessions/:id/events/:seq` returns that one event whole (404 for a seq the session
does not have). `after` together with `before` or `limit`, or a limit or before that is not a whole number from 1, is a 422 (validation failed). Both routes
are declared `view` on the session: another user gets the same 404 as a session that does not exist. Every JSON answer under `/api/*`
is gzip-compressed when the request accepts gzip and is at least 1 KiB (hono/compress, scoped to `application/json`); an event stream,
a WebSocket upgrade, a blob, a download or a range answer never is. Not yet built: the opaque cursor (T-382).

**Bounded transcript reads (T-2589, 2026-10-26).** One chat held 204k events (208 MB of payload); reading it whole took the portal past
its heap (exit 139). No server read takes a whole transcript any more. The `after` form above, the socket's subscribe backlog, the
delegated and VS Code event reads and the Portal MCP `session_get` read at most 2,000 events (`HISTORY_MAX_EVENTS`); past it they get the
NEWEST 2,000, oldest first, and the older ones stay reachable with `before`. A Move reads only the newest rows of the kinds a transfer
uses (messages, tool calls without their input, turn ends; at most 5,000 rows and about 8 M characters); when older ones were left
unread, the context file and the opening say older messages were left out. `session_get` lists pending requests from the request table
(the approvals dock's own source), so a request older than the newest 2,000 events is still listed. A turn the portal waits on (the
handoff, the new Claude's acknowledgement) is read forward a page at a time.

**History pages in the web (T-376, web slice, as built 2026-10-15).** Opening a session reads the newest page only (`?limit=200`; tried at 80 in T-376 S4 and reverted: a shorter page does not fill the screen, so older pages load by themselves during the open and add long tasks) and
shows it at the bottom, rendered in a React transition (the page's rows commit in slices the browser can interrupt; older pages too), with the
entry script split into `react`, `motion` and `router-icons` chunks so no single script is one long task. Measured on the built app with
`tools/qa/chat-open.mjs` (10,000-event chat, 4x CPU throttle): the budget is no long task over 200 ms at the open. **It is not always met**: before S4 the
longest task was 238 to 396 ms (render 312 to 449 ms, entry script 239 ms); after, 137 to 273 ms on a quiet node and 207 to 466 ms on a busy one (Job b966dee9, 3 runs x 4 configs: 1 of 12 under 200 ms; the
older-page budget of 300 ms also failed in 7 of 12 there, 143 to 555 ms; the same code is node-load sensitive). The cost is the commit of the 200-event page at 4x CPU. `content-visibility:auto` on the rows was tried once and removed: the row you read moved 160 to 430 px
when a page landed. The budget is unchanged; a person decides on it. One older page painted within 300 ms of the scroll that triggers its read, and DOM nodes and
JS heap recorded before and after the older pages (about 1,700 to 3,750 nodes, 10 to 16 MB for three pages); the socket subscribes after that page's newest seq (a subscribe without `afterSeq` sends the whole backlog, so it
waits for the page; a page that cannot be read falls back to exactly that). Reconnects resume with `afterSeq` as before, and live events
go at the end while older pages are read. Within a screen of the top (at least 400 px) the transcript reads the page before the first
event it holds, one at a time; while it reads, a fixed-height row at the top says "Loading earlier" (a failed read: "Load earlier" to
retry), and once the first event is held it says "Start of the chat". The reading position holds exactly as a page goes in above (QA A9):
the first row in view and its distance from the top of the view are measured before the commit and put back before paint (the rows carry
`data-anchor`, their last seq, which stays the same when older rows join a turn; `scrollAnchor.ts`, pure and tested). A cut `tool_result`
shows its preview in Output, marked "preview", with "Show all (N chars)", which reads the event whole (`…/events/:seq`) and puts it in
the preview's place; Copy waits for the whole. Rendering stays bounded by the pages held: folded activity rows, runs of calls and closed
tool rows do not mount their contents (not virtualized). An open request older than the pages held would be missing from the dock: while
the session's `waiting` count is more than the open requests held, the page reads up to 5 older pages by itself to find it. Usage says
"Loaded turns only" while older pages are not held (its figures count what is held). The paging state is `history.ts` (pure, tested).
The transcript has no search and no link to one event yet, so neither had to learn pages.

## 11. Per-target notes

|               | DinD                                                                                                                  | Host Docker Desktop                        | Kubernetes (k3s)                                                                                                                         |
| ------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Start         | `docker exec -d` runs `cp-session start` ✅ (S8)                                                                      | Same API 🔬                                | No detached exec: `start` redirects every descriptor under `setsid`, or the exec never returns 🔬                                        |
| Send          | Short `docker exec -i`, length-prefixed (unstaged ✅, S8)                                                             | 🔬                                         | `exec` with stdin; the length prefix avoids stdin half-close, which `@kubernetes/client-node` 2.0.0 sends only on `v5.channel.k8s.io` 🔬 |
| Read          | One `tail` per live session, renewed every 10 minutes (unbounded ✅, S8)                                              | 🔬; the socket proxy may drop long streams | One reader per workspace following all its segments, through the API server, renewed every 10 minutes and after drops 🔬                 |
| Restart       | Writable layer kept; process gone: Interrupted                                                                        | Same                                       | New pod, home PVC kept                                                                                                                   |
| Graceful stop | New images' CMD trap runs `cp-session stop --all` before exiting; existing containers keep a PID 1 that exits at once | Same                                       | `preStop` runs `cp-session stop --all` (up to 20 s); `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` 30, up from 5                                       |

- Everything is portal-initiated; the cluster never calls back. Every `exec` names the workspace
  container (Settings amendment 6 adds a sidecar), and Files and Diff use `cp-session` on every
  target. Sessions need `exec`, so they're unavailable on the foundation's Kubernetes fallback (its
  §6.2). DinD workspaces can reach the portal over the bridge gateway; Sessions never rely on that
  and treat bridges as untrusted.

## 12. User experience

Screens use `@isnanme/ui` and the Web app W1 kit (ImmersiveLayout, PushedScreen, StatusDot,
ConnectionBanner, CodeBlock, DiffView, Meter, and a new PickerSheet, A2): tokens only, no new visual
language. **Sessions** is the first phone tab, with a Needs input badge; workspace rows and pages
get **Chat**. Routes: `/admin/sessions`, `…/new`, `…/:id` (`?request=`) and `…/share`.

### 12.1 Layout by width

| Width                                       | Layout                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Under 640 px, or under 500 px tall on touch | List and session are separate screens; the session is immersive (48 px header, no tab bar). Agents, Files, Diff, Browser and Session info are pushed screens or sheets.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 640–1023 px                                 | Rail; list or session, one at a time; the same pushed screens.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 1024–1279 px                                | Rail; list (320 px) beside the session. The "Agents 3" and "+42 −18" chips open the panel as an overlay drawer that doesn't reflow the transcript.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 1280 px and up                              | Rail, list, transcript (up to 760 px) and a 360 px panel that's always there (480 px above 1680 px). Its Segmented "Agents · Files · Diff", plus "Browser" where one is assigned (a view-only mini view that connects only while visible, with Open full view and Take control, [Browser & desktop](browser-desktop-pool.md) §15.1), starts on Agents, which begins with session info: model, mode, persona, est. cost on a Meter against the budget, context. New background work pulses the Agents chip once and never opens anything. |

The `panes` route is one 100dvh column whose list, transcript and panel scroll on their own, with
the composer docked. The page transition is keyed by `/admin/sessions`, so switching sessions never
remounts the list; rotating or resizing never remounts the composer. ⌘- or Ctrl-click (or
middle-click) on a row opens the session in its own tab, the v1 split view.

