### 12.2 Session list (`/admin/sessions`)

| Part              | Design                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Header            | "Sessions" with **New session**; chips in the order of attention, "Needs you 2 · In progress 1 · Idle · Pinned · Ended · All", each only when it has sessions (Pinned and All always; FR2); one search field (desktop adds a workspace Select). Empty: "No sessions yet" or "Start a workspace first". Loading: RowsSkeleton.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Rows              | Two lines (T-375, FR2): "(o) Fix login tests [crew 3] 2m ⋯" over "● Working · Opus 5.5 · satria ▬▬── 62% · $2.39"; a third only for the waiting request ("Allow: Run npm test?") or, on a phone, the branch and ticket. Desktop rows add the branch, ticket, "2 agents" and "Bypass" on the same two lines. Interrupted rows carry **Resume**; a ⋯ Menu holds Rename, Open shell, Archive and End. Touch screens swipe a row (right pins, left archives; FR2). Rows sit in rounded cards and keep to the card's corners (FR2, T-100). A row's summary updates at most once a second. |
| Share (`…/share`) | The shared thumbnails on top, then New session and the Needs input and Working sessions; picking one opens it with the images in the composer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

**Words on screen (T-1710).** The app calls a session an **agent**: the nav and the page are "Agents", the button is "New agent", and
dialogs, toasts, empty states and tips say agent. Routes (`/admin/sessions`), the API, types and the database keep "session". Where
"session" means something else it stays: Claude Code's own session, the 5-hour session window of a plan, an app port's session, and the
server's setting keys and group id `sessions`. The server's labels follow (T-1825): the Settings group is "Running agents" (with
"Agents per workspace", "Agents in total", "Spend limit per agent", "Workers per agent"), the knowledge graph's group "Agents".

#### 12.2.1 Agents tree (T-1710)

A **Tree** toggle (icon only, next to Group by; remembered with the grouping in `claudepilot.sessions-view`) turns the list into trees:
each lead that started workers (`parentId`), with its workers and theirs, as connected cards (the owner's reference,
`docs/design/agent-tree-reference.png`). The chips, grouping and Select hide in it; the search keeps the trees with an agent that
matches. It reads the list the page already polls (every 5 s), and a clock ticks each second for the running ones' time.

| Part   | Design |
| ------ | ------ |
| Filter (T-1853) | All / Active / Waiting over every tree, kept in this browser (`claudepilot.agent-tree.filter`, Active by default). Active shows the agents running or waiting on a person and the leads above them; Waiting only the waiting ones and their leads. A tree's lead always shows. What is left out folds behind a count under the tree ("+1 idle · 2 ended"), which switches to All. The header counts and the failed list always cover the whole tree. |
| Card   | Kept short (T-1853): a model glyph read from the model id (Claude, OpenAI, Gemini or Ollama marks from dashboard-icons, Apache-2.0, bundled in `apps/web/src/assets/icons` with a NOTICE; a chip for a model it cannot place, a robot before the first report; T-1825), the role (`agent`) as the name, else the title; the model short ("Opus 5.5"); a status (Running with a slow spinner, Waiting on a person, Failed, Done, Asleep, Idle); the ticket id; cost (`sessionCost`, the cents muted); a child count of what is shown that folds its subtree. An ⓘ holds the rest: the title as the task, the time (from its start to now while it runs or waits, else to its last activity), its own worktree branch and Bypass on. The session carries no list of tools or MCP servers, so the card shows none. The whole card opens the agent. The lead's card is wider than its workers' from 1024 px. |
| Status | Failed: interrupted, or ended for a credential or integrity stop or an ending that reads as an error. Done: ended any other way. Idle (alive and quiet) is said as it is, never shown as Done. |
| Header | "N agents · M running · K failed · $total". "K failed" opens and closes a strip in the page's flow right under the lead's card, as wide as it, pushing the workers down (it covers nothing; its height animates, at once with reduced motion; Escape closes it and gives focus back to the chip; T-1825 replaced a floating list that kept covering cards). It lists the failed ones, one line each (the agent, its task cut short, the task and the reason in its tooltip), with **Dismiss** (hidden from the count in this browser, `claudepilot.agent-tree.dismissed`, until the agent fails again) and **Retry**: `recover` for an integrity ending, a short resume message over the chat socket for an interrupted one, none for a credential stop (open the agent). |
| Layout | Phone: an indented column with one line down the left and an elbow into each card. From 1024 px: the lead on top, its workers in a row under it joined by a bar (the row scrolls inside its box when wider than the page, and an edge with more beyond it fades out), deeper levels as indented columns. The lines are light blue and leave from a small node dot under the parent card (T-1825). |
| Motion | Cards fade and rise in, a status cross-fades, a fold animates its height; with reduced motion only fades remain (`MotionConfig reducedMotion="user"`) and the spinner stops. |

Rules and tests: `apps/web/src/features/chat/agentTreeData.ts`, `agentTreeData.test.ts`.

### 12.3 Session screen and approvals dock (`/admin/sessions/:id`)

| Part                                      | Design                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Floating header (2026-10-13, every width) | **Built (UX1).** A frosted bar in the composer's style (52 px, fully rounded), inset by the same gap as the composer (`--bar-gap`, Web app §10.5) and placed under the status bar; the transcript scrolls under it, under the composer and under the status bar's band. It holds only: back, the title (truncated; a tap opens **Session info**: persona, workspace, Claude, ticket, CI runs, worktree branch, and Rename), the state (a badge in one slot; "Reconnecting" with a spinner while the link is down), and ⋯. Bypass adds a red shield (one tap turns it off, FR20) and tints the bar; from 640 px it reads "Bypass ×" and Files and Agents are icon buttons. Everything else is in ⋯: Turn off Bypass (first, while on), Info, Files and Agents (phones), Rename, Usage, Notify when done, Show all details, Restart, Move, Archive, End, Delete. The permission mode is no longer in ⋯: its chip is always in the composer.                              |
| Phone header (before 2026-10-13)          | Frosted glass: the transcript scrolls under it and under the composer, a floating frosted card (the text on top; a row under it with + for images, the model-and-effort pill, the mode pill when it isn't Ask, the context ring and a round Send that turns into Stop while a turn runs, or Steer once something is written). Replies are 16.5 px and user messages grey bubbles; "Jump to latest" floats above the card once scrolled up. The screen follows the visual viewport, so focusing the composer never scrolls the header away (iOS included). 48 px plus the safe area: back with the number of other sessions waiting ("‹ 2", read as "Back to sessions, 2 need input"), the title (tap to rename), a subtitle with the state in words ("Working · api-dev · ~/work/api") that opens **Session info** (persona, pool and account, budget with Raise up to the policy's highest, context, Claude Code version), and ⋯.                                     |
| Desktop header                            | 56 px: title, StatusDot, workspace, diff and Agents chips, and ⋯.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ⋯ menu                                    | While working, Stop all and "Stop turn, keep queued" come first; then Files, Diff, Browser (where assigned), Search, Show all details, Session info, MCP servers, Reload plugins, "Notify when done" (Web app §11.1), Open shell, Keyboard shortcuts (desktop); after a divider, Archive and End session (danger).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Above the composer                        | Bottom up: queued messages ("2 queued ▾" past the first), then the activity strip, or the dock while a request waits.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Dock                                      | A labelled region ("Waiting for you") with "‹ 1 of 3 ›" in its header and 44 px Deny, Allow and ⋯ on every touch screen. The transcript keeps at least 25% of the visible viewport and the dock takes at most 45%, its preview scrolling inside with Deny and Allow pinned at its bottom. With the keyboard open it collapses to a 56 px bar ("Approval needed · Run npm test · Review") that expands on tap and closes the keyboard. **Built in part (2026-10-13):** the card is at most 45 % of the visible height and the stack above the composer leaves the transcript 25 % below the header (the card's text block gives way, down to about two lines); with the keyboard up, or on a screen under 500 px tall, the card is a 56 px bar ("3 approvals · Run a command … · Review"), Review puts the keyboard away and opens the card. Not built: "‹ 1 of 3 ›" and ⋯ in the card (it says "3 waiting"). Checked by `tools/qa/session-states.mjs` (Web app §10.5). |
| Transcript                                | Sticks to the bottom while streaming unless scrolled up. Streaming text is revealed word by word at a steady pace, never more than about half a second behind the stream (once it ends, what is left shows within a quarter of a second), each word pulling into focus from a soft blur; code shows line by line and a table row by row. The view follows the end with an eased glide that stops the moment the reader touches, scrolls or presses a key, and "Jump to latest" glides too; with reduced motion the text appears as it arrives and the view moves at once. Nothing animates when a transcript opens, and the stored reply takes the live one's place without a visible change (motion code: `apps/web/src/features/chat/motion/`). "Jump to latest" (44 px, bottom right above the composer, "12 new") appears when scrolled up; loading older blocks keeps the viewport anchored. The always-dark Bash block gets a hairline border in dark mode.      |
| Notices                                   | Toasts for other sessions sit above the dock and composer, in the attention tone (gold "!") with Open. Pushed screens pin "Approval needed · Review", which returns to the dock. Skill changes show "Skills changed · Reload" (`/reload-plugins` at a turn boundary); MCP set changes show "Start a new session to apply MCP changes", and a browser assignment "Start a new session to use the browser".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

### 12.4 Composer and pickers

| Part           | Design                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Card           | Attachments, a text box ("Message Claude", or "Steer Claude" while working; 1–10 lines on desktop, up to 40% of the visible viewport on phones), then the footer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Phone footer   | **Built (2026-10-13):** **+**, the model chip (its name only; the effort is in its label and the sheet), the mode as a 44 px icon chip that is always there (danger tint for Bypass, gold for other non-Manual modes), the context ring, btw and the trailing button. The mode chip and the ring have fixed sizes, so no mode (Bypass included) and no context figure changes the row: the composer's box is the same with Bypass on and off, keyboard up or down (`tools/qa/session-chrome.mjs`). Before, the Bypass chip's text squeezed the model chip to an empty 24 px pill. The card floats `--bar-gap` from the left, right and bottom edges of the screen (the physical edge, home indicator or not; Web app §10.5). Draft: **+** pinned left and one 44 px trailing button pinned right; the Mode and Model · Effort chips share the space between, truncating the model name first and then the mode to its icon, never wrapping or scrolling. The trailing button follows the state: with text or attachments, Send (it steers while working, and reads "Deny & send" or "Answer" while a card is docked); empty while working, Stop; idle and empty, Send disabled. The strip keeps its own Stop. |
| Desktop footer | **+** and Mode on the left; Model · Effort, the context ring, Stop (secondary, at least 8 px from Send) and Send on the right.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Pickers        | One level, in PickerSheet: an anchored popover from 640 px and a bottom sheet with × below it, with focus trap, Esc and Back.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Mode           | Manual "Asks before edits and commands"; Accept edits "Edits files without asking; asks before commands"; Plan "Reads and plans; changes nothing until you approve"; Auto "A safety check reviews each action before it runs"; Don't ask "Runs only pre-approved actions and denies the rest without asking"; Bypass, in the danger color and only in bypass-capable sessions, "Runs everything without asking. Isolated workspaces only". Blocked rows say why. Before the first Send, where policy allows, a SwitchRow "Allow Bypass later in this session". Layout (2026-10-13, checked at 390 px and 1280 px): the picker's five rows (Plan, Manual, Edit, Auto and, where allowed, Bypass) fit one sheet, each name on one line, inside the screen.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Model · Effort | Context use (phones); one row per model family (Default, Opus, Fable, Sonnet, Haiku) with its hint, picking a family switching to its newest version, and the picked family's versions as a row of buttons (newest first; an alias such as "opus" stands for the newest); effort as a slider over the model's own levels, Low to Max, each level named under its stop (dragging only moves it, letting go applies it; a model without "Extra high" has one stop fewer); the Ultracode switch ("Plans multi-agent workflows. Uses many more tokens. $6.20 left").                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| New session    | "Runs Claude Code in api-dev · Persona Reviewer r4 · pool team-a" under the composer; the persona part opens Persona's read-only sheet (its §15).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

### 12.6 Interaction details

- Every tap answers within 100 ms; sends render at once and reconcile by UUID. Reduced motion stops
  the Working pulse. Nothing autofocuses on touch, Shift+Tab stays with the browser, inputs are
  16 px, and Enter adds a line while an on-screen keyboard is in use.
- **Keys:** Enter sends whenever a hardware keyboard is in use, whatever the width (Web app §9.4),
  Shift+Enter adds a line, Alt+Enter sends when Claude finishes, and ⌘/Ctrl+Enter sends on every
  device. In an empty composer
  while Claude works, the first Esc shows "Press Esc again to stop" for 1.5 s and the second stops;
  an Esc that closes a picker, menu or the slash list doesn't count. ⌘/Ctrl+. stops at once. ⌘K
  opens the global palette scoped to sessions (Web app FR7), with "Go to waiting request" first
  when one waits. With the list focused, ↑
  and ↓ move and Enter opens. ⌘/ lists shortcuts. No browser-reserved combinations, and no
  single-key shortcuts outside a focused widget.

| On a phone                                     | Where                                             | Taps          |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------- |
| Answer a waiting approval                      | List row → Allow                                  | 2             |
| Steer; withdraw a queued message               | Send; ✕                                           | 1; 1          |
| Send when Claude finishes                      | Queued row ⋯ → item                               | 2             |
| Stop the turn; stop everything; stop one agent | Stop; strip ▴ → Stop all; strip ▴ → agent → Stop  | 1; 2; 3       |
| Model, effort, ultracode or mode               | Chip → row or switch                              | 2             |
| Bypass                                         | Mode chip → Bypass → tick → Confirm, then re-auth | 4 and re-auth |
| Open the diff or the turn's images             | Turn summary row                                  | 1             |
| Download an image                              | Thumbnail → Download → Save Image                 | 3             |

### 12.7 Speed targets

The reference device is a Moto G Power-class phone, or Chrome with 4× CPU throttling; every phone
target here and in §15 uses it.

| Measure                                                  | Target                                                                                                    |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| First token on screen after Claude Code writes it        | p95 ≤ 250 ms on Docker targets, ≤ 750 ms on Kubernetes 🔬; characters held back for redaction don't count |
| Keystroke to paint while a session streams at full speed | p95 ≤ 50 ms; no main-thread task over 50 ms while streaming                                               |
| Sent message visible; shown as Queued                    | Under 50 ms (optimistic); within 500 ms                                                                   |
| Approval tap to Claude Code's acknowledgement            | p95 ≤ 500 ms on Docker (one send, 37–270 ms ✅); ≤ 1 s on Kubernetes 🔬                                   |
| Open a cached session; cold open over Wi-Fi              | Under 150 ms; p75 ≤ 1 s (one request ≤ 100 KB, the last 200 blocks)                                       |
| Streaming render; scrolling 5,000 blocks                 | At most one React commit per animation frame per session; 60 fps; CLS ≤ 0.1                               |
| Chat code                                                | Its own route chunk, ≤ 150 KB gzip without the highlighter                                                |

Streaming a reply reveals it at most about 30 times a second and applies incoming deltas about 30 times a second; finished blocks are rendered once and only the block in progress is parsed again, and the rows already in the transcript are not rendered again for each piece. Measured on a 5,000-word reply streamed at about 70 words a second (production build, headless Chromium with software rendering, 390 px at 2x, 8 shared cores at load 4): 4,376 frames, p50 16.7 ms, p99 16.8 ms, 36 frames over 20 ms and 3 over 50 ms (worst 167 ms); script about 84 ms of each second, flat from 2,700 to 15,000 DOM nodes; the view moved in 97% of the frames, never backwards, p95 step 10 px, 30 px (p95 60) behind the end; no word already showing ever moved (apps/web `npm test` covers the pacing, block splitting and cut logic).

How: deltas flushed once per animation frame into a per-session store, so a token re-renders one
block; transcript updates in `startTransition`; markdown memoized per block (Streamdown);
highlighting in idle time; `content-visibility: auto` up to 150 blocks, then end-anchored
virtualization (TanStack Virtual); image boxes sized from upload metadata; hidden tabs pause all
but attention.

### 12.8 Accessibility

- The transcript is `role="feed"`, one article per message (`aria-busy` while streaming;
  `aria-setsize` and `aria-posinset` under virtualization). A visually hidden `role="status"` says
  "Claude replied" or the state change, never reply text; requests use `role="alert"` ("Approval
  needed: Run npm test"). The strip's timer is `aria-hidden`.
- Labels: the composer ("Message Claude", "Steer Claude"), the ring ("Context 64% used"), images
  ("Photo you attached, 1 of 3", "Screenshot from playwright"). StatusDot pairs color, shape and
  text. Everything works by keyboard (focus order: header, transcript, dock, composer; pickers trap
  focus and close on Esc and Back). On coarse pointers Send, Stop, Allow, Deny and the dock's arrows
  are 44 px at every width. Reflow at 320 px and 200% zoom.

## 13. Security requirements

| Area                  | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Threat model          | Code running as the workspace user can already do whatever an approval would allow, so approvals don't contain it; they keep the model from acting unasked. Such code can also write a session's FIFO and read its segment, so a forged line could approve a request, switch the mode or pose as the person. Chat detects that and ends the session (§10.4), and Settings' rules keep Claude's own file tools out of `~/.claudepilot/**` (A6).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Credentials and trust | Eligibility and the transition lock per §6; folder trust per FR43. No keys in flags, control requests or input lines; `apply_flag_settings` only from its allowlist.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Redaction             | Control responses and `system/init` are stored as allowlisted fields only: from `get_settings`, only `applied` (model, effort, advisor, ultracode, ultracodeRequested, ultracodeAvailable), never `effective` or `sources`; from `mcp_status`, name, status, scope, server name and version and tool names, never `config`; from `get_usage`, session totals and `rate_limits_available`, never rate-limit values (any reported limit only ends the session, §6 item 3); from `system/init`, `apiKeySource` only as match or mismatch. The shared value redactor (Secrets & env A9: delivered values, their encodings, credential shapes) then runs as a backstop before SQLite, a browser or Portal MCP sees anything. **Secret values (QA-006):** besides credential shapes, text from a workspace is redacted by the secret values Secrets & env delivered into it (current and the last 30 days; raw, JSON-escaped, URL-encoded, and base64 at any alignment), in stored events, requests, tasks, live deltas (a value split across deltas is held back until it can't be one) and previews (`controller.scrub`); the values are read into memory for 10 s at a time and never stored, logged or sent. The portal's own tokens (`cpat_`, `cpoa_`, `cpor_`, `cpst_`) are shapes too: a session's agent token sits in that process's environment, so a tool can print it, and it is stored as `[redacted]`. |
| Streamed text         | Never forwarded raw. Each open block (text, thinking, tool input) accumulates per session and is redacted whole; browsers get only the prefix that can no longer become a match. Trailing characters are held while they're a prefix of a redaction value, one of its encodings or a credential shape (a trie), and flushed at block end, where only the final block is stored.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Approvals             | Allow never echoes input, "always allow" adds only session rules the portal builds, and no directory is ever added (§10.3). Answers name their actor (device or MCP token), are idempotent per request, and are audited.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Files and Diff        | Reads open the file, check the opened descriptor's real path (`/proc/self/fd/N`) against FR33, then stream from it, so a swapped symlink can't redirect them; archives never follow symlinks. git runs as `git -c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c diff.external= --no-pager diff --no-ext-diff --no-textconv`, status with the same `-c` flags, an empty override for each `filter.<name>` in the repository's config, and a 5 s timeout.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Rendering             | Sanitized markdown, no raw HTML; remote images in replies render as links (an image URL can carry data out). Files: the type is sniffed from the bytes (the sender's claim and the extension are only hints), every response carries `nosniff`, and only an allow-list is served inline (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, PDF, audio and video, with Range); everything else, HTML, SVG and scripts included, is `application/octet-stream` with `Content-Disposition: attachment`. HTML previews only inside `<iframe sandbox>` (no scripts, forms, same-origin or top navigation); SVG is never rendered. Names are made safe (no path, control or direction-changing character, at most 120 characters, ASCII in the workspace). Observed in a production build: PDF, audio, video and sandboxed HTML preview under the app's CSP with no violation, and a script inside an HTML file never ran. The app's CSP replaces a response's own, so a blob's `sandbox` header is not what protects it: nothing active is ever served inline.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Transport and storage | Origin and cookie checks on the socket, CSRF on HTTP, 1 MB frames, FR30–FR31 sizes. Raw segments stay in the workspace until ingested and purged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Shared visibility     | With one shared server code, everyone signed in can read every session, and workspace users can read its files in the workspace.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

## 14. Observability and audit

- Event log (actor per row): session starts, resumes, sleeps, interruptions and ends with reasons;
  integrity ends, subscription alarms, credential refusals and drift ends (High); folder trust
  granted; transition-lock refusals; mode changes (entering Bypass is High); model, effort and
  ultracode changes; approvals (tool, decision, rules added, never input); budget, cap and memory
  stops; file transfers.
- Metrics for Monitoring: sessions by state, oldest Needs input, failed turns, first token on
  screen, send latency, reader lag, reattaches, interruptions, sleeps and wake time, characters held
  for redaction; per session, reader errors.

## 15. Testing and verification

| Suite               | What it covers                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | When                                              |
| ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| Sandbox ✅          | DinD, mock API, flags off. Raw exec: multi-turn stream-json, streamed deltas, steering and withdrawal, plain interrupt, model, effort, ultracode on, three mode switches, `bypass_not_launched`, stdio approvals, images, resume, subagent and retry events. FIFO host: reattach after one killed reader, a send with no reader, a 5.7 MB line, interrupt, `end_session`. Its mock API seeds the suites.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Done                                              |
| Contract            | Per CLI version, producing the capability matrix: every flag in §10.2 and row of §10.3; the first lines (start mode against a managed `defaultMode: bypassPermissions`, Bypass → default, default ⇄ auto); Stop with a non-empty queue and with background agents, with and without `perTaskStopAffordance`; §10.4's injections through the FIFO and from a stdio MCP server; a malformed line and an exec killed mid-send; `cp-session run`; the parent shell and `exit.json`; Q8's budget cases; `get_usage` fields with `behaviors` null; a command whose stored copy was redacted runs unchanged when allowed, and no file under `.claude/` changes after "Always allow"; a planted `.mcp.json` and hook (FR43). | CI, before the pinned version moves               |
| Durability          | All three targets: a reader killed mid-turn, a send with no reader, a portal restart mid-turn and with a pending approval, a container restart and resume, resuming twice, an OOM kill.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | CI (DinD, k3d); by hand on Docker Desktop and k3s |
| Guards and canaries | A workspace without a pool is refused; the transition lock refuses launches; drift on account files ends sessions; a user-level helper with an inline fake key, a managed MCP header and an exposed Secrets & env value never reach stored events, frames or MCP results; a key streamed one to three characters per delta never reaches a frame; symlink swaps and git config commands fail (§13).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | CI                                                |
| UI and load         | Playwright at 320×568, 360×780, 390×844 and desktop sizes; keyboard open at 375×667 and 390×844 on iOS Safari, an installed iOS app and Android Chrome (dock budget, Send never moves); Enter never approves; a double tap on Allow approves exactly one request; Bypass takes three deliberate taps plus re-auth; offline; 5,000 blocks at 60 fps on the reference device; axe clean; 20 streaming sessions within one portal core and 300 MB RSS.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | CI; devices by hand                               |

## 17. Success metrics

| Metric                                                                                                             | Target                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| First token on screen after Claude Code writes it                                                                  | p95 ≤ 250 ms (Docker), ≤ 750 ms (Kubernetes)                |
| Events lost or duplicated across tab drops, portal restarts and reattaches                                         | Zero, by automated test                                     |
| Sessions on credentials the portal didn't deliver; Bypass entered without policy, opt-in, confirmation and re-auth | Zero; zero                                                  |
| Injected inputs in the integrity suite that don't end the session                                                  | Zero                                                        |
| Secrets in stored events, socket frames, MCP results or logs                                                       | Zero (canary tests, split deltas included)                  |
| Phone: answer a waiting approval from the list; start a session                                                    | Two taps (row, Allow); one screen (New session, type, Send) |
| Interrupted sessions that resume with their history                                                                | 100% while Claude Code's transcript exists                  |
| Cold open of a session on the reference phone                                                                      | p75 ≤ 1 s                                                   |

## 20. Side questions (btw)

|               |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Status        | **Built** (server S1 2026-10-12, UI S2 2026-10-12, the two together S2b 2026-10-13). The investigation, the contract (`packages/contracts/src/chatBtw.ts`), the engine, usage rows, pin, authorization and the tests are built and were run against the real Claude Code and a real portal (§20.9); the UI was run against the real engine in a browser (§20.12, BV9).                                                                                            |
| Depends on    | §10 (the host and the stream protocol), §13 (redaction), [Workspace Claudes](workspace-claudes.md) (a session's Claude and its sign-in), [Claude usage](claude-usage.md) (the usage rows), [Authorization](authorization.md) (`can`, actors)                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Owner's words | "Add btw to the session chat, like `/btw` in Claude Code: a separate Claude instance that sees the session so far, without tools, one answer; it must not disturb a running turn, change the session or add to its history; a card that can be closed, with Pin; quota and cost marked as estimates; a rate limit; the reason when it fails. First test whether the real CLI supports it in the stream-json mode the portal uses; if not, build it." (2026-10-12) |

### 20.1 What it is

One question about the session, asked while the agent works, idles, sleeps or after it ended. A SEPARATE Claude Code run with every tool off answers it once from the session's redacted transcript up to that moment. The answer is a temporary card for the person who asked; **Pin** copies the question and answer into the transcript as one card. Nothing else is written: not the session's running turn, queue, counters, usage totals, transcript or Claude's context. A follow-up is a new btw.

Goals: cheap and safe to ask at any time; the person sees what it cost (an estimate) and why it failed. Non-goals: tools or actions of any kind; a conversation (no thread, no history of earlier btws is sent); answering from files the transcript doesn't show; a replacement for a message to the agent (a btw never reaches it).

### 20.2 Investigation: does the real Claude Code do /btw in the portal's stream-json mode?

**Method.** Claude Code `2.1.296 (Claude Code)`, the one on the dev machine and the one in `claudepilot-workspace:latest` (both print that version), started with the portal's flags (`-p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages --replay-user-messages --permission-prompt-tool stdio --permission-mode default --session-id <uuid>`), a private `HOME` and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` on a scripted stand-in for the Messages API that logs every request (no real credential, no network). What the stand-in cannot show (a real model's behaviour, real cache hits) is marked "inferred". The harness and raw outputs stay in the build's scratch area; the facts the design rests on are re-checked by `apps/server/src/chat/btw.integration.test.ts` (§20.9), so a new pinned CLI version re-tests them.

**Results.**

| #   | Question                                           | Observed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1   | `/btw <q>` as the text of a stream-json user line? | **No.** The CLI answers it locally, with no API call: `assistant ["/btw isn't available in this environment."]`, then `result/success … result="/btw isn't available in this environment." dur=13 usage={input_tokens:0, output_tokens:0}`; the stand-in saw no request. It would also show in the main stream as an assistant message and a result, like a turn. (`/btw` is an interactive-only command; `-p` has no flag for it.)                                                                         |
| 2   | Is the feature there at all in this mode?          | **Yes, as a control request** (the Agent SDK's `askSideQuestion`): `{"type":"control_request","request_id":…,"request":{"subtype":"side_question","question":"…","history":[{"question":"…","response":"…"}]}}` answers `system/control_request_progress {status:"started"}` then `control_response {subtype:"success", response:{response:"SIDE-ANSWER to: …", synthetic:false}}`; `control_cancel_request` ends it with `control_response error "Side question cancelled"` and the process stays healthy. |
| 3   | Does it disturb the main session?                  | Not the turn: with the stand-in holding the main request mid-stream it answered in 28 ms while the turn stayed held, which then finished on its own; with a tool call awaiting approval it saw the call as `[No result yet — this call is still in progress in the main conversation (running, awaiting approval, or queued)]`. Not the session file (its size did not change during the call) and the next main request carries no trace of it.                                                            |
| 4   | Is it cheap?                                       | Cache-sharing: its request has the same `tools` and `system` as the main request and the main messages as its prefix (compared byte for byte in the stand-in's log), so with a warm cache it reads the cache (inferred).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 5   | Are the tools off?                                 | **Denied, not absent.** Its request still carries all 23 tool definitions (that is how it shares the cache); a call is refused after the fact: `(The model tried to call Bash instead of answering directly. Try rephrasing or ask in the main conversation.)` `synthetic:true`, nothing ran. But a `PreToolUse` hook configured in the user's settings **did run** on that denied attempt (`hook.log: pre-1791783793`; `PostToolUse` did not).                                                             |
| 6   | What does it cost, and where does that show?       | Its usage is added to the process's running totals and not reported: after one side question `get_session_cost` went from `$0.0066 (40 output)` to `$0.0084 (73 output)`, and the next turn's `result.modelUsage` carried it (`inputTokens 2430 = 1200 + 30 + 1200`) while `result.usage` did not. The portal's per-turn rows are the change in `modelUsage` (§9), so the side question would land in the next turn's row, in `total_cost_usd` and in `--max-budget-usd`. The reply has no usage field.     |
| 7   | How does it fail?                                  | As text and slowly. 529: after two retries `synthetic:true` with `(API error: Repeated 529 Overloaded errors. … check your inference gateway (127.0.0.1:41919).)`, a raw host inside an "answer". 429 and 401: `api_retry` attempts 1 to 10 (delays 1 s, 1 s, 2.4 s, 4.5 s, 8.7 s, 17.6 s, …), not finished after 30 s.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| 8   | Without a live process?                            | Not available: a sleeping or ended session has none.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

**Candidates** for the separate run, compared on the same stand-in:

|                          | Native `side_question` on the session's process          | Fork: `--resume <id> --fork-session --tools "" --no-session-persistence` | Fresh run fed the portal's redacted transcript                                                                 |
| ------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Context                  | the live conversation, in-flight turn included           | the session file: whole, completed messages only                         | the stored events: bounded, up to the last stored message                                                      |
| Tools                    | offered (23) and denied; a user hook fired               | none: `init.tools=[]`, request `tools=0`                                 | none: `init.tools=[]`, `mcp_servers=[]`, `slash_commands=0`                                                    |
| Writes                   | `.claude.json` counters only                             | none: no new file, the original byte-identical (sha1 unchanged)          | none                                                                                                           |
| Cache                    | shared                                                   | none: `tools` (first in the cache order) differs from the main's 23      | none                                                                                                           |
| Cost bound               | none: the whole context, uncached when the cache is cold | none: the whole session, every time                                      | **bounded** by the context limit (§20.3)                                                                       |
| Usage                    | folded into the process's totals, not reported           | its own `result`                                                         | its own `result`: `total_cost_usd 0.00178`, `usage {input 30, cache_read 5000, output 33}` in the stand-in run |
| Redaction before sending | impossible (the CLI holds the context)                   | impossible (the CLI loads its own file)                                  | by construction                                                                                                |
| Needs                    | the live process                                         | the session file in that Claude's directory, same folder                 | nothing but a running workspace                                                                                |

**Decision: the fresh run.** It is the only candidate that satisfies W1 to W4 as written (tools absent and provable, session and usage totals untouched, exact usage, redacted before sending), works for every session whatever its state, and has a bounded cost. The fork is kept as the reference it was compared with; the native request is documented, **not built** (§20.11 says what adding it would take). The coordinator's engine rule also says never through the long-lived process.

**How "no tools" is proven.**

- The flags give the model nothing to call: `--tools ""` (the request carries no `tools`: the stand-in's log shows `tools=0`), `--strict-mcp-config` with no config, `--disable-slash-commands`, `ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false`, and `--safe-mode`, which also leaves out hooks, CLAUDE.md, skills and plugins: with a `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse` and `Stop` hook configured, **none ran** under `--safe-mode`, while without it `SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, Stop` ran.
- Every run's `system/init` is read before anything is shown: `tools` and `mcp_servers` must be empty, or the run is stopped and the btw fails as `unsafe` (a guard that should never fire; a test makes the stand-in report a tool). An answer that contains a `tool_use` block is dropped the same way.
- No session file is written (`--no-session-persistence`). The first Claude Code run in a workspace creates the CLI's own first-start config (`~/.claude.json` and a backup of it); after that, a btw run changes no file at all: the home folder's file hashes are identical before and after (checked in the image, §20.9).

### 20.3 How it works

1. **Ask** (`btw_ask`, chat socket). Refused with an `ack` when malformed (empty, over 2 000 characters), the session is unknown or the person may not operate it. Otherwise a `btw` message follows at once: `running`, or `failed` with a reason (`disabled`, `busy`, `rate_limit` with `retryAfterMs`, `no_quota`, `signed_out`, `workspace`).
2. **Gates, in order**: the setting is on; one btw in flight per session; the per-session limit (default 6 starts in 10 minutes); the portal's own spending gate (`sendGate`: a paused budget); the session's Claude is signed in or its pool is set up (the same check a start uses); the workspace answers.
3. **Context** (`chat/btwContext.ts`): the session's stored events up to now, newest whole messages first until the limit (default 60 000 characters): what the person sent, Claude's replies, each tool call as its name and one-line summary and a short excerpt of its result. Left out: thinking, sub-agent lines, system notes, pinned btw cards. Redacted by the chat's two redactors (credential shapes, and the workspace's secret values) **before** anything is sent, then fenced as data (`<session_transcript>`; a literal closing tag in it reads as text). The question stays outside the fence.
4. **Run** (`chat/btwRun.ts`), through the same `exec` the other host commands use, in a neutral folder (`$HOME`: no project settings are even candidates), under `env -i` plus `HOME`, `PATH`, the proxy variables and the session's Claude's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (the session's own sign-in; for a pool, the managed helper), never through the session's process or FIFO: `timeout <limit+5> claude -p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --include-partial-messages --tools "" --safe-mode --no-session-persistence --disable-slash-commands --strict-mcp-config --permission-mode default --effort low --max-budget-usd 0.5 --system-prompt <fixed> [--model <the session's>]`, the transcript and question as one user line on stdin.
5. **Answer**: text deltas go through the session's streaming redactor (a tail that could become a secret waits for the next delta) to the asking person's tabs as `btw_delta`; the final text, redacted again whole and cut at 12 000 characters, is the card's. The `result` gives the usage.
6. **End**: `answered`, `cancelled` (the person's cancel or close: `interrupt`, then the exec is closed; the workspace's `timeout` is the backstop), or `failed` with one reason code. The first retry notice of a 401 or 403 (`signed_out`) or 429 (`api_limit`) and the second of a 5xx (`overloaded`) stop the run at once instead of waiting out the CLI's ten retries; the portal's own limit (default 60 s) is `timeout`.
7. **Usage**: one `chat_usage` row, `kind = 'side'`, per run that reached the API (§20.6), through `onSideUsage` (not `onUsage`); the result's numbers, labelled estimates.
8. **Pin** (`btw_pin`): one `system` event with `btw`, from what the portal kept (never from the tab), once, by the person who asked.
9. **Close** (`btw_close`): stops a running one and hides the card; a refusal (never stored) is closed on the person's other tabs by a bare `closed` snapshot.

### 20.4 Requirements

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Status                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| BTW1  | **Separate, tool-free run.** Each btw is one `claude -p` run in the session's workspace with the flags of §20.3; never the session's process; `init.tools` and `mcp_servers` empty or the btw fails `unsafe`; no session file written.                   | **Built:** flags asserted in `btwRun.test.ts`; on the real CLI (`btw.integration.test.ts`) `init.tools=[]`, `mcp_servers=[]`, no configured hook ran, no file changed after the first run, and a stand-in that answers with a tool call ends as `unsafe` with nothing run                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

Refusals don't count toward the limit.                                     | **Built:** `btw.test.ts`: `busy` without a record, `rate_limit` with `retryAfterMs` and a sliding window (one allowance per session for everyone), `timeout`; the same three in the scratch portal (`rate_limit retryAfterMs=576331`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

| BTW6  | **Reasons**, never raw API text: `rate_limit`, `busy`, `no_quota`, `api_limit`, `signed_out`, `overloaded`, `timeout`, `workspace`, `unsafe`, `empty`, `disabled`, `error` (`BTW_REASON_TEXT`). Fail fast on the first 401/403/429 notice.               | **Built:** 11 failure shapes map to codes with no raw API text (`btw.test.ts`); a 401, 403 or 429 notice ends the run at once; in the scratch portal `[[err 429]]` is `api_limit`, `[[err 529]]` `overloaded`, a tool answer `unsafe`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| BTW7  | **Estimates.** Tokens (in, out, cache) and `costUsd` from the run's own report, always `estimate: true`; before it reports, a size-based token estimate with no cost.                                                                                    | **Built:** `estimate: true` always; `basis: 'reported'` from the run's `result` (`input 30, output 20, costUsd 0.00052` in the scratch run), `'estimated'` with no cost before it reports, none when the API was never reached                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
