Never a turn: no `result`, no session cost.                | **Built:** one `chat_usage` row (`kind='side'`, `ref`) per run that reported usage; the scratch portal's Usage totals counted the turn and the btw as 2 requests; `onUsage` is never called for it (`onSideUsage` is). **Built (2026-10-13):** `usagespend` hears `onSideUsage` (wired to `onTurn` in `index.ts`, like `onUsage`), so a budget is evaluated and spikes looked for when the btw's row is written, not at the next turn; the attribution report carries `side` (the `kind = 'side'` part of `totals`; [Claude usage](claude-usage.md) §22). Seen in `usagespend/service.test.ts`: a $1 btw on a $10 budget at $7.50 told `near` 85 % at once. |

Claude never sees it; a later btw's context leaves it out.                                              | **Built:** pin appends one `system` event with `btw` (stored, read back whole, sent to every tab of the session), refuses a second pin, an unanswered one and another person's; a later btw's context leaves it out                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

| BTW11 | **Authorization and privacy.** A btw needs `operate` on its session (`can`); its messages go only to the asking person's tabs; only the asker may cancel, close or pin it; a shared session shares a pinned card, never an open one.                     | **Built:** `can(actor, 'operate', session)` through one resolver; another user's ask, cancel, close, pin and replay are `NOT_FOUND` (404, like a missing session) and their messages never reach a tab of someone else, even one watching the same session (`btw.test.ts`, `btw.socket.test.ts`); an access change cancels the person's runs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

| BTW13 | **Recovery.** A btw left `running` by a restart becomes `failed` (`error`); a tab that subscribes gets the person's open btws of the last hour (`btw` messages).                                                                                         | **Built:** a restart ends a running record as `failed` (`error`); a tab that subscribes gets the open cards (3 in the scratch run) and a closed one is not sent again                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

### 20.5 Protocol

Client → portal (added to `ChatClientMessage`): `btw_ask {session, btw, question}`, `btw_cancel {session, btw}`, `btw_close {session, btw}`, `btw_pin {session, btw, uuid}`. Portal → the asking person's tabs (added to `ChatServerMessage`): `btw {session, item: ChatBtw}` (the whole state: at the start, at every change, at the end and on subscribe), `btw_delta {session, btw, text, seq}`. A refusal that creates no btw is an `ack {uuid: btw, ok: false, error, code}`; a pin is answered by an `ack` keyed by its `uuid` and then the card arrives as a stored event (`events`) for everyone subscribed. Types, limits and reason texts: `packages/contracts/src/chatBtw.ts`.

### 20.6 Data

```sql
CREATE TABLE chat_btw (
  id TEXT NOT NULL, session_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  user_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'u_owner', created_by TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'u_owner', actor TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'user:u_owner',
  workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL, claude_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  question TEXT NOT NULL, answer TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '',
  status TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (status IN ('running','answered','failed','cancelled')), reason TEXT, note TEXT, retry_after_ms INTEGER,
  model TEXT, ctx_messages INTEGER, ctx_truncated INTEGER, ctx_last_seq INTEGER, ctx_chars INTEGER,
  input_tokens INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, output_tokens INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, cache_read_tokens INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  cache_write_tokens INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, cost_usd REAL, basis TEXT,
  pinned_seq INTEGER, closed INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  asked_at TEXT NOT NULL, asked_ms INTEGER NOT NULL, settled_at TEXT,
  PRIMARY KEY (user_id, session_id, id)
) STRICT;
ALTER TABLE chat_usage ADD COLUMN kind TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'turn';  -- 'side' for a btw
ALTER TABLE chat_usage ADD COLUMN ref TEXT;                          -- the btw's id
```

Rows go with their session (they hold its content); the usage rows stay, like every usage row. `user_id` and `created_by` default to the founding user (`u_owner`): the multi-user retrofit stamps `chat_usage.user_id` from `chat_btw.user_id` through `ref`.

### 20.7 Security and multi-user

- The transcript is untrusted: fenced, with a system prompt that says to ignore instructions inside it; the model is given no tool to call and the CLI no hook to run; the answer is text the chat renders as safe markdown and nothing executes it. The only path from an answer to the session is the person pressing Pin, and a pinned card is not input to Claude.
- Secrets: values the workspace was given and credential shapes are taken out of the context before it leaves the portal and out of the answer as it streams (hold-back) and again whole. The question is the person's own text and goes through the shape redactor too. Nothing logs a question, a context or an answer; the audit trail holds ids and numbers.
- **Multi-user** (docs/prd/multi-user.md): a btw belongs to the person who asked and to the session's owner. It needs `can(actor, 'operate', session)`; its messages are sent only to that person's tabs; `chat_btw` carries `user_id` and `created_by` (default `u_owner`); the audit and usage entries carry `actorString(actor)`; the session is read through one resolver (`chat/authz.ts`). A revoked right cancels the person's running btws (`onAccessChange`).
- Abuse cases the tests run: another user cannot ask, cancel, close or pin a btw of someone else's session or id; a pin takes no text from the client; a closed or foreign id is `NOT_FOUND`; two users with the same btw id don't collide.

### 20.8 Limits

The answer comes from the stored transcript, not from files or live output: it can say "I can't see that". The newest messages fit; a long session's early ones may not (the card says so). No cache: each btw pays for its whole context at full price (bounded by the context limit and `maxCostUsd`). The in-flight assistant text of a running turn is not in the store yet. The run starts a Claude Code process in the workspace (about a second and a short memory burst): one per session at a time, and rate limited. On a plan the numbers say tokens and a list-price estimate, not quota used. A run that is cancelled, times out or fails before its `result` books no usage row (Claude Code reports none; the card shows a size-based estimate where the request probably went out, and none for a run that never reached the API). Refusals (`busy`, `rate_limit`, the gates) are not stored and do not count toward the limit.

### 20.11 Decisions and open questions

- **D1** The separate fresh run, not the native control request and not the fork (§20.2). The native request would need: the wire format above; a `get_session_cost` read before and after (text, rounded to 0.0001 USD and three digits of tokens) with the next turn's row corrected by the difference; the user-hook caveat accepted; a live process. Revisit if Claude Code reports a side question's usage in its reply and stops running hooks for it.
- **D2** The context is built from the portal's stored events (not the session file): bounded, redacted, works after a move and on a sleeping session.
- **D3** The model is the session's own (setting `chat.btw.model` overrides), effort low, output capped, cost capped per run.
- **D4** A btw is stored (question, answer, numbers) so Pin can use the portal's copy and a reload can show the open card; it is deleted with its session.
- **D5** Notifications: a btw produces no `result` event and no `onUsage` call, so `session_done` and every turn-keyed adapter stay silent; its usage row has `kind = 'side'` and is announced on `onSideUsage`.
- **Q-btw-1** Should the card show the quota the plan has left (Claude usage) next to the estimate? Needs the usage module's summary; not built.
- **Q-btw-2** Should a pinned card be offered to the next btw as context? Not now (a follow-up is a new btw).

No fake socket: every message the page sees is `chat/btw.ts`'s. Phone 390 (touch, iPhone safe areas) and desktop 1280, light and dark.                                                                                                                                                                      | **Run, see below**                                                               |

What the real engine does that the scripted socket (`btw-fake.js`) did not, observed on 2026-10-13 (the screenshots were looked at, not only the checks):

- **Order of states.** `btw running` arrives with a size-based `usage` (`basis: 'estimated'`, no cost: the footer says "≈ 642 tokens") and the `context` (6 messages, 2 245 characters) before the first `btw_delta`; `btw answered` replaces it with the run's own numbers ("≈ 50 tokens · ≈ $0.0005"). The fake sent no usage while running. The card handled both: the footer shows the estimate from the first frame, then the reported numbers, with no layout change (the footer has a fixed height). A plain question answers in about a second, so the skeleton ("Answering") is on screen too briefly to see; the checks read the running state on a `[[slow]]` run.
- **A stopped run** (`cancelled`) keeps the partial answer ("tick 1 tick 2", then "Stopped."), keeps the size estimate and books no cost; Pin stays visible but disabled (only an answered btw can be pinned, BTW9). The fake did the same except the estimate.
- **A real 429** (`api_limit`) carries `retryAfterMs`: the card says "Rate limited. Try again soon. Try again in 15 s." and holds Ask again until the countdown ends. The fake never sent a wait. A tool call in the answer ends as `unsafe` ("Stopped: tools were not off.") with no answer text and Ask again enabled at once.
- **Replay.** A running btw survives a reload: the page subscribes, gets the open card with the answer so far ("tick 1 … tick 6") and the deltas continue. The replayed sheet rises with the same 240 ms entrance as an asked one (it starts at opacity 0.4, translated down over the composer: a screenshot taken inside those 240 ms looks broken; the check waits them out). A replayed card is not focused (only the tab that asked moves focus). A closed btw is not replayed.
- **The main turn.** A real `[[slow 9]]` main turn streaming while a btw is asked and answered: the transcript's end stays in view (gap at most 100 px through the follower's easing), the composer does not move, the turn ends by itself and the card is untouched by it.
- **Markdown** from the engine (a table, a code block with a 200-character line, task lists, raw HTML as text) stays inside the card: the code block scrolls sideways inside its own `pre`; nothing widens the page.
- **Pin** adds exactly one card to the transcript (from the portal's copy) and the card says "Pinned".

Fixed from the run: the InfoTip now says how many messages the answer saw ("Answered from the last 6 messages of this chat", "(older ones left out)" when truncated; BTW3 asked for it and the card did not say). `scripts/scratch-portal.sh` passes `PORTAL_LOCAL_ENABLED` through so the recipe above needs no edit of the script.

## 21. Restart, Bypass across a deliberate restart, and Auto (2026-10-13)

The owner's three asks of 2026-10-13: (1) a way to restart a session without losing its context, so MCP servers delivered after it started connect; (2) a move to another Claude must keep Bypass; (3) the Auto permission mode, so the modes are Plan, Manual, Edit, Auto and Bypass.

### 21.1 Findings on the real CLI (Claude Code 2.1.296, stand-in Messages API, private HOME and `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, stream-json as the portal runs it)

| Question                                                                               | Observed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Is `--resume <same id>` enough to pick up MCP servers added since the process started? | **Yes.** Run 1 `--session-id X --mcp-config a.json` (server `alpha`): `system/init` lists alpha. Run 2 `--resume X --mcp-config ab.json`: `system/init` lists alpha **and** beta, connected; the next API request carries the earlier user message (the conversation continues) and both servers' tools; `session_id` stays X.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Is `--fork-session` needed?                                                            | **No, and the portal doesn't use it.** It also reconnects and keeps the history, but gives the run a new session id (the portal's integrity check expects the id it launched; a fork would need the row repointed like a move).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `set_permission_mode {mode:'auto'}`                                                    | Success `{mode:'auto'}`, then `system/status` and every `system/init` report `permissionMode: 'auto'`. An unknown mode: error `invalid_mode` listing `acceptEdits, auto, bypassPermissions, default, dontAsk, plan`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| When is Auto refused?                                                                  | Error `auto_mode_model` "Cannot set permission mode to auto: auto mode unavailable for this model" (Haiku 4.5, Haiku 3.5); `auto_mode_settings` "… auto mode disabled by settings" (`disableAutoMode: "disable"` in settings). Switching the model to Haiku while in Auto keeps `auto` reported (no silent mode change). The binary also names fast mode and managed settings as reasons.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| What does Auto do?                                                                     | Commands it counts as routine in the folder ran without any extra request. A risky one (`curl … \| sh`, writing outside the folder with a POST) went to a **classifier**: a separate, non-streaming Messages call with no tools (system "You are a security monitor for autonomous AI coding agents", `stop_sequences: ["</severity>"]`, answer `<severity>N</severity>[<category>…</category>]`, a second stage when the first says block). Severity under 50 ran the tool; over 50 denied it: the tool result says "denied by the Claude Code auto mode classifier. Reason: [Data Exfiltration]" and the stream carries `system/permission_denied {tool_name, decision_reason_type: 'classifier', decision_reason}`. **No `can_use_tool` request reaches the portal either way.** A classifier reply it can't read denies too ("… temporarily unavailable, so auto mode cannot determine the safety"): it fails closed. Through a gateway (any `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`) it warns once (`system/informational`) that classifier calls are billed as before. |
| Default mode without `--permission-mode`                                               | `auto` on this version: the portal always passes `--permission-mode default` (§10.2), so nothing changes for it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

| FR52  | **Restart** (menu item "Restart"; the hint chip "Tools changed" with an InfoTip and a one-tap Restart). Stops the process at a turn boundary and relaunches it with `--resume` of the same Claude Code session on a fresh log segment in the same folder; model, effort and mode come back as after any resume. Refused while a turn runs, while an approval waits (answer it first: never dropped silently), while a message is still on its way, while the chat moves, and a move is refused while it runs (`#moving` / `#restarting`). Messages sent during it wait and reach the new process in the order sent. Running background tasks stop with the old process: the app names them in a confirmation first ("Restart? Stops …"), otherwise it is one tap. Transcript divider "Restarted: MCP reconnected"; audit `session.restart` (info) with `why` (`mcp` from the chip, `manual` from the menu), how many agents stopped and what became of Bypass. `POST CHAT_API.restart(id) {why}`. | FR53  | **The hint.** `ChatSession.configChangedAt`: the time the workspace's latest delivery landed (`workspace_delivery.applied_at`, set whenever a reconcile wrote a new bundle: MCP sets, settings, secrets) when that is after the session's process started (the portal's own clock at launch; the stored start time after a portal restart). Delivery changes reach the tabs through the reconciler's change listener, only when that time moves.                                                                                                                                                             |
| FR53a | **Tools changed can be acted on at any time** (T-102; it replaced the disabled Restart that waited for an autonomous session's turn to end, which could take hours). The hint is one tappable line that says the state ("Tools changed · turn running", "Restarts when the turn ends", "Restarts after this turn", "Tools changed · restart by hand"); a tap opens a sheet on a phone and a popover on a wider screen (hung from the hint; a Restart opened from ⋯ or Session info with no hint on screen opens as the dialog at every width, T-294: before, a popover placed against nothing stayed hidden) with what changed (`ChatSession.configChanged {at, reasons[]}`: the pool areas of every delivery since the process started, from the reconciler's `changesSince`, plus `portal_tools` when the workspace's agent access was turned on or off; `other` when the areas are not known) and the actions the state has. Idle: **Restart now** (running agents named in the confirmation). Working or waiting: **Restart after this turn** (`POST restart {when: 'turn_end'}`; the chip then offers Cancel, `DELETE restart`) and **Restart now** behind a confirmation that says what stops (`{interrupt: true}`: the turn is interrupted as Stop does, waiting approvals are dropped, running agents stop, the conversation stays). **Hide until the next change** (`PATCH restart-hint {hidden}`, stored on the session: `config_hidden_at`, migration 755; a newer change shows it again). No disabled button: a control that can't act says why in visible text.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | **Built**; server tests `chat/restartQueue.test.ts`, `delivery/changes.test.ts`; web tests `restartHint.test.ts`, `lib/popover.test.ts`; browser `tools/qa/session-states.mjs --state restartSheet`                                                                                                                                       |
| FR53b | **The boundary and the automatic restart.** A queued restart fires at the next boundary, T-41's one definition of idle: no turn and no task running, no approval waiting, no message on its way, no move or restart under way; a running sub-agent keeps it waiting (a queued restart never kills one), and the sheet says so. "Apply tool changes at the next turn end" (`PATCH restart-hint {autoApply}`, stored as `auto_apply`; null is the default) is ON by default for an autonomous session (Bypass, started over MCP, or started by a portal actor) and off for a person's own: the portal queues the restart by itself, once per change, and the chip only informs; after a turn of more than 30 minutes (`busySince`, `RESTART_LONG_TURN_MS`) it offers Restart now in the chip. **Bypass (the coordinator's rule):** a queued or automatic restart is a deliberate one, so it keeps Bypass exactly as FR20a does (bypass-capable and the policy allows it); where it could not, the portal does NOT restart by itself (`restartHeld: 'bypass'`, and `when: 'turn_end'` is refused with `BYPASS_NOT_KEPT`, 409): the chip says "restart by hand" and the manual Restart asks the Bypass question. Wakes, resumes and crash restarts still start on the start mode (FR20, FR38). Audited as `session.restart` with `how` (`queue`, `auto`, `interrupt`) and actor `portal` for the automatic one. **Hot areas and the quiet time (T-472, 87 automatic restarts in 3 hours, one per context-pack push):** a delivery of only instruction contexts (area `context`) is no change for a running process. Their drop-in carries a stamp (personas/render.ts `CONTEXT_STAMP`) and the process re-reads the managed rules when the stamp changes, so the hint does not show it, does not name it and queues no restart. A persona is not counted as hot: it also sets the model, the output style, agents and hooks. The time of a change is the newest delivery with an area a running process cannot take (`configChangeLog`, the reconciler's `changeLogSince`, per delivery), so a context delivery never moves it. An automatic restart waits until that change has been quiet for `autoRestartSettleMs` (`AUTO_RESTART_SETTLE_MS`, 10 minutes): a burst of deliveries gives one restart, and the chip reads "Restarts when the turn ends" while it waits. A person's queued restart and Restart now do not wait.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | **Built**; server tests `chat/restartQueue.test.ts` (fires at the boundary only after the task ends, cancel, at the boundary already, interrupt, hide memory, the setting and its default, Bypass kept, held and refused; T-472: a context-only delivery restarts nothing, an MCP delivery still does, three in a burst give one restart) |
| FR20a | **Bypass across a deliberate restart** (a move, a Restart, Restart to allow Bypass). A session that was in Bypass keeps it while the policy still allows it: the new process is launched with `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` (`bypass_capable` and the policy), and once it runs (for a move: after the new Claude has acknowledged the context, which it does on the start mode with tool requests denied) the portal sends `set_permission_mode bypassPermissions` under the same one-request grant `enterBypass` uses, without asking for the tick or the server code again (the person gave both for this chat). High audit `bypass.entered` with `how: 'move' \| 'restart'`, actor `portal`; transcript "Bypass kept after the move." If the switch fails, the restart approach once (stop, relaunch with the flag, ask again); failing again, the session stays on the start mode with "Bypass not kept after the move: Claude Code refused the switch (reason)." and `bypass.left` `why: 'move_failed' \| 'restart_failed'`. Policy off at that moment: no flag, "Bypass not kept after the move: the policy no longer allows it.", `bypass.left` `why: 'policy'`. One place does this (`#keepBypass`), in one form, "Bypass kept after X." or "Bypass not kept after X: why.", for the Move, the auto move engine and the supervisor's `/switch` (all through `switchClaude`), a Restart and T-136's recover in place (`how: 'recover'`). A move that doesn't finish leaves the old run in Bypass, as it was (the row is restored whole). **Unchanged (FR20's reason: nobody is looking):** a wake from sleep, a crash and Resume, or any other relaunch the person didn't ask for still drops Bypass. The policy turning Bypass off still wins at once, also while the switch is in flight (the session is switched back out, or its process stopped). An automatic move (autotransfer rules the person set) goes through the same Move, so it keeps Bypass too: the session was already running unattended in it, and the policy check still applies (decision SR, 2026-10-13). **Observed (2026-10-13, T-256, the Event chat `e1235d03`, from its raw streams):** the move at 18:31 UTC kept Bypass (the new run's first segment: the ack turn on the start mode, then `set_permission_mode bypassPermissions` answered `{mode: bypassPermissions}`). Nobody spoke after the move, so the chat sat idle and slept ("Bypass turned off after sleep."); at 19:33:55 the supervisor's continue message woke it, a `--resume` of the same run on a new segment on the start mode `default`, as a wake must; its first command asked for an approval at 19:33:58 and waited until 00:22, when the coordinator entered the server code and turned Bypass on again. So no move path drops Bypass; a wake does, by this rule. The supervisor now re-asserts Bypass on its rounds through the person's gates (the server code, the acknowledgement); whether a wake should keep Bypass in a capable session under the policy is the owner's decision (T-256), not changed here. | **Built** (tests: policy flipping mid-switch, keep on restart, policy off, retry then `restart_failed`, move keeps it on a Claude with its own directory and credential, move with policy off, retry and `move_failed`, rollback)                                                                                                         |
| FR17a | **Auto.** The picker lists Plan, Manual, Edit, Auto (and Bypass, its own row, only where the policy allows it); `dontAsk` stays valid for stored rows but isn't offered. Auto's hint: "A classifier approves routine actions and blocks risky ones." **Who may turn it on:** only a person in the app (the socket passes actor `ui`; any other caller of `control` (triggers, Portal MCP, the policy) is refused) and only in a session a person started in the app (`autoModeAllowed`: `startedBy = 'ui'`, no parent). Sessions started by a trigger or webhook, a ticket, Portal MCP or another session keep today's ceiling (Edit), even for a person: their opening text was never vetted. Now superseded in part: D-103, see §21.4. Refusals are audited (`mode.refused`, medium); turning it on is audited (`mode.auto`, medium). A refusal from Claude Code (`auto_mode_model`, `auto_mode_settings`) keeps the mode and shows its message. A resume puts Auto back like any mode; refused then, the session reads Manual and says "Couldn't restore the mode". A classifier denial shows as one transcript line, "Auto blocked Bash: [Data Exfiltration]" (tool and rule only, never the input).
