### 21.3 Not done or not verified

- The full path through a real portal and a Docker workspace (restart of a live session with a newly delivered MCP set) was not run end to end; the CLI behaviour it relies on was run directly (21.1) and the controller against the fake host.
- T-102's queued and automatic restart against a real Claude Code process and a real delivery: not run end to end; the controller is tested against the fake host (`restartQueue.test.ts`) and the hint in a browser against the scripted socket.
- T-472: not observed on a live workspace yet. That a running process takes a context delivery through the drop-in stamp rests on the observation in persona-pool §11 (2.1.296). That Claude Code honours `paths` in a managed rule (`/etc/claude-code/.claude/rules/`) the way it does in a project rule is not verified. The restarts per hour after the change are still to be measured (T-472 item 4).
- Auto with a real account and the real classifier: not run (no real credentials); its eligibility by account or plan is only what the binary's messages say.

### 21.4 Autonomy modes for agents (D-103, PILOT-996, 2026-10-28)

The owner's decision replaces FR17a's "who may turn Auto on" and D-47's worker-mode clause. Built in PILOT-997 (T-2864):

- **Auto is allowed** (`autoModeAllowed`) for a session a person started in the app, for a worker whose parent runs in Auto or Bypass, and for any session while the policy `sessions.autonomousAuto` is on (Settings › Sessions › Auto for agents, ON by default). Any caller of `control` may ask for it (the app, Portal MCP, a lead for its worker). A refusal says why and what to do, and is still audited `mode.refused`; Auto is audited `mode.auto`.
- **A new session with no person behind it** (a trigger, a ticket, Portal MCP) starts in Auto while the policy is on (`mode.auto` with `how: 'start'`, actor `portal`). A person's own session starts as before.
- **A worker's mode** (`session_spawn`, the QA worker; T-2997, PILOT-1025, D-104 supersedes the never-wider and Manual-hands-on-dontAsk rules for Auto only) is Bypass from a parent in Bypass now (below) when none or Auto is asked (owner, T-2997 comment 50003: "if bypass is on lead, should bypass": the parent's Bypass wins over a requested Auto); else the one asked for (a narrower plan, dontAsk, default or acceptEdits asked by name is kept); unasked, Auto while `sessions.autonomousAuto` is on. With that policy off, the parent's mode as before: a Manual parent hands on dontAsk (a worker never waits on a person, D-62), no parent gives Auto. Auto asked by name is allowed whatever the parent's mode while the policy is on; every other ask, and Auto with the policy off, is never wider than the parent (plan < dontAsk < Manual < Edit < Auto < Bypass): a wider ask is refused with the reason before anything starts. `session_spawn` offers plan, default, dontAsk, acceptEdits and auto; Bypass by name is refused.
- **A Bypass lead's worker keeps Bypass** when the policy allows Bypass and the lead is in Bypass NOW (`bypassNow`: live, capable, its row in Bypass, the mode the portal set or confirmed for its current process is Bypass with no entry pending, no leave in flight, its newest Bypass record an entry; T-2997: the stored mode alone once handed a stale Bypass on, and the worker was then ended for integrity): the worker is made capable and enters through `#keepBypass('inherit')`, which asks the same live check again (audit `bypass.capable` and `bypass.entered`, `how: 'inherit'`). A parent whose row says Bypass without that gives Auto, and the spawn result says why (`bypass`: "Bypass not inherited: the Bypass policy is off" or "you are not in Bypass now as the portal sees it"); a keep that fails after the check gives Auto where the worker may run in it, else dontAsk, and the spawn result says so. No other Bypass gate changes.
- **An inherited Bypass never outlives the parent's** (review 42957). When a session leaves Bypass for good (a person's or a token's switch, a sleep or stop that did not keep it, its end, a downgrade, a gate's refusal, a failed keep), each live worker in an inherited Bypass leaves it too, to dontAsk, audited `bypass.left` `why: 'parent_left'` with the parent, and so on down to their workers. A pause that comes back (a kept sleep or vanishing) and a restart or move about to put it back do not cascade; the policy takes every session out itself. A session whose capability was inherited (`bypass.capable` `how: 'inherit'`) gets Bypass back on any path (a move, a restart, a recovery, Keep going) only while its parent is in Bypass now; otherwise `bypass.left` `why: 'parent_not_bypass'`. A lead in a kept pause that is then ended or stopped for good (its kept record superseded) takes its workers out too (review 43296); a lead's own restart that keeps Bypass (its row on the start mode for the moment, no kept record) does not, nor the restart a keep falls back on (review 43368). Not covered yet: a lead that vanished with Bypass kept and never runs or ends again (its workspace gone) leaves its worker in Bypass until the worker's own next restart, move or recovery; there is no hook for "gone for good" today (follow-up).
- **Portal MCP** (`session_start`, `session_configure`) accepts auto. Bypass is never set directly (PILOT-1000): `session_configure` with `mode: bypassPermissions` (alone, with an optional `reason`) files a `session.bypass` approval card for that session and returns the request; nothing about the session changes in the call, and no acknowledgement is taken from the caller (PILOT-996 c42754). Allow goes through `enterBypass` (operate, fresh server code, policy, capable); see [approvals](approvals.md). `session_start` refuses Bypass with the reason (a session started over MCP is not bypass-capable).
- **Keep going** puts Bypass back (act `rebypass`, trail `rebypassed`) on a chat that is out of it, capable, live, and LOST it within 24 hours (`bypassBackHours`; the newest `bypass.left` is a sleep, a stop, a vanished process, a restart, or a move or restart that failed to keep it), through the same gated path. A Bypass someone took away on purpose (a person's or a token's mode switch, now audited `why: 'mode'` with its actor; the policy; a gate's refusal; a downgrade) is never put back and no card is allowed; the cards raised meanwhile are allowed except AskUserQuestion and ExitPlanMode. Past 24 hours, or refused, it is reported as before.
- Not verified: a real lead and worker pair on a live workspace; the server tests cover it against the fake host (`chat/workerModes.test.ts`, `keepgoing/engine.test.ts`, `portalmcp/tools.test.ts`).

## 22. Sub-agents: whose line is whose, their models, a detail view (2026-10-13, T-5 and T-31)

The owner's asks: every sub-agent row says which model it runs on and opens to what the agent did, with Stop (T-5); and a sub-agent's text must
never stream in the main reply's place, nor the session read plain Idle while its agents still work (T-31).

### 22.1 Where the facts come from (Claude Code 2.1.296 against the stand-in)

- **Whose line.** Every stream line of a sub-agent carries `parent_tool_use_id`: the id of the `Task`/`Agent` call that started it. Stored events and
  live deltas carry it as `parentToolUseId` (null for the main agent), beside the older `agentLabel`.
- **Model.** Reliable in two places: the call's launch result (`tool_use_result.resolvedModel`, also when the call names no model) and each of the
  agent's own lines (`message.model`, which wins). The call's `model` argument is only a request (an alias, or absent: it inherits) and is never
  used. `ChatTask.model` (migration 382) is null until one of them is seen, and the UI then says "unavailable", never a guess.
- **Streaming.** Claude Code doesn't stream a sub-agent's text: its finished blocks arrive one by one; a delta of an agent is still kept apart.

### 22.2 Requirements and how they are built

| ID   | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Status                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ---- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| FR54 | **The reply slot is the main agent's.** The live state is split (`motion/deltas.ts`, `foldLive`/`settleLive`): the main agent's reply, and each agent's own live text by its call; an agent's stored block ends only its own live text.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | ✅ Built, pure tests (`agentFold.test.ts`: a background agent's deltas after the main result; two agents interleaved; a main reply while an agent streams).                                                                           |
| FR55 | **Agent lines fold into the turn that started them** (`agentFold.ts`, `buildItems`): every row whose call is in the transcript leaves the flow, however late it arrives (a background agent after the main turn ended); an agent started by an agent belongs to the one the main agent started. The call's row is the **agent row**: icon, name, model, latest line (shimmering while it runs; its result once done), opening the detail view. While one runs, the turn's activity line names it and what it does.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | ✅ Built, pure tests.                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| FR56 | **Working while tasks run** (T-41, which replaced T-31's "Idle · N agents"). One server definition: a session is working while its main turn runs OR any task of its current process runs (sub-agents, and background commands it started and has not seen finish), and idle only when neither does. The controller presents it (`state: working`, `tasksOnly: true` when only tasks keep it working; the stored state stays the turn's, which sends, restarts and moves go by) to tabs, the Sessions list (sorted as working) and the notifier; a change of the running count is news to tabs. The header and the Sessions row read "Working · 2 agents" with a spinner ("Working" on a phone); with `tasksOnly` the composer offers Send, not Stop. **`session_done`** fires only on the move to idle, after 20 s with no new turn and no running task, once per move, never while tasks run, mid-move or for an ended session; "minutes" is from the first working moment of the stretch (not a turn's `result`). | ✅ Built; server tests (`chat/workingTasks.test.ts`, `notify/adapters.test.ts`: a result while tasks run is no `session_done`; the last task ending emits it once; a new turn within the debounce cancels it; not mid-move or ended). |
| FR57 | **Model label** on each Agents-panel row (sonnet, opus, fable, haiku; the id as reported for another; "unavailable" until reported; none for a background command).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | ✅ Built.                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| FR58 | **Detail view** (the Agents panel on wide screens, the sheet on a phone; a row of the panel or of the transcript opens it): description, type, model, status, tokens, tool uses, time, result, and its own transcript (`GET …/tasks/:taskId/events?after=`, redacted as stored, the newest 200 with `truncated`, behind the session's view permission), polled every 2 s while it runs, newest at the bottom; Stop while it runs; back returns to the list.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | ✅ Built.                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| FR59 | **A task never outlives its process** (T-39). When a process ends (a Move, a restart, sleep, the end, a crash, a fresh launch), every task it still had running reads `killed` with its end time; a Move ends only the old run's (the new run's own keep running) and an undone move ends nothing; at a portal start, tasks of a session whose process isn't live are closed the same way. The Agents panel's header counts sub-agents and background commands apart ("2 agents, 3 commands · 4 done"; since T-149 the filter chips show the counts and this line is the panel's status for a screen reader).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | ✅ Built; server tests (`chat/taskEnd.test.ts`: Move, undone Move, restart, a launch after a stale sleep, the portal start) and a pure test of the count.                                                                             |

### 22.3 Checked in a browser

`tools/qa/subagents.mjs` against a scratch portal whose local workspace runs the real Claude Code with the stand-in: `[[agent sonnet,fable,- steps 6 2 <file>]]`
starts three background agents that work about 12 s after the main turn is done, at 390 px (touch, safe areas) and 1280 px, light and dark.
Observed (2026-10-13): 390 px light and dark 72/72 checks except the Sessions row, which then looked for the agents on the Idle chip
(the session counts as Working since T-41; the harness now picks the Working chip); 1280 px light and dark passed 116/116 before T-41's
wording, and light again after it through the detail view (opened from the panel and from the transcript's agent row). The page showed:
no agent text in the reply slot; the activity line naming the running agent ("QA agent 2 (fable) · Read qa-notes.txt"); "Working · 3
agents" (phone: "Working") with Send, not Stop; models sonnet, fable, and opus for the agent with no model argument (it inherited the
session's); Stop ending one agent ("Stopped"). Not re-run after T-41: the 1280 px Sessions row and 1280 px dark (paused for a deploy gate).

### 22.4 The Agents panel's list (2026-10-14, T-149)

The owner's screenshot (an orchestrator chat with 4 agents, 13 commands and 80 done): long text made the panel bloated, and finding the
other agents took a lot of scrolling. **Observed** (Claude Code 2.1.296 against the stand-in): a background command's task is named by
the call's own `description` when Claude gave one, else by its whole command line (the portal keeps 200 characters); its summary is
`Background command "<that name>" completed (exit code 0)` or `failed with exit code N`, and a code Claude Code does not count as a failure
reads `completed (exit code 1: No matches found)` (a grep that matched nothing); its output goes to a file in the workspace that the portal
does not read; Claude Code drops a leading `cd <the session's folder> &&` from the command it reports.

| ID   | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Status                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ---- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| FR75 | **Short labels** (`commandLabel.ts`, on the transcript's shell tokenizer): a sub-agent by its description; a command by its own description, else by the first command that does the work (past `cd x &&`, env prefixes, `timeout`, `flock`, `nice`, a pipe's later stages, heredoc bodies, `bash -c`) and its first meaningful argument, a long path's middle elided (`node test: imageBuild.test.ts`, `git push origin`, `/workspaces/…/coordinator/ph.sh GET`), never the whole command line. A label is built only from what the portal stored redacted, and never takes a flag's value, an assignment, `user:password` or a credential-shaped word; disguising characters show as `⟦U+202E⟧`. | ✅ Built; a table test with real command lines from this repository's sessions (pipes, heredocs, `cd x &&`, env prefixes) and a test that a password in a command never reaches the label. |
| FR76 | **Rows of one height** (60 px, two lines; the list holds still while lines change): a state dot, the label, a sub-agent's model and the time; then one line cut with an ellipsis: what a running agent does now, a finished one's result, a failure in red, "Stopped"; a command's exit code (with Claude Code's reason when it gives one) and its command line in the code font. Nothing the label says is printed again. Stop sits on each running row (44 px on a phone); Stop all says what it stops.                                                                                                                                                                                          | ✅ Built; pure tests (`agentList.test.ts`); the harness measures every row's box before and after a live update.                                                                           |
| FR77 | **Groups, chips, search**: sticky headers with counts: running agents, running commands, failed or stopped (never folded away), done (folded, newest first, ten more per Show more). Chips count and filter (Running, Failed, Done, Agents, Commands; one status and one kind at a time; icon and count, the chosen one named); a search field from 640 px, behind a button on a phone. Filters, search and the fold survive a visit to a detail view, and back returns to the same scroll position.                                                                                                                                                                                               | ✅ Built; pure tests (counts equal the data, a failed task is never in the done group under any filter, a task listed twice shows once, search).                                           |
| FR78 | **A command's detail**: its label, status, the whole command in the shared code block (shell colours, wrap, copy), exit code, time and start; the output reads "Unavailable" with a note (it stays in the workspace). The transcript publishes the calls behind the tasks and each agent's own rows (`taskFeed.ts`), so the panel shows the whole command and an agent's latest line without the transcript rendering again. An agent's detail names the ticket its description gives (T-n, a link) and, while it runs, the tool calls its own transcript shows so far.                                                                                                                            | ✅ Built.                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Not built yet (the rest of T-149): agents nested under their parent (a task does not carry its parent), the two-pane layout on a laptop,
the compact density and its toggle, the Home widget's rows (T-125), a mini bar of recent tool activity, tokens and cost while an agent
runs (Claude Code reports them when it ends), a command's output tail (the server would have to read the task's output file).

**Checked in a browser** (2026-10-14): `tools/qa/subagents.mjs` now starts three background commands before the agents (`[[bg …]]`: one
described, one named by its long command line, one that fails) and checks the list while the agents run and after: one row height, rows
that hold still (boxes before and after a live update), no label with `&&`, `|` or `2>&1`, Agents first with its count, Failed open,
Done folded, the failing command's exit code on its second line, every running agent on the first screen, the Agents and Done chips,
labels (`QA described`, `node test: qa-none.test.mjs`), the command's detail (the whole command, exit code, copy), back keeping the filter,
search (a field on 1280 px, behind its button on a phone), and Stop on a running command's row on a session of its own. Before the
change: 135/138 (the three misses were the first run's timing: a cold page compile let the agents finish before the detail opened);
after: 233/233 at 390 px (touch, safe areas) and 1280 px, light and dark, in four runs in a row of the final harness; one run before its last fix missed an agent's brief "Step k." line (231/233), which the harness now also reads from the activity row. Not built: a journey with 80 done and 300 rows on the phone profile. **Seen once, not reproduced in the eight full runs after it:**
a harness session ended for integrity in a run where a background command was stopped (Claude Code replayed a user line with origin
`task-notification` and no `session-task` producer); the harness now stops a command on a session of its own and checks that it lives on.

## 23. Keep going (the watchdog) (2026-10-14, T-259 slices 1, 2 and 3; T-104, T-108 item 1, T-257)

The supervisor (`docs/dev-sessions/supervisor.mjs`) ran on the owner's machine and stopped for hours when its private network dropped
(2026-10-14 00:15 UTC: nothing nudged, woke, answered or reported any chat for about 3.5 hours). Keep going is that watchdog INSIDE the
portal: a per-chat policy and a deterministic sweep in the server (`apps/server/src/keepgoing/`), with no person, no owner code and no
process outside the portal. Slice 1 keeps a chat going and says what it may not repair; slice 2 brings an ended chat back and shows the
watched chats on Home. Neither ever repairs Bypass (the lease is the owner's open decision, T-256).

### 23.1 Requirements and how they are built

| ID   | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Status                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| FR60 | **A per-chat policy** `keepGoing`: on/off (off by default), nudge after N idle minutes (4), the continuation prompt (the supervisor's CONTINUE text; `{status}` is the policy's status file, default `docs/dev-sessions/status/orchestrator.md`; `{ticket}` the chat's ticket), caps per 24 h (60 nudges, 20 wakes). Stored in SQLite (migration 790). `GET`/`PUT /api/chat/sessions/:id/keep-going` (view / operate on the chat through the seam, declared in `keepgoing/authz.ts`; a person or an admin token; an agent, its own chat or another, and a token below admin are refused; audited `keepgoing.set`). The session payload carries it (`ChatSession.keepGoing`: the policy, today's nudges and wakes, the last action, `paused`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | ✅ Built; server tests (`keepgoing/routes.test.ts`, `keepgoing/controller.test.ts`, `keepgoing/store.test.ts`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| FR61 | **The kill switch** `keepgoing.enabled` (Settings, group "Keep going", on by default: nothing runs until a chat's policy is on). Off: no round does anything; the policies stay.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | ✅ Built; test.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| FR62 | **The sweep**: every 60 s, the chats whose policy is on and that are due (next look stored, +0-15 s jitter), at most 100, one decision each (`keepgoing/decide.ts`, pure): ended → reported (a person's close turns the policy off instead); a move, restart or launch under way → nothing; a QUESTION (`question`, `elicitation`) → denied with "decide yourself by the written defaults"; a TOOL, plan or workflow APPROVAL → never answered, reported once after 10 minutes; out of Bypass on a bypass-capable chat → reported once, never re-entered; interrupted, or asleep with a nudge due → woken (`control resume`), and the next round nudges at once; idle (T-41: no turn, no task, nothing waiting) for N minutes → the prompt at priority `later`, as `portal:keepgoing`, once per idle period, at least 10 minutes apart. A failed action backs off 15 minutes; a failed wake is reported once. Restart-safe: the next look, the idle period nudged, a continue owed after a wake, the back-off and the reports are rows. It acts for the chat's owner and re-checks each time that the owner is enabled and may operate the chat.     | ✅ Built; server tests on a fake clock (`keepgoing/engine.test.ts`: not before N minutes, once per idle period, an approval never answered and reported once, a question answered, wake then nudge, asleep, caps, a failed wake, the kill switch, a portal restart in the middle, ended once and down as a comment, a person's close, out of Bypass, an owner who may no longer operate, a busy chat). |
| FR63 | **Reports**: one notification `keepgoing_attention` ("Keep going", body "<workspace> · <what> · T-n", urgency high, Web Push, on by default; migration 791 adds it to every destination that hears `session_failed`) and ONE ticket per chat and cause (type bug, priority high, tag `watchdog`, title "<chat title>: <what> since HH:MM UTC", the evidence: state, process, mode, what waits by tool name and the portal's own title, the ended reason redacted, why it is left down, the last trail entry; never transcript text, never a command). Not repeated while the cause stands; when it comes back its open ticket gets a comment. Since slice 2 an ending is a QUIET mark (`ended`: no ticket, no push, the chat is brought back, FR66) and the one loud report for it is `down` ("left down"), at once when the cap, a refused or a failed start keeps it down (T-257 item 1), or when it is still ended 15 minutes after the sweep first saw it (T-257 item 4). The chat's title is never in the notification text (agents write titles): it travels as the untrusted detail, shown only where a destination turned on Include detail. | ✅ Built; tests (`keepgoing/engine.test.ts`, `keepgoing/restart.test.ts`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| FR64 | **The trail**: every action (nudge, wake, answer, report, failure, cap, stand-down, a person's change) is one `keepgoing_events` row and one platform audit row (`keepgoing.*`) with a fixed sentence; nothing is written into the transcript except the nudge itself (a portal message).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | ✅ Built.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| FR65 | **The chat shows it**: Session info has a "Keep going" switch (the explanation in its InfoTip), today's nudges and wakes and the last action; the session header shows a 44 px icon-only chip while it is on, which opens Session info.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | ✅ Built; browser check below.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

| FR66 | **An ended chat is brought back** (slice 2; T-257 items 2 and 3). A person's close, an archive or the chat's ticket closing (done) turn the policy off instead. An INTEGRITY ending is first recovered in place (T-136, `recover(id, { by: 'agent' })` as `portal:keepgoing`: the portal's own refusals, day cap and pause apply; the pause means "next round"; a refusal is one `recover_refused` row). A refusal, or any other ending, starts a NEW chat that continues it (`continuedFrom`, T-270: its unfinished tickets move with it), in the same workspace, folder, Claude, title and ticket, whose first message is the continuation brief (which chat it continues, why that one ended, redacted, one line; that its sub-agents are gone; then the policy's prompt with `{status}` and `{ticket}` filled). The policy goes with it (the ended chat's turns off); the ended chat's reports clear and its open ticket gets "Started again: the chat <id> continues it". **Caps per root cause over the lineage** (`continuedFrom` back, 24 h, read from the trail, so a portal restart keeps them): after a harmless integrity ending 12 (its own ceiling, never counted against the chat), after a suspicious one 3, after any other ending 6 (the two count together); two starts at least 5 minutes apart; capped: left down (FR63). A refused or failed start backs off 15 minutes and is reported left down at once. **Bypass**: a chat started again is never bypass-capable (FR20: only a person's opt-in) and starts in the default mode; the trail says so ("Bypass is a person's choice and is not carried"). A recover in place keeps Bypass as a Restart does (§10.4). | ✅ Built; server tests (`keepgoing/restart.test.ts`: recover first, refused then a new chat with the brief and the policy carried, the pause then down after 15 minutes, a failed start reported at once then retried, the cap per cause, the gap, the cap across a portal restart, a closed ticket and an archive, a brief that did not go through; `keepgoing/controller.test.ts`: the real controller makes the continuation, not bypass-capable). |
| FR67 | **`session_keepgoing_set`** on the Portal MCP: scope `admin` (an agent's token never reaches it), session control on, declared in `keepgoing/authz.ts` (operate on the session, confirmed, audited `keepgoing.set`); a chat the token's user may not operate, or an unknown one, is NOT_FOUND; turning it ON returns a confirmation first (the preview says what the portal will then do by itself); off or a change of a number does not. | FR68 | **The Home card "Autonomy"** (and its glance chip, off in the strips by default): `GET /api/keepgoing` (`self`: only the chats the caller may view; an agent its own at most) lists the chats with Keep going on, the ones with a cause standing first, then by their last action. The card shows Watched, Down, Reported and Today (nudges and wakes in 24 h) from that answer, then one 44 px row per chat (its title, cut; its state and what stands or the last action; how long ago), opening the chat; "Paused" when the kill switch is off; "No chat has Keep going on." when none. The explanation is the card's InfoTip. It is loaded again on the live topic `keepgoing`, which the sweep publishes on every action and when a watched chat's state changed between two looks. The chip: the number watched, red while one is left down, amber for another report or the pause. | ✅ Built; web tests (`glance/widgets/autonomy/model.test.ts`), server test (`keepgoing/routes.test.ts`), browser check below. |
| FR69 | **The supervisor steps back** (`docs/dev-sessions/supervisor.mjs`): a track whose newest chat has Keep going on (and not paused) is the portal's: the script neither nudges, wakes, recovers nor starts it; it still re-asserts Bypass on it as before (the lease is not built). | ✅ Built; `node --check`. |
| FR70 | **The program pause switch** (slice 3, the owner's order 2026-10-14: Konfersi and Event paused). A policy may carry a **program** (a slug, `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$`, the supervisor's track keys `orchestrator`, `konfersi`, `event`; carried to a continuation chat). A **pause** is one row of `keepgoing_pauses` (migration 792, with the `program` column): a program, or all (`*`), a person's one-line reason, since when and who. A paused chat (all, or its program) is still looked at every minute (its state, the reports that clear, the Home card) but nothing is done: no nudge, wake, answer, recover, new chat, ticket or push; turning itself off (a person's close, an archive, its ticket closed) still happens. The quiet `ended` mark moves with the clock while paused, so "left down" counts 15 minutes from the resume, not from the pause. **Resume** makes the program's chats due and runs a round at once: an idle chat gets the policy's prompt (its `{status}` and `{ticket}`), an ended one is recovered or continued with the continuation brief. A back-off after a failure stands. Who: a platform admin as a person (`PUT /api/keepgoing/pauses`, declared `settings` `manage`) or an admin token of a platform admin (`keepgoing_pause_set`, confirmed first, both directions); an agent, a token below admin and a user who is not an admin get NOT_FOUND. One audit row `keepgoing.pause` per change (the program, paused or not, the reason's length; never the text). The session payload says `paused` with the `pause` that holds it; `GET /api/keepgoing` lists the pauses. The program is set on a chat through the PUT route or `session_keepgoing_set` (`program`), not yet in Session info. | ✅ Built; `keepgoing/pause.test.ts` (engine, route, tool). |
| FR71 | **The Autonomy card shows and sets the pause**: a row per pause (all first: "Paused · All" or "Paused · <program>", the reason, how long ago), each with a 44 x 44 px Resume; a 44 px Pause (icon only on a phone) opens two fields (the program, empty for all; the reason) and "Pause <program>"/"Pause all"; the InfoTip says what a pause holds. The chip turns amber and names the paused programs. Session info says "Paused (<program>): <reason>". | ✅ Built; web test (`autonomy/model.test.ts`) and the browser check below. |
| FR72 | **The supervisor reads the portal's pauses** (one direction: the portal is the source of truth from 2026-10-14): each round it reads `GET /api/keepgoing`; a pause of all, or of a track's key, leaves that track alone exactly like its `PAUSE-<track>` file (which still works, as a local override; the portal never reads the files). | ✅ Built; `node --check`. |
| FR73 | **A chat left down is escalated** (T-257 items 1 and 4): next to the `down` ticket (priority high) and the `keepgoing_attention` notification (urgency high), it is a **Needs you** item `keepgoing_down` ([attention.md](attention.md) §2) until it runs again, and the **coordinator's chat** is told once per report: the chat the setting `keepgoing.coordinator` names (a session id, Settings group "Keep going"), else the chat's parent. A message at priority later, as the portal, with ids and fixed words only (never the chat's title); only to a coordinator chat that is not ended and that the policy's owner may operate; one `escalated` trail and audit row (`keepgoing.escalated`); a send that fails is a `failed` row. | ✅ Built; server tests (`keepgoing/escalate.test.ts`: 15 minutes still ended gives one ticket, one notification, one Needs you item, one message to the coordinator, nothing repeated, all cleared when it runs; the setting wins over the parent; an ended coordinator or none is not told; a harmless ending after the integrity cap is still recovered in place; `attention/attention.test.ts`). |
| FR74 | **Programs in the UI** (slice 3): Session info has a **Program** field under the Keep going switch while it is on (44 px, 16 px text on a phone, an InfoTip; lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, empty is none; saved on Enter or when left, `PUT …/keep-going { program }`). The chat list has a **Program** picker beside Group by (icon only, 44 x 44 px, the native list: All programs, then each program with its count; shown only when a chat has a program); a program narrows the chips' counts and the list. | ✅ Built; web tests (`listOrder.test.ts`); browser: `tools/qa/session-list.mjs` and `tools/qa/keepgoing.mjs`. |
| FR80 | **A drained chat is not nudged** (T-3189; a drained lead got 60 identical nudges in 24 h, hit the cap and a "left for a person" ticket was filed, T-3187). When a nudge (or the wake of a sleeping chat before one) is due, Keep going skips it when ALL hold: the chat **holds tickets** (assigned to it as a session) and none is open or in progress (all in review, blocked or closed); **nothing changed on them since the chat's last activity** (the newest `updated_at` of its tickets, which a comment, a verdict, a check, a status change or an assignment moves, is not after the chat's `lastActivityAt`); no sub-agent or background task runs; and no worker it started has not ended. A skip sends nothing and is not counted toward `maxNudgesPerDay` or `maxWakesPerDay`; one `drained` trail row and one `keepgoing.drained` audit row (info) per idle period say so, not one per round. The next round after a change lands on one of its tickets (or a ticket is newly assigned to it, or one is open or in progress, or a worker runs) nudges it as before. **A chat that never held a ticket keeps today's behaviour** (nudged); so do an interrupted chat (woken) and a continue owed after a wake. Anything unknown (no ticket or worker port, a failing read) counts as not drained. Restart, recover, the Bypass put-back and the caps' values are unchanged. | ✅ Built; server tests (`keepgoing/drained.test.ts`: all in review or blocked gives no nudge in 6 hours, no cap spent, one drained row per idle period; a comment, a status change, a new assignment nudges at the next round; an open ticket or a live worker is nudged, an ended worker does not count; no tickets keeps the old nudges and cap; a sleeping drained chat is not woken, an interrupted one is; `tickets/heldBy.test.ts`). |

### 23.2 What is still open

The Bypass LEASE (T-104 item 3: re-entering Bypass after a wake or a restart under a time-boxed grant the owner gives once with the server
code; whether a wake keeps Bypass is the owner's call on T-256): until then Keep going suits a chat that may come back in the default mode
after a NEW chat (a recover in place keeps Bypass); a track that must stay in Bypass across a new chat stays with the supervisor (Keep going
off for it). Also open: agents' runner and pipeline triggers for a paused program (the coordinator's item; CI does not know programs); the status
file marker as a done condition (needs a file read in the workspace; a closed ticket is one already); the move by D-12/D-39 from the sweep;
editing the prompt, the caps and N in the UI (the API and the MCP tool take them); a portal setting for the number of sub-agents (D-43);
the portal's own external heartbeat (designed in §23.3, T-315, not built); deleting `supervisor.mjs` (after the lease).

**Needs you (T-146):** a tool request a waiting session has held for ten minutes or more is listed on Needs you ([attention.md](attention.md) §2, `chat/attention.ts`); answering stays on the session page.

### 23.3 The external heartbeat (design for a decision, T-315; slice 1 below is built, the rest is not)

**Built, slice 1 (T-315, decided under D-51: no owner question, no new account, no new secret).** A scheduled GitHub Actions workflow,
`.github/workflows/heartbeat.yml` (every 5 minutes and `workflow_dispatch`; permissions `issues: write`, `contents: read`; only the
workflow's own `GITHUB_TOKEN`), runs `scripts/heartbeat.mjs` (Node 22, no dependencies) against `https://pilot.ai.isnan.me/healthz`. A miss is
anything but a 200 within 10 s. **The miss count is kept in the run, not stored:** a run that misses waits 60 s and probes once more; two
misses in that one run are "two in a row", so there is no cache or counter to go stale, and the alarm comes after about 1 to 6 minutes. Two
misses open ONE issue labelled `portal-down` (never a second while one with the label is open: the check reads the open issues first); a
healthy answer while one is open comments and closes it. The owner is told by GitHub's own notifications. Probe B is not in slice 1.
**Blind is not down.** A name that does not resolve or a connect that cannot route (`ENOTFOUND`, `EAI_AGAIN`, `ENETUNREACH`, `EHOSTUNREACH`)
means the runner cannot see the portal, which says nothing about it: no `portal-down` issue; ONE issue labelled `heartbeat-blind` instead
(never a second while one is open; the log names the error class only, no headers or bodies). Only a resolved host that answers non-2xx or
times out twice is a miss. A probe that reaches the portal (an answer, or a miss on a resolved host) closes an open blind issue with a comment.
Proof: `apps/server/src/qa/heartbeat.test.ts` runs the script against a fake health endpoint and a fake GitHub API: one miss opens nothing; two
open exactly one; a third run opens no second; recovery comments and closes; an unresolvable name opens one `heartbeat-blind` and no
`portal-down`, no second, and closes when the portal is seen; a 503 pair closes a blind issue and opens `portal-down`; `--dry-run` reads and writes nothing.

**Not observed, and plainly limited.** (1) A schedule runs only from the default branch: the heartbeat does not start until the workflow is on
`main`. (2) GitHub's runners are outside the cluster and `pilot.ai.isnan.me` resolves only on the private network (T-259), so the runners
probably cannot reach it. That has not been run; the expected result is one `heartbeat-blind` issue, not a false alarm. If the first runs show
it, the next slice moves the probe to a host that can reach the portal, still without a new secret.

**Why.** Keep going runs inside the portal (§23, `apps/server/src/keepgoing/`), so it cannot see the portal go away, and it cannot see the
outage of 2026-10-14 either: then the portal was not the thing that broke; `pilot.ai.isnan.me` did not resolve from the owner's dev
container and the k3s API timed out (T-259; `docs/dev-session.md:181`). What exists today, checked in the repository:

- `GET /healthz` answers `{ ok: true, service: 'portal' }` without sign-in, before any route (`apps/server/src/app.ts:265`). It is a constant:
  it says the process serves HTTP, not that the database opens or the portal is unlocked.
- Kubernetes probes it: readiness every 10 s, liveness every 30 s, restart after 5 failures (`deploy/k3s/portal.yaml:96-107`). That
  restarts a hung pod; it tells nobody.
- The only route in is the Traefik `IngressRoute` for `Host(\`pilot.ai.isnan.me\`)` (`deploy/k3s/portal.yaml:149-164`), a name that resolves
  only on the cluster's private network; public resolvers answer NXDOMAIN (T-259). **An outside uptime service therefore cannot probe the
  portal** without a new inbound path, which this design refuses.
- The portal already sends outbound HTTPS through one guarded client, `notify/egress.ts` (https only, no redirect, private and metadata
  addresses refused with the name checked at connect time, 5 s to connect, 10 s in all; `apps/server/src/notify/egress.ts:1-12`, the
  `Egress` interface at `:51-55`). Channel URLs are sealed and never returned (`packages/contracts/src/notify.ts:298`).
- Every notification channel (Web Push, webhook, Discord, Slack) is sent BY the portal, so none of them can report the portal's own death.
  The kind `portal` ("Portal", urgent, `phase: 'later'`, `packages/contracts/src/notify.ts:88`) is declared and never sent.
- The only heartbeats built are Connect's, between the hub and a dialled-in agent (`packages/contracts/src/connect.ts:145-147`); they
  watch remote hosts, not the portal.
- The relay (`docs/dev-session.md:173-206`) is the manual stand-in and says this heartbeat is not built (`:205`).

**Two failures, two probes.** "The portal is down" and "the owner cannot reach the portal" are different, and T-259 was the second.

|                | A. Check-in (dead man's switch)                                                                             | B. Reach probe on the owner's side                                                                                                                 |
| -------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Where it runs  | In the portal, outbound only, to an outside check-in service                                                | On the coordinator's machine (where the supervisor runs, D-14), outside the cluster                                                                |
| What it proves | The portal process lives, its database answers, it is not Locked, and the cluster still has a way out       | From the owner's network: the name resolves, the usual path (`tools/coordinator/up.sh`, `tunnel 1080 up`) is up, TLS holds, `/healthz` answers 200 |
| How often      | One check-in every 60 s                                                                                     | One probe every 60 s                                                                                                                               |
| Down           | The service saw no check-in for 60 s + a 120 s grace (two missed)                                           | Two probes in a row failed (10 s limit each); the report names the stage: dns, tunnel, tls or http                                                 |
| Catches        | Process dead or hung, pod not scheduled, node or cluster down, database broken, Locked, cluster uplink down | The T-259 case, a broken tunnel or Cloudflare Access login, an expired certificate, and everything A catches                                       |
| Misses         | The owner's own path (T-259)                                                                                | A machine that is off or asleep goes silent; A covers the portal side meanwhile                                                                    |

A is the portal's job (no person, no machine of the owner's); B is the coordinator's, as the supervisor is. A check-in is sent only when the
portal checks itself first: a `SELECT 1` on the store, not Locked, the Keep going sweep ran in the last 3 minutes (the 60-second sweep,
`apps/server/src/keepgoing/index.ts:1`; the round needs a "last ran" it can read, part of the slice). A sick portal goes quiet and the outside service raises the alarm, so one rule covers "dead" and
"alive but broken".

**Who is told, and how.** The owner, on the iPhone, through a channel that does not need the portal: the check-in service's own alerts (a
push app, a message app or e-mail, the owner's choice). B posts its failure to the SAME service (a second check that it fails explicitly), so
the owner has one place to look and one app to install. One message when down, one when up again; no repeat while it stays down. The
owner sets up: an account at the service; two checks ("portal", period 1 min, grace 2 min; "reach", the same); the alert integration to
the phone; and gives the two ping URLs: A's goes into a Kubernetes Secret made by hand, like `claudepilot-secret-key`
(`deploy/k3s/portal.yaml:122-133`), mounted read-only and named by a `PORTAL_HEARTBEAT_URL_FILE` environment variable; B's stays on the
coordinator's machine, never in the repository or a ticket. Candidates (the owner picks; the design does not depend on which): a hosted
check-in service with period and grace (Healthchecks.io-style), or one the owner runs on another network. Not the portal's Web Push, not a
portal webhook, not a workspace in the same cluster, not the Coder workstation (inside the cluster network, `docs/dev-session.md:182`).

**What it must never do.**

- Act on a chat, a workspace or a ticket. A sends one request and reads its status; B calls `/healthz` and nothing else. Neither nudges,
  restarts, recovers, moves or answers; that stays with Keep going (§23.1) and the supervisor.
- Carry a secret. The check-in body is empty (or a fixed version string); no session id, title, user, token or transcript text. B uses no
  sign-in code and no cookie (`/healthz` needs none), so it can run where the supervisor's code is not loaded. The ping URLs are
  credentials: sealed or in a Secret, never logged, never in an error, an audit row or a response (`chat/redact.ts` covers transcripts;
  the heartbeat logs a fixed code and the HTTP status only).
- Open an inbound hole: no new IngressRoute, public DNS name, port forward or tunnel to the portal.
- Call out except through the guard: A uses `notify/egress.ts` as it is (https, no redirect, public addresses only; no `allowPrivate`).
- Flap or spam: one state change, one message; a failing check-in is retried at the next minute, never in a loop.

**The smallest first slice (A only, effort S, about 3 hours).** `apps/server/src/heartbeat/`: a 60 s timer started by the server, the three
self-checks above, one POST through the notify `Egress` to the URL read from `PORTAL_HEARTBEAT_URL_FILE` (no file: off, said once in the
log). A platform audit row only on a state change (`heartbeat.ok`, `heartbeat.failing`, with the fixed code), never per minute. Tests on a
fake clock and a fake egress: a check-in every minute when healthy; none when the store throws, when Locked or when the sweep is stale;
the URL never in a log, an error or an audit row; no file, nothing sent. Docs: this section built, `docs/dev-session.md` (the relay: the
heartbeat tells the owner first), `deploy/k3s/README.md` (making the Secret). No migration, no UI, no new route. The owner's part: the
account, the check, the alert, the Secret. Slice 2: B as `tools/coordinator/heartbeat.mjs` (Node, no dependency, run by the same loop as
the supervisor, its URL from a local file). Slice 3, if wanted: a "Heartbeat" row in Settings (last check-in, state; never the URL) and the
`portal` notification kind when the portal comes back after a gap.

**Questions for the owner.**

1. Which outside service and which alert channel on the phone (a push app, Telegram, e-mail)? The account is yours; agents never hold it.
2. Do you want B, and where does it run: the coordinator's machine (it sleeps when you close it) or a small always-on box on your network?
3. A's URL in a hand-made Kubernetes Secret (recommended, no UI) or a sealed field in Settings?
4. Two misses at 60 s (alarm after about 3 minutes, as T-315 asks), or a longer grace at night to avoid waking you for a short deploy? A
   deploy restarts the pod (`/deploy`): should the check pause for it, or is one "down, up again" pair acceptable?
5. Should the Docker deployment on the Mac (`pilot.dev.isnan.me`) get its own check, or only the cluster portal?

## 24. Auto hand-off: an implementer's DONE starts QA (2026-10-15, T-548 slice 1)

Opt-in per lead (`auto_handoff`, migration 779, off by default; `ChatController.setAutoHandoff`; no UI toggle yet). Engine: `apps/server/src/chat/handoff.ts`, called from `#crewWatch` when a worker turns idle.

- **Known limit (T-548 S1b, review F3).** The trigger fires only for a _worker session_ with role `implementer`. D-47 keeps implementers as Agent-tool sub-agents until the owner decides Bypass for workers, so today it never fires for them, and function personas (`konfersi-*`) do not trigger it either. Whether to widen it or wait is the coordinator's call; nothing changes here.
- **Trigger.** A worker with role `implementer` whose latest reply has `DONE <40 lower-case hex>` alone on line 1. Anything looser (case, prefix, trailing text, a quote, a later line) starts nothing. The ticket is the worker's own (set by its spawn), never read from the text. One QA per (lead, ticket, sha).
- **QA spawn.** The lead's worker through the same `create` as `session_spawn`, so depth, the lead's max workers (and the global cap) and the hourly rate hold. Role `qa-verifier`, sonnet, `dontAsk`, same workspace, folder and ticket; another Claude than the author's when the workspace has one. A full cap or no free slot spawns nothing (no queue in S1): `handoff.skipped` is audited and the lead is told in one line.
- **Verdict.** The QA worker's line 1 `PASS <sha>` or `FAIL <sha>: reasons`, only for the sha it was spawned for. PASS tells the lead. FAIL goes back to the implementer (reasons quoted as untrusted, in the crew report's own quoting `crewQuote`: redacted, no brackets or invisible characters, 400 characters) while fails are at most 2 and its context is under 150k tokens and it is live; otherwise the lead is told. After the third FAIL a further `DONE` starts no more QA. The counter is in memory: a portal restart resets it.
- **Who speaks.** The QA opening prompt and the hand-off messages come from the portal (`portal:handoff`, "Auto hand-off"), not in the lead's name and not in the crew report's "your worker" frame. The second-Claude choice (another than the author's) takes the first non-removed one without a usage look; create's credential check still refuses an ineligible one.
- **Audit.** `handoff.qa_started`, `handoff.skipped`, `handoff.fail_loop`, `handoff.pass`, with ticket, sha and worker ids, never worker text.
- **Not built (S2/S3).** The reviewer step, the "ready to merge" line, the UI toggle, the scorecard metric. Tokens are approximated by the session's context use.
